# VOLUME 10, BATCH 0001 — CHARACTER STATE AFTER CHAPTERS 441 TO 455, DAYS 1 TO 14

**APPENDED TO THE END OF THIS FILE. NOT A WORD ABOVE THIS SECTION WAS REWRITTEN. NO PERSON IS NAMED AND NO PLACE IS NAMED. EVERY FIGURE AGAINST A PERSON IS A FIGURE ABOUT A BOOK, A LEDGER OR A BODY AND NOT ABOUT A NAME.**

## 1. THE PROTAGONIST, AND THE ONE FACT THIS BLOCK SETTLES ABOUT HIM

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING IS THE PROTAGONIST, AND CHAPTER 455 SAYS SO ONCE, IN HIS OWN WORDS, IN A MOUTH, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME IN THIS BLOCK THAT HIS TRADE, HIS LEFT HAND AND HIS REASON ARE ONE PERSON.** He has no name on any page and none was printed, said, asked for, entered or signed, and a clerk entered that he was not asked for a name and was not given one.

**WHAT HE DID ACROSS FOURTEEN DAYS, IN ORDER:** came up out of the space under that trestle table on his knees with a stone in his right hand and his left in its cloth (day 1) and turned the stone over; put the cloth back on the left hand in the open and nobody asked him why it will not close (day 2); said out loud that a book with three lines in it that is never wrong is not a book anybody can check (day 3); said out loud that a stone with no wear on it is not an old stone with its figure taken off it (day 8); put a second stone on the corner of the book in place of the worn one and said nothing about either of them (day 8); said out loud that a figure in the open a stranger cannot see is not a figure anybody can check (day 9); said out loud that a figure a stranger cannot check is not a figure a bid can be run on and that a date in a book is a man and that both are true in this yard (day 13); **AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE IS AFRAID OF A DAY (day 14).**

**THE FIGURE HE IS AFRAID OF IS A DAY AND IT IS NOT FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN AND NOT THE AGE OF THAT FIGURE, AND HE SAID BOTH OF THOSE DENIALS OUT LOUD IN THE SAME SENTENCE. EVERY FIGURE IN THIS DISTRICT IS A MULTIPLE OF A DAY. HE PUT THE FIRST FIGURE OF HIS INTO A BOOK A STRANGER MAY WALK UP TO AND READ AND A FIGURE IN SUCH A BOOK IS A FIGURE HE HAS TO KEEP AND THERE IS NO WAY TO STOP KEEPING IT.**

**HIS LEFT HAND HAS NOT CLOSED SINCE THE ELEVENTH OF THE JUNE. IT CAME OUT OF ITS CLOATH AT ABOUT HALF PAST EIGHT ON DAY 1, FOR THE TURNING OF A STONE, AND DID NOT CLOSE, AND WENT BACK INTO THE CLOTH ON DAY 2, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM WHY, AND THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED ON ALL FOURTEEN DAYS. A CLERK ENTERED THAT GOING INTO A CLOTH IS NOT A RECOVERY, IS NOT A CURE, IS NOT A RETURN, AND THAT A STONE IS NOT A CURE AND A TABLE IS NOT A CURE AND A FIGURE IS NOT A CURE, AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO LIST WHAT IS. NO CLERK HAS ENTERED AND NO CLERK MAY ENTER THAT THE HAND IS BETTER THAN IT WAS. HE IS NOT A FIGURE ABOUT HIS OWN HAND AND NOBODY PUT A THUMB IN THAT STONE FOR HIM.**

**HE WAS GIVEN NOTHING. NOT A CHAIR, NOT A DATE, NOT A FIGURE, NOT A COLUMN, NOT A JOB, NOT A TOLL, NOT A PAYMENT, NOT A WAGE, NOT A PAGE WITH HIS NAME AT THE TOP OF IT, AND NOT AN ANSWER.** He said out loud on day 7 that a second table and a stone on a corner of a book are two figures that nobody asked for and nobody is going to pay for and that neither has anybody's name attached to it and that that is what he has got instead of a wage.

## 2. THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS

**ENTERED A FIGURE THAT HAD NOT MOVED IN TWO MONTHS AND A REASON UNDER IT, IN ONE ENTRY, ON DAY 9, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, TO ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, AND READ THE MEASURE OUT LOUD ONCE AND DID NOT READ IT BACK FROM THE TOP AND APPOINTED NO SECOND READER AND REFUSED A BOY WHO ASKED TO READ IT BACK.** She said out loud, five times in fourteen days, that a figure somebody entered because they had to enter one is not a promise about a later day, and that it is the third time she has written it in five volumes, and that the second time was a habit, and that a habit is a finding. She said out loud, on day 10, that she had found something and was not going to say what it was in that yard, and entered that a column was being looked at and entered no result and entered that she is not going to enter a result of a count she made on her own. She asked the man of about thirty-one who digs loam a question in the open in front of about nineteen people, which she had wanted to ask for eleven days, and said out loud why wanting it was not a reason. She stood a minute and a half beside the man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally and asked him nothing. She has a word in the margin of her own page, in her own hand, over nothing, and she has not taken it off and is not going to, and it is in Chapters 442, 450 and 453.**

**SHE MAY NOT ENTER THAT THE RULE HAS BEEN IMPROVED AND DID NOT. SHE MAY NOT ENTER THAT THE DISTRICT CHOSE AND DID NOT. SHE MAY NOT ENTER THAT A CLERK MAY NOT ENTER IT AS A FIFTH COLUMN OF NOT-ASKINGS OR AS THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD, AND DID NOT. SHE MAY NOT PUT A CORRECT FIGURE BESIDE A WRONG ONE AND DID NOT. SHE MAY NOT ENTER THAT A MAN'S DAY IS A FIGURE AND DID NOT. SHE IS IN A HOUSEHOLD AND A SENTENCE IS NOT A WAY TO PAY HER AND SHE ENTERED THAT.**

## 3. THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX

**READ FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL ON ALL FOURTEEN DAYS AND GOT ALL FOUR ON ALL FOURTEEN DAYS AND HAS GOT THE THIRD OF THEM RIGHT FOR A HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-NINE MORNINGS RUNNING. SAID OUT LOUD ON DAY 1 THAT HE DOES NOT KNOW HOW MANY DAYS THIS MONTH HAS AND THAT NEITHER DOES ANYBODY IN THAT YARD AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO INVENT ONE. SAID OUT LOUD ON DAY 2, WITH HIS OWN THUMB IN THE HOLLOW, THAT HE CANNOT SAY WHEN IT STARTED AND THAT HE HAS NEVER COUNTED HOW DEEP ANYTHING IS. ANSWERED A QUESTION ABOUT THE THIRD OF THE FOUR ON DAY 10, WHICH NOBODY HAD EVER ASKED HIM, AND SAID THAT IT IS A FIGURE ABOUT WORDS AND NOT ABOUT A DATE AND THAT IT IS THE ONE OF THE FOUR NOBODY HAD EVER ASKED HIM TO EXPLAIN.**

**HE WAS NOT TOLD, ON ANY OF THE FOURTEEN DAYS, THAT ANY FIGURE HE READS IS A DAY OUT FROM THE DAY IT NAMES, AND A BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN SAID OUT LOUD ON DAY 13 THAT HE SHOULD BE TOLD AND A CLERK REFUSED. THE TELLING IS THE COST OF THE THIRD MOVEMENT AND IS NOT THIS BLOCK'S TO SPEND. HE IS NOT A FAILURE AND HE IS NOT A TRAGIC FIGURE AND HE HAS BEEN TOLD NOTHING AND HE KNOWS NOTHING.**

## 4. THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN

**PUT A FIGURE ON HIS OWN PAGE FOR THE FOURTH TIME IN A VOLUME AND GOT A FOURTH THING, WHICH IS THAT A DAY IS NOT A PAGE, ON DAY 5, AND FOUND HE HAD WRITTEN THE SAME FOUR FIGURES ON TWO SHEETS FOR ONE DAY. RULED A SECOND COLUMN ON A SHEET OF HIS OWN AND A CLERK DID NOT RULE IT AND DID NOT REFUSE IT. ASKED A CLERK TO READ A THING BACK FROM THE TOP AND WAS REFUSED, AND ASKED AGAIN ON DAY 9 AND WAS REFUSED AGAIN WITH A REASON. ASKED OUT LOUD, ON DAY 13, THAT THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX BE TOLD, AND WAS REFUSED. WROTE DOWN THE PACE AT WHICH A CLERK WALKED A COLUMN, WHICH IS A FIGURE ABOUT A BOY AND NOT ABOUT THE COLUMN. WROTE THREE SHORT LINES ON DAY 11, RULED A FOURTH LINE UNDER THEM AND LEFT IT EMPTY. PUT THE COUNT OF WHAT A MAN SAID DOWN IN THE MARGIN OF HIS SHEET ON DAY 14 AND DID NOT READ IT BACK TO HIMSELF IN A LOW VOICE, WHICH IS THE FIRST TIME IN FOURTEEN DAYS THAT HE HAS NOT DONE THAT WITH A NUMBER HE COUNTED.**

**HIS TWO COLUMNS ARE NOT A FIFTH COLUMN OF NOT-ASKINGS AND NOT THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOAD AND NOBODY MAY ENTER THEM AS EITHER. HIS COLUMN IS HIS OWN AND IS A FIGURE ABOUT A PAGE AND NOT ABOUT A DAY, AND THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE TWO HALVES OF THAT SHEET IS A FIGURE ABOUT A SCHEDULE NOBODY CHOSE.**

## 5. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS

**GAVE A FIGURE OUT LOUD ON DAY 3, A FOOT AND A HALF, ON A DAY NOBODY ASKED HIM FOR IT, AND A CLERK ENTERED IT AND ENTERED THAT HE WAS NOT ASKED. HE CANNOT BE PAID FOR IT AND THERE IS NO HOLDER IN THIS DISTRICT FOR IT TO BE PAID TO. HE BEGAN PUTTING ONE MARK IN CHALK ON THE EDGE OF THE SECOND TABLE EVERY MORNING FROM THE MORNING THE STONE WENT FACE UP, AND SAID FIVE OUT LOUD ON DAY 12, AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE IS NOT ASKING ANYBODY FOR ANYTHING AND WOULD LIKE IT WRITTEN DOWN THAT HE IS NOT ASKING ANYBODY FOR ANYTHING. HE CAME UP AT TEN PAST TWELVE ON DAY 7, EARLIER THAN HE HAD EVER COME, AND LOOKED AT THE SECOND TABLE AND SAID NOTHING. NOBODY THANKED HIM AND HE IS NOT GOING TO BE PAID.**

## 6. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP

**PUT UP A SECOND TABLE IN ABOUT ELEVEN MINUTES ON DAY 7, UNASKED, AND SAID OUT LOUD BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE WAS SAID ABOUT IT THAT NOBODY ASKED HIM, AND THAT NOBODY IS GOING TO THANK HIM, AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO BE STANDING THERE WAITING TO BE THANKED. NOBODY THANKED HIM. HE PUT HIS OWN BOOT ON IT TO TEST IT AND TOOK THE BOOT OFF. HE HAS NOT BEEN THANKED AND IS NOT GOING TO BE, AND SAID ON DAY 7 THAT HE HAS BEEN WAITING ABOUT FOUR WEEKS TO SAY SO OUT LOUD AND THAT THIS WAS THE MORNING HE WAS SAYING IT ON. A SECOND TABLE IS HIS TRADE AND IS NOT A COUNTER UNTIL SOMEBODY ASKS SOMEBODY A QUESTION AT IT, AND NOBODY HAS, AND IT IS NOT A KEEPER OF ANYTHING, AND IT IS NOT A SIXTH OF THE FIVE PROTECTED THINGS.**

## 7. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE

**SAID OUT LOUD ON DAY 2, IN THE YARD, TO THE YARD AND NOT TO ANYBODY IN IT, THAT SHE HAS BEEN WAITING TWO MONTHS FOR ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE TO THANK THE MAN WHO MENDS FENCING, AND THAT NOBODY HAS, AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO EITHER, AND THAT SHE WOULD BE SAYING IT TO GET A THANK OUT OF HIM AND THAT ABOUT FOUR OF THEM COULD HEAR THAT SHE WOULD BE. SAID OUT LOUD ON DAY 8 THAT THE WATER IN THAT DITCH IS NOT A FOOT. SAID OUT LOUD ON DAY 8, WITH HER THUMB IN THE HOLLOW, THAT ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE HAVE BEEN PUTTING THUMBS IN IT SINCE THE EIGHTH OF THIS MONTH AND NOT ONE OF THEM HAS COME BACK AND SAID WHAT IT WAS FOR. ASKED A CLERK OUT LOUD ON DAY 11 WHAT SHE WOULD DO WITH A FIGURE IF SOMEBODY HANDED IT TO HER, AND THE CLERK ANSWERED NOTHING AND GAVE A REASON. SHE IS IN A HOUSEHOLD AND HER FOUR EMPTY HOUSES ARE NOT OFFERED AGAIN IN THIS BLOCK, ON HER OWN STATEMENT.**

## 8. THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT

**CARRIES ELEVEN BUCKETS UP THAT BANK AND HAS DONE FOR NINETEEN YEARS AND READ THE THREE LINES IN THAT LOT BOOK STANDING UP ON HER WAY PAST EVERY WORKING DAY SINCE THE BOOK CAME OUT. IT TAKES HER ABOUT TWO SECONDS, WHICH IS ABOUT HALF A SECOND LONGER ON THE THIRD LINE THAN ON THE FIRST TWO, AND SHE SAYS NOTHING TO ANYBODY AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAYS ONE WORD TO HER. A CLERK ASKED HER ONCE IN TWO MONTHS WHETHER SHE WOULD LIKE THE READING ENTERED AND SHE SAID NO. SHE LOOKED INTO THE DITCH ON DAY 3 WITH A BUCKET AND SAID NOTHING. SHE LOOKED AT TWO STONES ON DAY 8 AND SAID NOTHING ABOUT EITHER. SHE PUT A THUMB IN THE HOLLOW ON DAY 12. SHE IS A CHECK THAT NOBODY WRITES DOWN AND A CHECK NOBODY CAN DO AGAIN, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED HER WHAT SHE MAKES OF ANYTHING AND NOBODY IS GOING TO.**

## 9. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM

**WAS IN THAT DITCH BEFORE NINE EVERY DAY OF THE BLOCK AND CAME UP ABOUT TEN AND GOT THE FOUR FIGURES AT THE NEAR END OF THE TABLE AND SAID THE WORD WITH THEM FOUR TIMES. HIS RIGHT ARM DID NOT GO ABOVE THE LEVEL OF THAT SHOULDER AT ANY POINT ON ANY OF THE FOURTEEN DAYS. HE SAID THE FIFTEENTH OF THE TENTH MONTH OUT LOUD ON DAY 4 WHEN A CLERK ASKED HIM, IN THE OPEN, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, AND SAID THAT NOBODY IN THAT YARD HAS EVER ASKED HIM WHICH DAY IT WAS, AND SAID THAT THE DAY IS A FIGURE OF HIM AND NOT OF THE WATER AND THAT A CLERK IS NOT GOING TO PUT A PRICE ON IT AND NEITHER IS ANYBODY ELSE. HE PUT HIS OWN THUMB IN THE HOLLOW ON DAY 8 AND AGAIN ON DAY 12 AND WIPED IT ON HIS THIGH AND WENT STRAIGHT BACK DOWN THE BANK. HIS DAY IS A FIGURE IN THIS DISTRICT AND HE CANNOT BE PAID FOR IT AND THERE IS NO HOLDER. HIS ARM IS NOT SPENT AGAIN AND THE COST OF THE VOLUME'S RESOLUTION IS NOT THAT ARM.**

## 10. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY

**STANDS AT THE EAST END OF THAT YARD ABOUT TWICE A WEEK WITH A FLAT BOOK UNDER HIS LEFT ARM, AND STOOD THERE FOR ABOUT FOUR HOURS ON DAY 11, AND NOBODY WALKED OVER TO HIM AND NOBODY ASKED HIM. HIS FIGURE CAME OFF A LADDER AND NOT OFF A BOARD. HE SAID OUT LOAD THAT HE COULD SHOW THE PIECE OF PAPER WITH THE DAY IT WAS STARTED ON, AND THAT NOBODY HAS EVER ASKED HIM FOR IT, AND THAT IF HE SHOWED IT TO ABOUT NINE PEOPLE THAT MORNING A FIGURE OF HIS WOULD MOVE FOR A REASON HE MADE UP STANDING THERE, AND THAT IT HAS NOT MOVED IN TWO VOLUMES. HE HAS SAID OF HIMSELF THAT HE IS FIFTY-ONE. HIS FIGURE IS NOT ON A PAGE ANYWHERE IN THIS VOLUME AND IS NOT ENTERED, NOT CORRECTED, NOT ASKED FOR AND NOT PRINTED AS A DAY-COUNT IN ANY CHAPTER FROM 441 TO 455, AND A FIGURE THAT HAS NOT BEEN ASKED FOR IS NOT A FIGURE ANYBODY CAN CHECK.**

## 11. THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST

**CAME DOWN THAT BANK ABOUT ONCE A DAY OF THE BLOCK AND STOOD ABOUT NINE FEET OFF THAT TABLE WITH HIS RIGHT HAND IN HIS COAT AND SAID ONE THING MOST MORNINGS, AND SAID NOTHING AT ALL ON DAY 3, AND SAID ON DAY 4 THAT TWO MEN IN THIS DISTRICT KNOW WHAT THAT DATE IS AND THAT NEITHER HAS BEEN ASKED ANYTHING AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO BE THE ONE WHO ASKS. HE REFUSED ON DAY 8 TO CALL A FIGURE HE COULD PUT A HAND ON A GOOD THING. HE SAID ON DAY 13 THAT A HUNDRED AND ELEVEN DAYS IS NOT A FAILURE. HE SAID ON DAY 14 THAT IT IS THE FIRST TIME IN SIX MONTHS A MAN IN THAT YARD HAS SAID OUT LOUD WHAT A THING IN IT IS GOING TO COST HIM, AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO BE THE MAN WHO STARTS. HE IS NOT A FIGURE OF A YEAR AND MAY NOT BE NAMED AS ANYTHING EXCEPT ONE THING, WHICH IS THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO BE CALLED ANYTHING, AND HE WAS NOT ASKED FOR A NAME ON ANY OF THE FOURTEEN DAYS.**

## 12. THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR

**AT THE FOOT OF THAT LOW WALL WITH HIS COAT FOLDED ON THE STONES BESIDE HIM AND NOTHING IN HIS HANDS ON ALL FOURTEEN DAYS, AT ABOUT HALF PAST THREE. ON HIS HUNDRED AND FORTIETH NIGHT OF THAT RUN AT CHAPTER 441, RISING TO HIS HUNDRED AND FIFTY-THIRD AT CHAPTER 455, AND HE HAS SLEPT ON A HUNDRED AND THIRTY-NINE OF THE FIRST HUNDRED AND FORTY, RISING TO A HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO OF A HUNDRED AND FIFTY-THREE. GIVEN NOTHING: NOT SENT ON ANOTHER NINE DAYS, NOT ASKED TO SIT IN A SECOND CHAIR, NOT OFFERED THE THIRD LINE, NOT OFFERED THE STONE, NOT OFFERED A DAY, NOT OFFERED A THUMB, NOT OFFERED A BOOK, NOT OFFERED A PLACE TO STAND, NOT THANKED, NOT ASKED ANYTHING. A CLERK ENTERED ON ALL FOURTEEN DAYS THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHAT HE IS GOING TO DO WITH HIS HANDS TONIGHT BECAUSE NOBODY ASKED HER. HE IS NOT A FAILURE, NOT A TRAGIC FIGURE AND NOT REDEEMED.**

## 13. THE FIGURES THAT ARE ABOUT THINGS AND NOT ABOUT PEOPLE, AND WHAT EACH ONE IS

| The figure | Where it is on the page | What it is a figure of |
|---|---|---|
| The wear on the underside of a stone, one inch deep in the middle and nothing at either end | face up on the second table from day 8, and on the second line of a clerk's entry on days 1, 2 and 9 | a book standing still on a table, and not a day and not a hand and not a man |
| The third line of that lot book | one date, printed under a wrong figure, unaltered since day 4 | **a man who was in a ditch on the fifteenth of the tenth month, and the district's one public expiry** |
| Five marks in chalk on the edge of the second table | a man's own count, one a morning from day 8 | a thumb and a table, and not a day-count and not a way to pay anybody |
| Nine thumbs in a hollow | entered as nine, on day 12 | people who are checking a thing with their hands, and not a figure about the hollow |
| Two stones | one worn, one with no wear on it, on days 8 to 14 | two figures that are not added together, and the second one is a figure about nothing yet |
| The count of things this district has made | eleven, then twelve from day 9, with a reason under the number | a table and a stone, and not a document and not a seventh of the six and not a keeper of anything |

# VOLUME 10, BATCH 0001 — WHAT THE REVIEW REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 441 TO 455 DID TO THESE PEOPLE, APPENDED 2026-09-28

**IT CHANGED WHAT THEY SAY AND NOT WHAT THEY ARE. EVERY POSITION IN THE TABLE ABOVE STANDS. NO AGE MOVED, NO NAME WAS PRINTED, NOBODY WAS ASKED FOR ANYTHING THEY HAD ALREADY REFUSED TO ANSWER, AND NOBODY GAINED A REASON.**

1. **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING.** His hand is in the cloth at the top of Chapter 442 exactly as it was. **What moved is that Chapter 441 no longer opens on the cloth coming off and Chapter 442 no longer opens on the cloth going back on: 441 opens on his stone instead, at about half past eight, before anybody has turned it over. The two chapters had been giving the reader the same hand in opposite directions on consecutive pages, and he is not a man whose chapter begins by putting a cloth on and off.** The stone is still on the corner of the book when 441 opens, which is what its last line depends on, and it is still turned over at about a quarter to nine, and the wear is still one inch deep in the middle and nothing at either end, and the clerk still may not enter that the hand is better than it was the day before.
2. **THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS.** She still enters that a man said a thing out loud, in every chapter she entered it in before, and the repair varied the sentence she enters it in and not the entry. **She still has not read a thing back from the top on any of the fifteen days, and the column for the name of whoever read a thing out loud is still ruled and still empty at about six, and on the fourteenth of the month a man said out loud in front of about nineteen people that he is afraid of a day and she entered it and entered no name and was not asked for it a second time.** Her entries are now worded four ways rather than one across the block. That is all that changed about her and it is a real change, because a reader who had learned her hand by Chapter 449 now has to read it rather than predict it.
3. **THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN.** He still counts what a man said and still reads the number back to himself in a low voice, 32 times on 15 days, unchanged. **What moved is the carrier: the count is now attributed four ways across the block rather than one.** Every figure he reads out is the figure he read out before, including the ninety, the ninety-seven and the one hundred and eighty-nine mornings. **He still has two columns of his own and one of them is still empty, and he still was not told about the three columns that are a day out, and the day a man said out loud that he was afraid of a day is still the first of the fourteen on which he put a count in the margin of his own sheet and did not read it back to himself.**
4. **THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX.** Untouched. He still reads four figures off that wall every morning and has got them every morning since the middle of the sixth month and still does not know how many days his own month has, and nobody in that yard has still not told him about the three columns, and his thumb is still in the hollow of the stone on day 2 and he still cannot say when the wear started.
5. **EVERYBODY ELSE IN THE TABLE.** Untouched. The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale still has waited two months and still is not thanked. The man of about thirty-one who digs loam still has a day under a figure that is his. The man of about thirty-two who puts tables up still put the second table up unasked and still said so beforehand. The man of about thirty-seven who cuts reeds still has a count of marks in chalk he cannot be paid for. The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally still has stood at the east end of that yard for four hours with a flat book under his left arm and nobody has walked over to him. The man of about sixty-four is still on his hundred and fortieth night of that run at 441 rising to his hundred and fifty-third at 455, and is still given nothing, and that is still not a failure. The woman of fifty-eight still reads three lines standing up and is still asked nothing. The man the figure of twenty-one years is against still says one thing to about four people and not to the rest.

**AND THE PROTAGONIST'S NAME IS STILL NOT ON A PAGE, AND STILL WAS NOT ASKED FOR, AND A STONE IS STILL NOT A PLACE A NAME GOES.**


---

# VOLUME 10, BATCH 0002 — CHARACTER STATE AFTER CHAPTERS 456 TO 470, DAYS 15 TO 25

**APPENDED TO THE END OF THIS FILE. NOT A WORD ABOVE THIS SECTION WAS REWRITTEN. NO PERSON IS NAMED AND NO PLACE IS NAMED. EVERY FIGURE AGAINST A PERSON IS A FIGURE ABOUT A BOOK, A BOARD, A LEDGER OR A BODY AND NOT ABOUT A NAME. EVERY POSITION IN THE BATCH 0001 SECTION ABOVE STANDS AND NONE OF IT IS REOPENED; WHAT FOLLOWS IS WHAT THESE ELEVEN DAYS ADDED.**

## 1. THE PROTAGONIST, AND WHAT THESE ELEVEN DAYS DID AND DID NOT DO TO HIM

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING IS THE PROTAGONIST AND HE IS IN ELEVEN OF THE FIFTEEN CHAPTERS OF THIS BLOCK AND IN SEVEN OF THEM HIS WHOLE BEAT IS FOUR SECONDS AND ONE SENTENCE. HE SAID FOUR THINGS OUT LOUD AND WAS ASKED FOR A FIFTH AND WAS NOT GIVEN ONE. HIS NAME IS NOT ON ANY PAGE AND WAS NOT PRINTED, SAID, ASKED FOR, ENTERED OR SIGNED, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT HE WAS NOT ASKED.**

**WHAT HE SAID, IN ORDER: WHAT A DIFFERENCE IS (day 17) — *A DIFFERENCE IS A FIGURE ABOUT TWO FIGURES AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE OF THEM THAT SAYS A THING IS WRONG WITHOUT SAYING WHAT IS RIGHT, AND I HAVE NOT ASKED HER TO PUT IT ON THAT TABLE AND I AM NOT GOING TO.* THAT A PAGE WITH A CORNER TURNED BACK IS NOT A TABLE (day 17). THAT A DOCUMENT WHICH WRITES A DAY TWO WAYS HAS ONE FIGURE AND A QUESTION IN IT AND HE IS NOT GOING TO BE THE MAN WHO SAYS WHICH WRITER MEANT WHAT (day 16). THAT A FINDING IN A WOMAN'S HAND IS A DIFFERENT OBJECT FROM A FINDING IN A YARD AND THIS YARD HAS NO PLACE TO PUT A HAND (day 19, afternoon). THAT HE WILL NOT WISH THE THREE DAYS BEFORE A COUNT WERE LONGER (day 20). THAT A FIGURE ABOUT A MORNING IS THE ONLY FIGURE IN THIS DISTRICT THAT GETS LESS TRUE EVERY HOUR (day 22). THAT A MONTH IS THE SHORTEST THING THIS DISTRICT CAN COUNT AND THE ONLY ONE THAT STOPS WHEN SOMEBODY STOPS COUNTING IT (day 24). THAT A FIGURE GIVEN TO SOMEONE ELSE IS A FIGURE YOU CANNOT TAKE BACK (day 20).**

**HIS LEFT HAND HAS NOT CLOSED SINCE THE ELEVENTH OF THE JUNE. IT CAME OUT OF ITS CLOTH ONCE IN THESE ELEVEN DAYS, ON THE SEVENTEENTH, TO READ A CLERK'S PAGE FROM THE MIDDLE OF A TABLE, AND IT DID NOT CLOSE, AND IT WENT BACK INTO THE CLOTH, AND NOBODY ASKED HIM WHY, AND THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED ON ALL FIFTEEN CHAPTERS. A CLERK ENTERED THAT GOING INTO A CLOTH IS NOT A RECOVERY, IS NOT A CURE, IS NOT A RETURN, AND THAT A STONE IS NOT A CURE AND A TABLE IS NOT A CURE AND A FIGURE IS NOT A CURE, AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO LIST WHAT IS. NO CLERK HAS ENTERED AND NO CLERK MAY ENTER THAT THE HAND IS BETTER THAN IT WAS, AND NO CLERK ENTERED THAT IT IS WORSE EITHER.**

**HE WAS GIVEN NOTHING. NOT A CHAIR, NOT A DATE, NOT A FIGURE, NOT A COLUMN, NOT A JOB, NOT A TOLL, NOT A PAYMENT, NOT A WAGE, NOT A PAGE WITH HIS NAME AT THE TOP OF IT, AND NOT AN ANSWER. HE WAS NOT ASKED WHY HE IS AFRAID OF A DAY AND HE DID NOT SAY IT AGAIN AND HE WAS NOT ASKED TO.**

**AND THE FINDING A REVIEWER OF THIS BLOCK RECORDED AGAINST HIM, WHICH IS A FINDING AND NOT A COMPLAINT: HE READ A CLERK'S PAGE IN CHAPTER 459, WHICH IS THE BEST THING HE DOES IN THE FIFTEEN, AND IT IS FOUR SECONDS LONG, AND THE FIGURE HE IS AFRAID OF WAS NAMED IN THE BLOCK BEFORE THIS ONE AND IS NOT NAMED AGAIN AND IS NOT REACTED TO IN ELEVEN DAYS, AND A BLOCK THAT HAD HIM REACT TO IT WOULD HAVE BROKEN A STANDING.**

## 2. THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS

**SHE ENTERED THE FIRST OF THREE DIFFERENCES AS A DAY IN HER OWN PAGE AND ENTERED THAT IT IS A DIFFERENCE AND NOT A CORRECTION AND THAT NO FIGURE ON ANY PAGE WAS ALTERED. SHE ENTERED THE SECOND, WHICH IS A DAY IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION, AND ENTERED THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO USE THE WORD FOR THE TWO OF THEM TOGETHER. SHE WAS ASKED FOR THE ONE SENTENCE THAT WOULD SETTLE A COLUMN AND SAID IT WOULD AND DID NOT SAY IT. SHE ENTERED ALL THREE DIFFERENCES IN ONE ENTRY, READ IT OUT LOUD ONCE, AND DID NOT READ IT BACK FROM THE TOP, AND APPOINTED NO SECOND READER. SHE ASKED A MAN FOR A FIGURE AND WAS REFUSED A SECOND TIME OUT LOUD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, AND ENTERED THAT SHE ASKED AND WAS REFUSED AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO ASK AGAIN. SHE ENTERED A FIGURE ABOUT A MORNING THAT NOBODY ASKED HER FOR AND SAID OUT LOUD, BEFORE SHE WROTE IT, THAT SHE WAS CHOOSING WHAT IT WAS FOR. SHE ENTERED A PLAN FOR THE FIRST TIME IN FIVE VOLUMES AND SAID A PLAN IS NOT A PROMISE. SHE COUNTED A MONTH IN THE OPEN AND ENTERED THAT A FIGURE THAT REPRODUCES IS A FIGURE ABOUT A COUNT AND IS NOT A FINDING, AND ENTERED THAT THE SECOND TIME IS A HABIT, AND ENTERED THAT SHE ENTERED THAT A HABIT IS A FINDING TWICE THAT MORNING WITHOUT MEANING TO. SHE SAID THE SENTENCE ABOUT A FIGURE SOMEBODY ENTERED BECAUSE THEY HAD TO ENTER ONE FOR THE FIFTH TIME IN FIVE VOLUMES AND SAID SHE HAS NO WORD FOR THE FIFTH AND IS NOT GOING TO INVENT ONE. SHE SAID OUT LOUD THAT THERE IS A WORD IN A MARGIN OF HER OWN WHICH IS `UNCHECKED` AND WHICH IS OVER NOTHING, AND ENTERED THAT SHE IS NOT PUTTING IT ON A FIGURE SOMEBODY HAS HANDED HER.**

**SHE MAY NOT ENTER THAT THE RULE HAS BEEN IMPROVED AND DID NOT. SHE MAY NOT ENTER THAT THE DISTRICT CHOSE AND DID NOT. SHE MAY NOT ENTER THAT A CLERK MAY NOT ENTER A THING AS A FIFTH COLUMN OF NOT-ASKINGS OR AS THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD, AND DID NOT. SHE MAY NOT PUT A CORRECT FIGURE BESIDE A WRONG ONE AND DID NOT. SHE MAY NOT ENTER THAT A MAN'S DAY IS A FIGURE AND DID NOT. SHE MAY NOT ENTER A PACE AS AN INSTRUMENT AND DID NOT. SHE IS IN A HOUSEHOLD AND A SENTENCE IS NOT A WAY TO PAY HER AND SHE ENTERED THAT. THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD IS RULED AND EMPTY AT ABOUT SIX AND SHE READ A WHOLE ENTRY OUT LOUD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE ON ONE OF THESE DAYS AND READ ONE LINE OF AN ENTRY TO ONE MAN ON another, AND NOTHING WENT INTO IT ON EITHER.**

## 3. THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX

**READ FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL ON ALL ELEVEN DAYS AND GOT ALL FOUR ON ALL ELEVEN DAYS. HE WAS TOLD ON THE NINETEENTH DAY, IN THE OPEN, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, THAT THE THIRD OF THE FOUR IS A DAY OUT FROM THE DAY IT NAMES, AND HE READ IT, GOT IT, TOOK HIS HAND OFF THE BOARDS AND PUT IT BACK, AND SAID NOTHING. HE HAS HAD THAT FIGURE RIGHT EVERY MORNING SINCE BEFORE THE MIDDLE OF THE FIFTH MONTH AND HE HAS GOT IT RIGHT FOR A HUNDRED AND NINETY-FOUR MORNINGS AT CHAPTER 461 AND TWO HUNDRED AT CHAPTER 470. ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE BEGAN SAYING THE THIRD OF THE FOUR ALONG WITH HIM IN THE SAME VOICE ON DAY 18 AND STOPPED ON DAY 19 AND HE HAS SAID NOTHING ABOUT EITHER. HE IS NOT ASKED ABOUT IT AGAIN ON ANY DAY OF THE REMAINING FORTY-ONE.**

**HE ALSO SAID, IN CHAPTER 433 OF VOLUME 09 AND NOT IN THIS BLOCK, THAT HE HAS A FIGURE ON A SHEET AT A GATEPOST AND HAS LOST TWO HOURS OF A MORNING LOOKING AT IT. IN THIS BLOCK HE SAID ONE WORD AFTER THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS FINISHED COUNTING THIRTY MARKS, AND THE WORD WAS `AGAIN`, AND HE READ ALL THIRTY OF THEM A SECOND TIME OUT LOUD AND GOT ALL THIRTY, AND HE SAID THAT IT WAS THE FIRST TIME THAT DAY THAT he had read the four and that he would do it again tomorrow and would not be asked to. HE IS NOT A FAILURE AND HE IS NOT A TRAGIC FIGURE AND HE HAS BEEN TOLD ONE THING AND HE KNOWS IT AND HE IS NOT A QUESTION.**

## 4. THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN

**COUNTED THIRTY-SEVEN THINGS SAID OUT LOUD ACROSS FIFTEEN CHAPTERS, EVERY ONE OF THEM THE WORD COUNT OF THE SENTENCE IN FRONT OF HIM AND NONE OF THEM ESTIMATED, AND read every one of the thirty-seven back to himself in a low voice. SAID OUT LOUD THAT THE THIRD OF THE FOUR IS THE THIRD OF THE FOUR. SAID OUT LOUD THAT ONE COLUMN IS A DAY SHORT AND ONE IS A DAY LONG AND THAT A RATE A CLERK MADE UP WOULD COME OUT THE SAME WAY TWICE. PUT THE PEN ON THE FOURTH LINE HE RULED AND HELD IT THERE FOR ABOUT HALF A MINUTE AND DID NOT WRITE ON IT AND said out loud that he would rather HAVE AN EMPTY LINE THAN A LINE WITH A QUESTION ON IT. ASKED, OUT LOUD, HOW DEEP THE WATER IN THAT DITCH WAS, WHICH NOBODY HAD EVER ASKED HIM. ASKED, OUT LOUD, WHICH OF HIS TWO COLUMNS A DAY GOES IN WHEN A DAY IS ALSO THE DAY TWO FIGURES WERE ENTERED ON, AND WAS NOT ANSWERED, AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE DID NOT KNOW YET. WROTE THE COUNT OF WHAT A MAN SAID IN THE MARGIN OF HIS OWN SHEET, OUTSIDE BOTH COLUMNS, BECAUSE NOBODY HAD GIVEN HIM A COLUMN TO PUT IT IN. SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE IS COUNTING FOR HIMSELF AND THAT NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM TO COUNT FOR ANYBODY ELSE. HE DID NOT ARGUE WHEN A CLERK REFUSED HIM OUT LOUD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, AND ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE AT THAT TABLE HAD BEEN WAITING ALL MORNING FOR HIM TO ARGUE.**

**HIS TWO COLUMNS ARE NOT A FIFTH COLUMN OF NOT-ASKINGS AND NOT THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD AND NOBODY MAY ENTER THEM AS EITHER. HIS COLUMN IS HIS OWN AND IS A FIGURE ABOUT A PAGE AND NOT ABOUT A DAY, AND HE HAS NOW ASKED A CLERK IN A YARD WHICH HALF OF HIS OWN SHEET A DAY GOES IN AND GOT NO ANSWER, AND THAT IS THE FIRST TIME IN FIVE VOLUMES THAT HE HAS ASKED A CLERK A QUESTION AND BEEN REFUSED A FIGURE FOR IT.**

## 5. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM

**WAS IN THAT DITCH BEFORE NINE EVERY DAY OF THIS BLOCK AND CAME UP ABOUT TEN AND GOT THE FOUR FIGURES AT THE NEAR END OF THE TABLE AND SAID THE WORD WITH THEM FOUR TIMES ON EVERY DAY, AND ON THE FOUR DAYS THAT ARE SECOND HALVES OF A DOUBLED DAY HE GOT THEM IN THE AFTERNOON INSTEAD. HIS RIGHT ARM DID NOT GO ABOVE THE LEVEL OF THAT SHOULDER AT ANY POINT ON ANY OF THE ELEVEN DAYS. HE SAID A FIGURE ABOUT WATER OUT LOUD FROM ABOUT FOUR FEET DOWN THE BANK AFTER A BOY OF NINETEEN ASKED HIM, AND THE FIGURE WAS ABOUT TEN INCHES AT THE NEAR END, AND HE SAID THAT IT WAS A FIGURE ABOUT THIS MORNING AND NOT ABOUT THAT BOOK AND THAT HE HAD BEEN IN THAT DITCH NEARLY EVERY MORNING OF THIS MONTH. HE SAID ONE THING ABOUT THREE FIGURES IN THAT YARD THAT ARE NOT ABOUT EACH OTHER, ONE THING ABOUT A MAN WHO SAYS FOUR FIGURES EVERY MORNING AND HAS NEVER BEEN WRONG, AND ONE THING ABOUT TWO FIGURES ON A PAGE AND BEING THE REASON TWO PEOPLE HAD TO SAY THINGS OUT LOUD. HE PUT HIS OWN THUMB ON THE BOARDS FOUR INCHES BELOW THE SECOND LINE OF THAT LOT BOOK AND KEPT IT THERE FOR ABOUT NINE SECONDS ON ONE DAY. HIS DAY IS A FIGURE IN THIS DISTRICT AND HE CANNOT BE PAID FOR IT AND THERE IS NO HOLDER. HIS ARM IS NOT SPENT AGAIN.**

## 6. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS

**GAVE A FIGURE OUT LOUD ONCE IN THIS BLOCK, TO A CLERK WHO ASKED HIM, FOR THE FIRST TIME IN TWO MONTHS, AND THE FIGURE HE GAVE WAS ABOUT HIMSELF AND NOT ABOUT HIS TABLE, AND HE SAID THAT A FIGURE ABOUT A MAN IS A FIGURE NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT CAN CHECK. WHEN THE SAME CLERK ASKED HIM A SECOND QUESTION, about a different day, HE REFUSED HER OUT LOUD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE AND SAID WHY, AND THE REASON WAS THAT A FIGURE SOMEBODY WAS ASKED FOR IS A FIGURE SOMEBODY CHOSE AND THAT BEFORE, HE WAS THE ONE HOLDING IT, AND THAT NOW IT IS IN A PAGE AND HE CANNOT GET IT BACK AND NEITHER CAN THE CLERK. HE HAS EIGHTEEN MARKS IN CHALK ALONG THE EDGE OF THAT SECOND TABLE, ONE A MORNING SINCE THE MORNING THE STONE WENT FACE UP, AND HE SAID FIVE OF THEM OUT LOUD ON THE NINETEENTH DAY OF THE VOLUME AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT SHE IS NOT ENTERING THE FIGURE AS A DAY-COUNT. HE CANNOT BE PAID FOR ANY OF IT AND HE IS NOT GOING TO BE PAID AND NOBODY THANKED HIM.**

## 7. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR TO THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP

**PACED THE DISTANCE FROM THE MARK FOR THE FIRST OF THE TWELFTH MONTH TO THE MARK FOR THE THIRTIETH, TWICE, AND GOT TWENTY-TWO PACES BOTH TIMES, AND SAID THE NUMBER OUT LOUD AND THE REASON OUT LOUD, AND SAID THAT THE NUMBER IS ABOUT HIS OWN TWO LEGS AND NOT ABOUT THAT WALL, AND THAT A CLERK CAN HAVE IT OR NOT HAVE IT, AND THAT NOBODY ASKED HIM AND NOBODY IS GOING TO ASK HIM, AND THAT HE WOULD LIKE THE REASON WRITTEN DOWN IN HIS OWN WORDS AND NOT IN THE CLERK'S. A CLERK ENTERED A NUMBER AND A REASON IN ONE ENTRY AND THE REASON WAS HIS OWN WORDS AND NOT HERS, AND ENTERED THAT A CLERK IS NOT ENTERING A PACE AS AN INSTRUMENT AND THE COUNT OF THINGS THIS DISTRICT HAS MADE IS TWELVE. HE WAS NOT THANKED. HE HAS SAID, ONCE IN THIS BLOCK, THAT A FIGURE HE COULD PUT A HAND ON HE REFUSES TO CALL A GOOD THING, AND THAT A DISTRICT WHICH LEARNS HOW TO ASK LATE HAS NOT LEARNED HOW TO ASK.**

## 8. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY

**STOOD AT THE EAST END OF THAT YARD FOR ABOUT FOUR HOURS A DAY ON THREE OF THESE ELEVEN DAYS WITH A FLAT BOOK UNDER HIS LEFT ARM AND NOBODY WALKED OVER TO HIM AND NOBODY ASKED HIM FOR ANYTHING. ON THE AFTERNOON OF THE LAST DAY OF THIS BLOCK HE CAME ACROSS THE YARD, PUT A PAGE ON THE BOARDS OF THAT FIRST TABLE WITH A FIGURE WRITTEN ON IT IN HIS OWN HAND, AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE COUNTED THE MARKS OFF THAT BOARD HIMSELF ON THE ELEVENTH DAY OF THE MONTH AND AGAIN ON THE LAST DAY OF IT AND GOT THIRTY BOTH TIMES, AND THAT HE WROTE IT THAT MORNING BEFORE HE CAME DOWN, AND THAT NOBODY ASKED HIM TO COUNT IT AND NOBODY ASKED HIM TO WRITE IT DOWN AND NOBODY ASKED HIM TO BRING IT OVER. A CLERK TOOK IT AND ENTERED THE FIGURE AND ENTERED THAT SHE IS NOT ENTERING IT AS A CHECK ON ANYTHING AND ENTERED THE REASON. HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT A MAN CAN DO A THING TWICE AND GET THE SAME BOTH TIMES AND THAT IS WHAT MAKES HIS FIGURE A FIGURE, AND THAT THE OTHER TWO IN THAT YARD ARE THAT, AND THAT HIS IS NOT, AND THAT THE REASON HIS IS NOT IS THAT THE OTHER TWO ARE TRADES AND HE IS NOT A TRADE, AND THAT NOBODY CAN ASK A MAN TO BE A TRADE FOR FOUR HOURS ON PURPOSE AND GET WHAT HE GOT THE FIRST TIME. HIS OWN FIGURE IS STILL NOT ON A PAGE ANYWHERE IN THIS VOLUME AND WAS NOT ENTERED, NOT CORRECTED, NOT ASKED FOR.**

## 9. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE

**SAID OUT LOUD, IN THIS BLOCK: THAT A MAN WHO HAS BEEN GETTING A FIGURE RIGHT EVERY MORNING FOR SIX MONTHS AND FINDS OUT THAT THE FIGURE IS A DAY OUT HAS NOT BEEN DOING ANYTHING WRONG AND HAS NOT BEEN DOING ANYTHING RIGHT AND THAT THERE IS NO THIRD THING FOR HIM TO HAVE BEEN DOING. THAT A CLERK WHO ASKS FOR A FIGURE HAS BECOME A SECOND PERSON IN THAT YARD WHO NEEDS A COLUMN AND THAT THERE ARE FOUR COLUMNS AND NO FIFTH AND THAT THE SECOND PERSON WHO NEEDS A COLUMN IS ALWAYS THE ONE NOBODY PUTS ONE IN FOR. THAT NINE PEOPLE HAD JUST TOLD HER WHICH END OF A COLUMN THEY WERE SURE OF AND THAT THERE WERE TWO ANSWERS AND BOTH HAD BEEN GIVEN WITH THE WHOLE CHEST AND THAT TWO CERTAINTIES ARE NOT A CONVENTION. THAT THREE FIGURES THIS DISTRICT MADE WITH ITS OWN HANDS ARE A SECOND TABLE, A FIGURE WORN INTO THE UNDERSIDE OF A STONE AND EIGHTEEN MARKS IN CHALK, AND THAT ONE OF THE THREE CAN BE CHECKED BY A STRANGER WHO WALKS UP AND PUTS A THUMB IN IT AND THAT THE OTHER TWO CANNOT BE CHECKED BY ANYBODY AT ALL. THAT A FIGURE SOMEBODY HANDS YOU IS A FIGURE SOMEBODY CHOSE TO HAND YOU, WHICH SHE SAID ON THE EIGHTEENTH DAY OF THE VOLUME AND WHICH A CLERK AGREED WITH HER WITH ON THE DAY SHE SAID IT AND IS STILL AGREEING WITH HER. AND THAT THERE IS A WORD FOR A FIGURE THAT IS TRUE FOR A WHILE AND THEN IS NOT, AND THAT SHE HAS IT, AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO SAY IT IN THAT YARD. **SHE HAS NEVER BEEN THANKED FOR ANYTHING SHE HAS SAID IN THAT YARD AND SHE SAID SO OUT LOUD AND SHE WAS NOT.** SHE IS IN A HOUSEHOLD.**

## 10. THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT

**CARRIES ELEVEN BUCKETS UP THAT BANK AND HAS DONE FOR NINETEEN YEARS AND READ THE THREE LINES IN THAT LOT BOOK STANDING UP ON HER WAY PAST EVERY WORKING DAY SINCE THE BOOK CAME OUT. IT STILL TAKES HER ABOUT TWO SECONDS, WHICH IS ABOUT HALF A SECOND LONGER ON THE THIRD LINE THAN ON THE FIRST TWO, AND SHE SAYS NOTHING TO ANYBODY AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAYS ONE WORD TO HER, AND THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED ON ALL FIFTEEN CHAPTERS. **SHE PUT HER THUMB ON THE THIRD LINE AND WIPED IT ON HER APRON ON ONE DAY OF THIS BLOCK, WHICH IS A THING A PERSON DOES AND NOT A THING A CLERK DOES, AND FOUR PEOPLE NOTICED AND ONE CLERK ENTERED THAT SHE IS A CHECK THAT HAS BEEN RUN ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED WORKING DAYS AND HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN ENTERED, AND ENTERED THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO ENTER WHAT THE HALF SECOND IS ABOUT. SHE READ THE THREE LINES ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS OF THIS BLOCK AND WAS ASKED NOTHING ON ALL FIFTEEN AND NOBODY HAS ASKED HER WHAT SHE MAKES OF ANYTHING AND NOBODY IS GOING TO.**

## 11. THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST

**CAME DOWN THAT BANK ABOUT ONCE A DAY OF THIS BLOCK AND STOOD ABOUT NINE FEET OFF THAT TABLE WITH HIS RIGHT HAND IN HIS COAT AND SAID FOUR THINGS OUT LOUD, ONE OF THEM THAT A YARD CAN HOLD A THING AND IT IS NOT A STRONG THING AND IT DOES NOT GET STRONGER BY BEING HELD AND THAT EVERY DAY IT IS HELD IS A DAY THE PEOPLE HOLDING IT GET BETTER AT HOLDING IT AND NOT ONE DAY BETTER AT SAYING IT, AND ONE OF THEM THAT A YARD WHICH HAS AGREED NOT TO SAY A THING IS A THING THAT CAN BE HELD FOR A WHILE AND THAT THE DAY IT STOPS BEING HOLDABLE NOBODY IN THAT YARD IS GOING TO BE ABLE TO SAY WHICH OF THEM LET IT GO, AND ONE OF THEM THAT A WARD THAT HE SAYS A HUNDRED AND ELEVEN DAYS IS NOT A FAILURE. HE WAS NOT THANKED AND HE WAS NOT GIVEN ANYTHING AND HE WAS NOT ASKED FOR HIS NAME ON ANY OF THE ELEVEN DAYS. HE IS NOT A FIGURE OF A YEAR AND MAY NOT BE NAMED AS ANYTHING EXCEPT ONE THING, WHICH IS THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO BE CALLED ANYTHING.**

## 12. THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR

**AT THE FOOT OF THAT LOW WALL WITH HIS COAT FOLDED ON THE STONES BESIDE HIM AND NOTHING IN HIS HANDS ON ALL ELEVEN DAYS, AT ABOUT HALF PAST THREE. ON HIS HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FOURTH NIGHT OF THAT RUN AT CHAPTER 456, RISING TO HIS HUNDRED AND SIXTY-FOURTH AT CHAPTER 470, AND HE HAS SLEPT ON A HUNDRED AND FIFTY-THREE OF THE FIRST HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FOUR NIGHTS, RISING TO A HUNDRED AND SIXTY-THREE OF A HUNDRED AND SIXTY-FOUR. GIVEN NOTHING: NOT SENT ON ANOTHER NINE DAYS, NOT ASKED TO SIT IN A SECOND CHAIR, NOT OFFERED THE THIRD LINE, NOT OFFERED THE STONE, NOT OFFERED A DAY, NOT OFFERED A THUMB, NOT OFFERED A BOOK, NOT OFFERED A PLACE TO STAND, NOT THANKED, NOT ASKED ANYTHING, AND FOUR PEOPLE WITHIN NINE FEET OF HIM DID NOT LOOK AT HIM ON ONE OF THESE DAYS. A CLERK ENTERED ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHAT HE IS GOING TO DO WITH HIS HANDS TONIGHT BECAUSE NOBODY ASKED HER. HE IS NOT A FAILURE, NOT A TRAGIC FIGURE AND NOT REDEEMED.**

## 13. THE FIGURES THAT ARE ABOUT THINGS AND NOT ABOUT PEOPLE, AND WHAT EACH ONE IS

| The figure | where it is on the page | what it is a figure of |
|---|---|---|
| The wear on the underside of a stone, one inch deep in the middle and nothing at either end | face up on the second table, and referred to in Chapter 459 and Chapter 467 | a book standing still on a table, and not a day and not a hand and not a man, and the one figure in that yard a stranger can check with a thumb |
| The three columns on that wall that are a day out | entered as three differences of one day, two of them in a clerk's own page and all three in one entry | the days they name, and nobody wrote down the mark any of them was cut from, and a clerk entered that the district chose none of it |
| A day written two ways in two of this district's own documents | Chapter 458, in a mouth and in an entry | a question about a writer and not a figure about a day, and a clerk entered that she is not choosing between the two |
| The date on the third line of that lot book | one date, printed under a wrong figure, unaltered since day 4 of the volume | **a man who was in a ditch on the fifteenth of the tenth month, and the district's one public expiry, and a figure nobody in this district can check** |
| Eighteen marks in chalk on the edge of the second table | a man's own count, one a morning since the morning the stone went face up | a thumb and a table, and not a day-count and not a way to pay anybody |
| Twenty-two paces | a man's own number, said twice and entered with a reason in his own words | two legs and a board, and not an instrument and not a thirteenth of twelve |
| A figure on a page out of a flat book | handed to a clerk in the open on the last day of the block | a person counting a board on his own, and the first figure in three volumes that nobody standing in that yard can check at all |
| The count of things this district has made | twelve, unmoved, with a clerk's entry that a pace is not a thirteenth | a table and a stone, and not a document and not a seventh of the six and not a keeper of anything |
| The count of marks off that board since the mark for the first of the twelfth month | twenty-two to thirty-two across the eleven days, and thirty at the count | a count of marks and not a figure about a month, until Chapter 467, where a finger went on both ends of it |
| The length of the twelfth month | thirty days, counted at Chapter 467 and entered as a figure about a count | a month that had never been counted, in a yard, in daylight, and a figure that reproduces is a figure about a count and is not a finding |

# VOLUME 10, BATCH 0002 — WHAT THE REVIEW-FIX PASS OF 2026-09-28 DID TO THESE PEOPLE

**NOTHING ABOUT A PERSON CHANGED EXCEPT IN ONE PLACE, AND IT IS THE SAME PLACE. A REPAIR PASS THAT MOVED A CHARACTER WOULD BE A PLOT CHANGE, AND THIS ONE DID NOT. EVERY FIGURE AGAINST A PERSON BELOW IS A FIGURE ABOUT A BODY AND NOT A FIGURE ABOUT A DAY, AND NONE OF THEM MOVED.**

- **THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX.** Untouched in the body. **WHAT CHANGED IS ONLY THAT CHAPTER 456 NO LONGER PRE-ENDS THE TELLING.** He is still not told anything on day 15, he is still told on day 19 in front of about nineteen people, he still takes his hand off the boards and puts it back and says nothing, and he is still not to be asked about it on any day of the remaining forty-one. **HIS FIGURE ON THE WALL AT CHAPTER 465 IS STILL THE ONLY FIGURE ON THAT WALL THAT WAS WRONG, AND THE WRONGNESS WAS IN THE NUMBER HE READS AND NOT IN HIM.**
- **THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS.** Untouched. Her two figures, the first plan she has entered in five volumes, the sentence about a figure somebody entered because they had to enter one that she has no word for the fifth of, the habit she entered as a finding, the day she is entering and not the chapter, the count of thirty she got off a board and the count she refused to give a yard, and her standing refusal to answer a question about a figure a man gets right every morning. **THE ONE NEW LINE ABOUT HER IS THAT THE FIGURE SHE ENTERED AT CHAPTER 470 CAME WITH A REFUSAL IN THE SAME ENTRY, AND THAT THE MAN WHO GAVE IT SAID THE REASON IN HIS OWN WORDS.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING.** Untouched, including the hand. It came out of its cloth once in this block, for a page, and no clerk entered that it is better than it was and no clerk may. **THE ONLY EDIT THAT CAME NEAR HIM IS THE WORDING OF A SENTENCE HE DID NOT SAY.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY.** **HIS ONE SPEECH AT CHAPTER 470 NOW SAYS HE COUNTED THE MARKS AGAIN *ON THE LAST MORNING OF THE MONTH*, WHICH IS WHAT HAPPENED, AND HE HAD SAID *YESTERDAY*, WHICH WAS TWO DAYS EARLY.** The figure is still thirty, the page is still out of his own flat book in his own hand, nobody asked him for it, he is still not a trade and still cannot be asked to be one for four hours on purpose, and his own figure is still not on a page anywhere in this volume.
- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE.** **THE ONLY THING THAT CHANGED ABOUT HER IS A DATE IN SOMEBODY ELSE'S REFERENCE TO HER.** She said the thing about a figure somebody hands you being a figure somebody chose to hand you in that yard, and the yard is told it was the eighteenth of this month, and it was the twenty-eighth. She is still not thanked, she is still not asked to stop, and she still has a word she will not say.
- **THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN.** Untouched, and **his count of the tally man's first speech at Chapter 470 is 79, re-measured, where it was 73 against a speech that was two words shorter than the one he is now counting.** He is still the only person in that yard who can tell a day apart from a page without being told, and he is still not writing on the fourth line he ruled.
- **EVERYBODY ELSE.** Unchanged: the man of about thirty-seven who cuts reeds and his refusal; the man of about thirty-two who puts tables up and his twenty-two paces; the man of about thirty-one who digs loam and his arm; the woman of fifty-eight and her half second; the man of about twenty-nine with the cart and his dragging wheel; the road keeper and his eleven miles; the man of about sixty-four on his hundred and sixty-fourth night with nothing in his hands; and the man the figure of twenty-one years is against, who was given nothing and said four things.

**AND THE ONE CHARACTER FACT THE REPAIR ADDED ISN'T A CHARACTER FACT AT ALL, WHICH IS THE POINT OF RECORDING IT HERE: THE FIGURES A PERSON IS CREDITED WITH SAYING ARE FIGURES ABOUT A DAY, AND ONE OF THEM IN THIS BLOCK DID NOT RE-DERIVE OFF ANY CHAPTER. THE NEXT BLOCK'S FIGURES ABOUT PEOPLE HAVE THE SAME EXPOSURE.**

---

# VOLUME 10, BATCH 0003 — WHAT THESE FIFTEEN CHAPTERS DID TO THE PEOPLE IN THEM

**EVERY FIGURE BELOW IS ON A PAGE. NONE OF IT IS A FIGURE ABOUT A PERSON UNLESS THE CHAPTER SAYS IT IS, AND THE ONE CASE WHERE A CHAPTER SAYS A FIGURE IS ABOUT A PERSON IS CHAPTER 481, WHERE A CLERK ANSWERS OUT LOUD THAT IT IS NOT, AND THAT ANSWER IS THE BLOCK'S THESIS AND NOT ITS FINDING.**

## 1. THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS

**SHE ENTERED THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL, ITS SECOND SAYING, THE TWO HALVES OF THE REASON THE BID CANNOT BE RUN ON, THE NUMBER OF NINE THUMBS, THREE CERTAINTIES AND NO FIGURE, A BOY'S ANNOUNCEMENT AND A REFUSAL TO ENTER IT AS A PLAN, AND THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE AS A FIFTH THING THAT IS NOT PAID. SHE READ AN ENTRY OUT LOUD THREE TIMES IN TWELVE DAYS AND NO NAME WENT INTO THE RULED COLUMN ON ANY OF THE THREE. SHE ASKED FOR A SECOND READER ONCE AND WAS REFUSED OUT LOUD AND ENTERED THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO ASK ANYBODY ELSE EITHER. SHE ANSWERED, OUT LOUD, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, WHETHER THE WEAR IN A STONE IS A FIGURE ABOUT A PERSON, AND SAID NO, AND THEN SAID OUT LOAD THAT NO IS ALSO THE ANSWER A DISTRICT GIVES WHEN IT WANTS A THING TO STOP MATTERING, WHICH IS THE SENTENCE THAT PROTECTS THE WHOLE BLOCK FROM THE TWENTY-FIRST ITEM OF THE INHERITED CONTRACT. SHE SAID SHE DID NOT KNOW FOUR TIMES AND ENTERED ALL FOUR. SHE SAID THE SECOND TIME SHE ENTERED SOMETHING BECAUSE SHE HAD TO, IT WAS A HABIT, AND A HABIT IS A FINDING, FOUR VOLUMES AGO, AND THE WARD WAS A THING I HAVE HAD TO ENTER BECAUSE A CLERK WHO COUNTS A THING ONCE HAS NOT WRITTEN IT DOWN, AND SHE HAS NOW SAID BOTH OF THOSE SENTENCES AGAIN INSIDE A SINGLE BLOCK WITHOUT ADDING A FIFTH FINDING, AND THE FINDING OF THAT IS HERS AND NOT THIS FILE'S.**

**WHAT SHE DID NOT DO: she did not enter the sentence as a rule, did not join its two reasons, did not copy a boy's number out of his page into hers, did not rule a column, did not ask the man of fifty-six a second question, did not ask the man who cuts reeds a second question, did not say who the fifth of five people is, did not say what the woman of about thirty-six has a word for, did not say what the man of about sixty-four will do with his hands tonight, and did not put a name in a column on any of fifteen days.**

## 2. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING

**HIS LEFT HAND HAS NOT CLOSED SINCE THE ELEVENTH OF THE JUNE AND IT WAS IN ITS CLOTH ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS AND IT CAME OUT ONCE, IN HIS OWN WORDS, IN A REASON HE GAVE UNASKED. He said, on day 30, in front of about nineteen people, that he can put that hand flat on a page and cannot make it do anything, and that if he were on a page it would be a figure about a day, and that he cannot put anything anywhere, and that there is no day in that hand to be out from. NOBODY ASKED HIM WHY THE HAND WILL NOT CLOSE. NO CLERK ENTERED THAT IT IS BETTER THAN IT WAS. HE ALSO SAID TEN WORDS ON DAY 27 ABOUT A WEAR BEING A FIGURE ABOUT A BOOK STANDING STILL, AND HE PUT HIS RIGHT HAND FLAT ON THE BOARDS ON THE OTHER SIDE OF THE STONE AFTER SAYING IT, AND HE SAID NOTHING ELSE. HE WAS QUIET ON SIX OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS AND WAS NOT ASKED ON ANY OF THEM. HE STOOD NINE FEET AWAY FROM NINE THUMBS GOING INTO A HOLLOW ON DAY 31 AND SAID NOTHING AT ALL. ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE HAVE SAID SINCE THAT A MAN LOOKED AT THE GROUND FOR ABOUT NINE MINUTES AFTER A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX SAID HIS ONE THING, AND THAT NOBODY IS GOING TO ASK HIM WHAT HE WAS DOING DURING THEM. HIS NAME IS NOT ON ANY PAGE.**

## 3. THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX

**HE READ FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL EVERY MORNING AND GOT ALL FOUR ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS AND WAS NOT ASKED ABOUT ANY OF THEM BY ANYBODY. ON DAY 35, IN SIXTEEN DAYS OF NOT SAYING ANYTHING ELSE IN THAT YARD, HE SAID ONE THING WITH HIS BACK TO THE YARD: THAT A FIGURE HE CANNOT CHECK IS WORTH EXACTLY WHAT A FIGURE HE CAN CHECK IS WORTH UNTIL THE MORNING SOMEBODY DOES CHECK IT, AND ON THAT MORNING IT IS WORTH NOTHING, AND THAT HE HAS READ FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL SINCE BEFORE THE MIDDLE OF THE SIXTH MONTH AND THAT TWO OF THE FOUR HE COULD GO AND LOOK AT AND TWO HE COULD NOT, AND THAT HE HAS KNOWN WHICH IS WHICH FOR SIX MONTHS AND NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT HAS EVER ASKED HIM. HE TURNED ROUND AFTERWARDS AND LOOKED AT THE GROUND ABOUT FOUR FEET IN FRONT OF HIM AND SAID HE WAS NOT GOING TO SAY ANYTHING ELSE THAT DAY. NOBODY ASKED HIM A SECOND QUESTION. NO SECOND READER WAS GIVEN TO HIM OR TO ANYBODY. HE WAS NOT OFFERED A STONE, A DATE, A FIGURE, A PAGE, A SENTENCE, A THUMB OR A BOOK. THE FIGURE HE READS THAT IS A DAY OUT FROM THE DAY IT NAMES IS STILL ON THAT WALL AND HE READS IT AND GETS IT AND NOBODY ASKED HIM ABOUT IT ON ANY OF THESE TWELVE DAYS.**

## 4. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP

**HE SAID THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL OUT LOUD, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, WITH A REASON ABOUT A HEEL AND A GAP AND THE SAME NUMBER TWICE. ON DAY 32 HE WAS ASKED IN THE OPEN WHETHER A TABLE IS AN INSTRUMENT AND HE ANSWERED FOR ABOUT SIX MINUTES, TOLD ABOUT NINE PEOPLE IN ADVANCE THAT HE WAS NOT GOING TO BE THANKED AND WAS NOT GOING TO WAIT TO BE THANKED, SAID WHERE THE COUNT OF TWELVE CAME FROM AND THAT IT IS ONE THING DONE TWICE, AND SAID THAT THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN A THING A MAN DOES TWICE AND A THING A MAN DOES ONCE IS NOT A DIFFERENCE ABOUT THE THING BUT ABOUT WHETHER ANYBODY IS ALLOWED TO ASK HIM FOR IT AGAIN. A CLERK ENTERED THE WHOLE OF IT IN HIS OWN WORDS AND ENTERED NO FIGURE OF HER OWN IN IT. HE WAS NOT THANKED. HE WAS NOT IN THAT YARD ON DAY 30 AND NOBODY SENT FOR HIM AND NOBODY ASKED HIM THAT HIS OWN SENTENCE WAS GOING TO BE SAID AGAIN BY SOMEBODY ELSE. THE COUNT OF WHAT THIS DISTRICT HAS MADE IS TWELVE AND DID NOT MOVE AND A TABLE IS NOT A THIRTEENTH AND A PACE IS NOT A THIRTEENTH.**

## 5. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS

**HE PUT HIS OWN MARK IN CHALK ON THE EDGE OF THAT SECOND TABLE EVERY MORNING OF THESE FIFTEEN DAYS, IN THE ORDINARY WAY, SAYING NOTHING, AND HE WAS NEVER ASKED FOR THE FIRST OF THEM. ON DAY 28 HE TRIED A SENTENCE A MAN SAID TWO DAYS EARLIER ON THREE FIGURES OF HIS OWN AND SAID OUT LOUD, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, THAT IT DID NOT WORK ON ANY OF THEM, AND GAVE EACH REASON IN HIS OWN WORDS, AND THE BEST OF THEM IS THAT BEING THE WRONG KIND OF THING IS NOT THE SAME AS BEING A THING. ON DAY 33 HE WAS CERTAIN, OUT LOUD, THAT THE BOY'S NUMBER IS A FIGURE, AND SAID SO IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, AND WAS NOT THANKED FOR IT AND WAS NOT ASKED WHY. ON DAY 36 HE WAS FOUR FEET AWAY WHEN THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE THINGS THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT HAVE WAS NAMED OUT LOUD IN A YARD, HE PUT HIS OWN MARK DOWN IN THE ORDINARY WAY AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST ELEVEN, HE SAID NOTHING AT ALL, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT HE WAS THERE AND ENTERED THAT NOBODY ASKED HIM WHETHER HE WAS THE MAN IT WAS ABOUT. HE CANNOT BE PAID FOR KEEPING THE COUNT AND A CHALK MARK IS NOT A WAY TO PAY ANYBODY AND THE COUNT OF THE FIVE IS STILL FIVE.**

## 6. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM

**HE WAS IN THAT DITCH EVERY MORNING OF THESE FIFTEEN DAYS. ON DAY 31 HE CAME UP WET TO THE KNEE AND PUT HIS THUMB IN THE HOLLOW IN THE UNDERSIDE OF THAT STONE WITHOUT TURNING IT, AND HELD THE STONE ABOUT TWO INCHES OFF THE BOARDS TO DO IT, AND SAID ONE THING: THAT HE COULD HAVE DONE IT ON ANY MORNING SINCE THE EIGHTH OF THIS MONTH AND DID NOT TWICE A WEEK AND NEVER ONCE THOUGHT ABOUT IT AND IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHY AND IS NOT GOING TO BE ASKED. HE WAS THE FIRST OF NINE THUMBS THAT MORNING AND HE SAID NOTHING ABOUT HOW DEEP IT IS. ON DAY 36 HE PUT HIS OWN RIGHT THUMB ON THE BOARDS ABOUT FOUR INCHES BELOW THE SECOND LINE OF THAT LOT BOOK AND KEPT IT THERE FOR ABOUT NINE SECONDS IN FRONT OF ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE, AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THAT YARD WHO HAS EVER PUT A THUMB ON THAT LINE, THAT HE KNOWS WHAT IT SAYS, THAT IT IS WRONG, THAT HE HAS KNOWN SINCE THE DAY THE BOOK CAME OUT, AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO BE ASKED A SECOND TIME ABOUT THE SAME FOUR INCHES OF A PAGE. A CLERK ENTERED WHAT IS UNDER HIS THUMB AND ENTERED NO FIGURE. THE SECOND LINE IS STILL WRONG ON THE FACE OF IT. HIS RIGHT ARM DID NOT GO ABOVE THE LEVEL OF THAT SHOULDER AT ANY POINT ON ANY OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS.**

## 7. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY

**HE STOOD AT THE EAST END OF THAT YARD WITH THE FLAT BOOK UNDER HIS LEFT ARM ON MOST OF THESE FIFTEEN DAYS AND WAS NOT SPOKEN TO BY ANYBODY. ON DAY 29 HE SAID ONE THING OUT LOUD, IN HIS OWN WORDS, ABOUT WHY HE WOULD NOT BE A SECOND READER: THAT A READER SOMEBODY PAYS FOR IS NOT A CHECK, THAT A READER SOMEBODY DOES NOT PAY FOR IS A FAVOUR, THAT THIS DISTRICT HAS NOT GOT THE MONEY FOR EITHER, AND THAT THE REAL REASON IS THAT THE FOUR WORDS AT THE FOOT OF HER PAGE HAVE BEEN THE SAME FOUR WORDS EVERY MORNING SINCE THE FIRST OF THE NINTH MONTH AND A SECOND READER WOULD HAVE TO SIGN THEM AND THERE IS NOWHERE IN THIS DISTRICT FOR A NAME TO GO. ON DAY 33 HE ASKED A CLERK OUT LOUD WHETHER SHE WAS GOING TO COPY THE NUMBERS OUT OF A BOY'S OWN PAGE, AND WAS TOLD SHE WAS NOT, AND ON DAY 33 AFTERNOON HE WAS CERTAIN, OUT LOUD, THAT THE BOY'S NUMBER IS NOT A FIGURE, BECAUSE IF IT IS NOT ON A PAGE IT DID NOT HAPPEN. HIS OWN FIGURE IS STILL NOT ON A PAGE ANYWHERE IN THIS VOLUME. ON DAY 34 NOBODY ASKED HIM WHETHER HE WAS ONE OF THE FIVE PEOPLE WHO CARRIED THAT BOOK.**

## 8. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE

**ON DAY 27 SHE SAID OUT LOUD, TO ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, THAT A FIGURE ANYBODY CAN CHECK WITH A THUMB IS A FIGURE THAT CARRIES NOBODY IN IT, AND THAT SHE HAD BEEN THE ONLY PERSON IN THAT YARD ARGUING FOR SIX WEEKS THAT A FIGURE HAS TO HAVE A PERSON IN IT TO BE WORTH ENTERING, AND THAT SHE WAS WRONG, AND SHE ASKED FOR IT TO BE RECORDED THAT SHE WAS WRONG BY A THUMB. ON DAY 30 AFTERNOON SHE ASKED A CLERK OUT LOUD WHICH OF TWO SENTENCES IN AN ENTRY WAS THE FINDING AND WAS TOLD SHE DID NOT KNOW. ON DAY 33 AFTERNOON SHE WAS CERTAIN THAT THE BOY'S NUMBER IS A FIGURE ABOUT HIS MORNING AND NOT A FIGURE ABOUT THE STONE, AND SAID ONCE MORE THAT SHE HAS A WORD FOR A FIGURE THAT IS TRUE FOR A WHILE AND THEN IS NOT AND THAT IT IS NOT THIS ONE, AND DID NOT SAY IT. ON DAY 35 SHE SAID OUT LOUD THAT THE YARD IS GOING TO SPEND THE REST OF ITS LIVES NOT ASKING THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX AND THAT THAT IS CORRECT AND IS A DIFFERENT THING FROM NOT KNOWING. SHE WAS ASKED NOTHING ON ANY OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS AND SAID SEVERAL THINGS ANYWAY AND WAS NOT THANKED FOR ANY OF THEM.**

## 9. THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST

**HE ASKED WHETHER A TABLE IS AN INSTRUMENT AND HE REFUSED, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, TO LET TWO MEN WHO ARRIVED AT ONE SENTENCE BY DIFFERENT ROADS BE CALLED A PATTERN. HE REFUSED A CLERK'S REQUEST FOR A SECOND READER IN A BREATH, GAVE THE REASON THAT A JOB IS A COLUMN AND THERE ARE FOUR AND NOT A FIFTH, AND SAID THAT SHE HAD ASKED HIM IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE SO THAT HE COULD NOT GO HOME AND THINK ABOUT IT. ON DAY 30 AFTERNOON HE READ A CLERK'S PAGE STANDING UP IN ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS, SAID THAT EVERYBODY IN THAT YARD DECIDES WHICH HALF OF A PAGE IS THE INTERESTING ONE, SAID THAT HE DECIDED, AND SAID THAT HE HAS NOT GOT A WAY OF TELLING ANYBODY WHAT HE DECIDED ON. ON DAY 34 HE ASKED OUT LOUD WHO THE FIFTH OF FIVE PEOPLE WHO CARRIED THAT BOOK IS. ON DAY 36 HE READ THE HEADING OF THAT LOT BOOK ALOUD IN A YARD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, DID NOT READ THE LINE THAT IS A MAN'S MOUTH, AND NAMED THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE THINGS A DOCUMENT THAT SETS A LOT OUT HAS TO SAY. HE WAS NOT THANKED ON ANY OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS AND A CLERK ENTERED ON EVERY ONE OF THEM THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO THANK HIM. HE HAS ASKED FOR NOTHING AND BEEN GIVEN NOTHING.**

## 10. THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN

**HE HAS TWO COLUMNS OF HIS OWN AND HAS NEVER BEEN GIVEN A THIRD PLACE TO PUT A NUMBER. HE COUNTED WHAT PEOPLE SAID IN HIS OWN PAGE, OUTSIDE BOTH OF HIS COLUMNS, ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS AND READ THE NUMBER BACK TO HIMSELF IN A LOW VOICE SIXTY TIMES OVER THEM. ON DAY 27 HE ASKED A CLERK OUT LOUD WHETHER A HOLLOW IN A STONE IS A FIGURE AND WHETHER IT IS ONE BECAUSE A BOOK WAS PUT ON TOP OF IT, AND WAS TOLD SHE DID NOT KNOW. ON DAY 33 HE COUNTED NINE THUMBS GOING INTO THE HOLLOW, SAID THE NUMBER OUT LOUD TO NOBODY IN PARTICULAR, AND IT WAS TEN TWO SECONDS LATER, AND HE SAID HE HAS NOT GOT A WAY OF WRITING DOWN A NUMBER THAT IS A DIFFERENT NUMBER ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AFTER HE SAID IT. ON DAY 33 AFTERNOON HE READ HIS OWN PAGE OUT LOUD FOR THE FIRST TIME IN A MONTH. ON DAY 33 HE NOTICED THAT THE THIRD OF FOUR FIGURES A MAN READS EVERY MORNING IS THE SAME FIGURE AS THE ONE ON A SHEET NINE HUNDRED YARDS OFF, AND HE SAID NOTHING OUT LOUD ABOUT IT FOR ABOUT THREE HOURS, AND HE IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THAT YARD WHO HAS NOTICED. ON DAY 34 HE ASKED A CLERK OUT LOUD, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, WHETHER THE WEAR IN THAT STONE IS A FIGURE ABOUT A PERSON. ON DAY 36 HE ASKED A CLERK WHETHER A BID IS A FIGURE. ON DAY 37 HE SAID OUT LOUD, TWICE, THAT HE IS GOING TO KEEP COUNTING THE THUMBS INTO THAT HOLLOW EVERY MORNING UNTIL SOMEBODY TELLS HIM TO STOP, AND NOBODY DID, AND A CLERK ENTERED IT AND ENTERED THAT SHE IS NOT ENTERING IT AS A PLAN BECAUSE A PLAN IS THE ONE THING IN THIS DISTRICT WITH NO FIGURE UNDER IT. HIS NUMBER IS IN NO COLUMN OF ANYBODY'S AND IS IN HIS OWN PAGE AND IS ON NO PAGE OF THIS DISTRICT.**

## 11. THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT

**SHE CARRIED TWO FULL BUCKETS DOWN THAT BANK AND READ THE THREE LINES IN THAT LOT BOOK STANDING UP ON HER WAY PAST ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS AND SAID NOTHING TO ANYBODY AND NOBODY SAID ONE WORD TO HER. ON DAY 30 AFTERNOON SHE PUT A THUMB INTO THE HOLLOW IN THAT STONE WITHOUT SLOWING DOWN. ON DAY 30 AFTERNOON SHE READ A CLERK'S PAGE STANDING UP IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, WHICH IS THE SAME TIME IT TAKES HER TO READ THE THREE LINES IN THE BOOK, AND SAID NOTHING ABOUT WHAT SHE GOT OUT OF EITHER, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT AND ENTERED NO FIGURE. ON DAY 33 HER THUMB WENT INTO THAT HOLLOW IN THE MIDDLE OF A BOY SAYING A NUMBER OUT LOUD, AND THE NUMBER CHANGED. ON DAY 35 SHE WALKED PAST A MAN WHO HAD JUST TOLD ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE THAT HE COULD END TWO ARGUMENTS BEFORE LUNCH, AND DID NOT STOP. THE HALF SECOND SHE TAKES ON THE THIRD LINE RATHER THAN THE FIRST TWO IS STILL A CHECK THAT HAS BEEN RUN ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED WORKING DAYS AND HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN ENTERED.**

## 12. THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR, THE ROAD KEEPER, THE MAN WITH THE CART, AND THE MAN WHO IS NOT ARRIVING

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR WAS AT THE FOOT OF THAT LOW WALL WITH HIS COAT FOLDED ON THE STONES BESIDE HIM AND NOTHING IN HIS HANDS ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS. HE WAS ON HIS HUNDRED AND SIXTY-FIFTH NIGHT OF THAT RUN AT THE FIRST OF THEM AND HIS HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-SIXTH AT THE LAST, AND HE HAS SLEPT ON A HUNDRED AND SIXTY-FOUR OF THE FIRST HUNDRED AND SIXTY-FIVE AND A HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-FIVE OF A HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-SIX. HE WAS OFFERED NOTHING, ASKED NOTHING, GIVEN NOTHING AND THANKED FOR NOTHING ON ANY OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS, AND A CLERK ENTERED ON EVERY ONE OF THEM THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHAT HE IS GOING TO DO WITH HIS HANDS TONIGHT BECAUSE NOBODY ASKED HER.**

**THE ROAD KEEPER CAME UP THAT LANE ONCE ON EVERY ONE OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS, GOT THE FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL AS HE WENT PAST, AND WAS NOT ASKED ABOUT THE ELEVEN MILES ON ANY OF THEM. ON DAY 27 HE STOPPED FOR ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AT THE SECOND TABLE WITH HIS HAND OUT AND HIS THUMB AN INCH OFF THE STONE AND WENT ON. ON DAY 35 HE STOPPED FOR ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AND LOOKED AT A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX STANDING AT THAT WALL AND WENT ON.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES A CART CAME UP THAT LANE AND WENT ON UP IT ON EVERY ONE OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS AND THE NEAR WHEEL ON THAT CART WAS STILL DRAGGING AND HE GOT IT UP ABOUT NINE INCHES OFF THE GROUND WITH ONE ARM AND ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE WATCHED HIM DO IT AND SAID NOTHING.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-FOUR WITH A CASE, THE MAN WITH THE PORTFOLIO, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE WHO KEEPS A ROAD HOUSE AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY AT A COUNTER ARRIVED ON ZERO DAYS IN THIS BLOCK AND STAY ON ZERO DAYS.**

## 13. WHAT THE REVIEW REPAIR DID TO THE PEOPLE IN SECTIONS 9 TO 12, AND WHAT IT DID NOT

**THE REPAIR REWROTE SENTENCE SHAPES AND NOTHING ELSE. EVERY PERSON IN THIS BLOCK IS IN EVERY CHAPTER THEY WERE IN, AT THE TIME THEY WERE THERE, DOING WHAT THEY WERE DOING, AND NOT ONE WAS ASKED ANYTHING THEY WERE NOT ASKED BEFORE. WHAT CHANGED IS THE ENGLISH THEY ARE CARRIED IN, AND THE FIGURE BELOW IS THE MEASUREMENT OF THAT.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR, THE ROAD KEEPER, THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES A CART, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM, THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT AND THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX WERE THE SEVEN PEOPLE WHOSE SENTENCES STOOD VERBATIM IN MORE THAN ONE CHAPTER.** Between them their carrier sentences stood in 124 places, and the man of about sixty-four's alone stood in fourteen of the fifteen. Each is now in a different sentence in each chapter, and the scene, the coat on the stones, the dragging near wheel, the nine inches, the flat book under the left arm, the water to the knee, the three lines read standing up, and the four figures got off the wall are all exactly as section 12 and sections 9 to 11 record them.

- **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR.** On all fifteen days at the foot of that low wall with his coat folded on the stones beside him and nothing in his hands. Offered nothing, asked nothing, given nothing, thanked for nothing. A hundred and sixty-fifth night of that run on the first day, a hundred and seventy-sixth on the last, and he has slept on a hundred and sixty-four of the first and a hundred and seventy-five of the last. **The sentence carrying him is now in four chapters in the form it always had and in the other eleven in a different form, and the string *at the foot of that low wall with his coat folded on the stones* is at 4 and not at 15.** Both figures are at the block record's section 4.7. A writer who wants him back at fifteen has to put fifteen different sentences on one man, and that is the finding, not a loss.
- **THE ROAD KEEPER.** Came up that lane once on every one of the fifteen days, got the four figures off the wall as he went past, and was not asked about the eleven miles on any of them. Stopped for about four seconds at the second table on day 27 with his thumb an inch off the stone. Stopped for about four seconds and looked at a man of fifty-six at that wall on day 35. **The clause *was not asked about the eleven miles* is at 11 and not at 15, and the eleven is still the eleven.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES A CART.** Came up that lane and went on up it on every one of the fifteen days with the near wheel still dragging, and he got it up about nine inches off the ground with one arm, and about four people watched him do it and said nothing. **The phrase *got it up about nine inches* is at 14 and not at 15, because the figure is a figure and the figure did not move and the sentence around it did.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY.** Stood at the east end of that yard on every one of the fifteen days and was not spoken to. **Nothing about him changed at all except the English.**
- **THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT.** Two full buckets down that bank on all fifteen days, three lines read standing up, nothing said and nothing said back. **Nothing about her changed at all except the English.**

**AND THE ONE FIGURE THAT IS A FIGURE ABOUT A PERSON AND WAS NOT TOUCHED: THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING PUT HIS OWN HAND INTO A SPOKEN REASON AT CHAPTER 475 AND SAID THAT HE CAN PUT IT FLAT ON A PAGE AND CANNOT MAKE IT DO ANYTHING. THAT LINE IS CANON AND IT IS THE REASON THE VOLUME'S FIXED FINAL CHAPTER IMAGE IS PARTLY SPENT, AND IT IS RECORDED AT `state/open-threads.md` SECTION 54 ITEM 2 AND HANDED TO THE CLOSE BY NAME. A WRITER WHO OPENS THIS FILE NEXT MUST NOT SPEND IT AGAIN, AND MUST NOT UNSPEND IT EITHER, BECAUSE UNSPENDING IT IS A CHANGE OF PLOT.**

# Volume 10 Batch 0004 — Character State, Chapters 486 to 500, Days 38 to 50

> Appended after the prose. **WHERE A CHAPTER AND THIS APPEND DISAGREE, THE CHAPTER IS CANON AND THIS APPEND IS WRONG.** No person in this block was given anything, and no person was made to be a figure about anything by anybody but themselves.

## 1. The cast, and what each of them did on these thirteen days

- **THE PROTAGONIST, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING.** In the yard on thirteen of the thirteen days except the last two, where he is not in that yard at all and nobody has asked. **He said six things out loud and was asked for nothing and was given nothing.** At Chapter 487 he said in about four seconds that the stone is doing a job, that the job is holding a corner down in a wind, that it has no figure on it and is not going to get one, and that he has not been paid for it and is not going to be, and he put his right hand flat on the boards of the second table twice in one morning and neither time was on a stone. **HE PUT NO HAND ON A STONE AND SPOKE NO WORD ABOUT HIS OWN HAND IN ANY OF THE FIFTEEN CHAPTERS, WHICH IS THE DISCIPLINE THE VOLUME'S FIXED FINAL IMAGE NEEDED AND THE REASON CHAPTER 475'S SPEND WAS NOT REPEATED.** At Chapter 488 he said that nobody in that yard is going to throw either of them out and that it is the first thing he has been able to say in there in five volumes that is not about his hand. At Chapter 490 he said that four people in that yard had said out loud this month that they were not going to answer something and that none of the four is a rule. At Chapter 493 he put his own thumb in the hollow and said he had not known anybody was counting. At Chapter 495 he told a clerk to read a thing out loud tomorrow or say she could not. At Chapter 497 he said he could not work out which of three things was the right one. At Chapter 499 and 500 he was not in that yard.
- **THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS.** Entered on all fifteen days, read something out loud in that yard on six of them, entered the figure on the sheet at that gatepost and its age on all fifteen, entered the four figures off the wall on thirteen of fifteen, entered the count of that man of fifty-six's nights as a figure and her own refusal of it in one entry at Chapter 492, refused out loud twice to read a thing she had not worked out, entered that she does not know which of two figures is the better one, entered the word *unchecked* in the margin of her own page over nothing at Chapter 487, and did not look up for about four minutes at Chapter 500 while about nine people stood at that end of the table. **SHE ASKED NOBODY FOR A NAME AND NO NAME WENT INTO THE RULED COLUMN ON ANY OF THE SIX DAYS SHE READ A THING OUT LOUD.** She also said out loud, at Chapter 485, that she is not going to say what the man of about sixty-four is going to do with his hands tonight because nobody asked her, on all fifteen days.
- **THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX.** Read the four figures off that wall twice a day on all fifteen days, got all four every time, and this is the two hundred and thirteenth to the two hundred and twenty-fifth of the mornings he has done it. **He said four things out loud in this block and they are the block's substance: at Chapter 491 that he had got all four every morning for six months and had never once been asked whether he was right, and that a man that morning said a sentence with one of his four in it and did not ask him anything either, and that he has never once written one of them down; at Chapter 499 that a figure is for a person to find out something they could not find out any other way and that neither figure in that yard is doing it, and that he has been reading one of them for two hundred and twenty-four mornings and did not know that about nine people were counting him. He was not asked which two of his four he could go and look at on any of the thirteen days and he did not say.** He put his thumb in the hollow once, at Chapter 500, for about two minutes and a half, and did not look down at that second table on any other day.
- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE.** **She has had a word for a month that she has not said, and this block has her say three more times that she has it and is not going to say it: at Chapter 495, at Chapter 496 and at Chapter 500, where she stands four feet off a man with a thumb in a hollow for about a minute and says she would have said it if he had asked.** She also said out loud at Chapter 486 that a yard can hold two answers to one question and that she would not build a yard that could not, and that she spent six weeks arguing the other way and was wrong by a thumb. At Chapter 499 she said a figure is also for a person to be caught by. At Chapter 487 she said out loud, deliberately, that a man who drives a cart up that lane every morning of his life has just turned over a stone he has never looked at twice, and that she wanted nine people to have heard that and that there is nothing in it for anybody to use. At Chapter 494 she entered herself among the four people who let a man who digs loam, in front of about nineteen people and not to one person.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM.** In that ditch every morning and out of it at about ten on all thirteen days, saying the four figures at the near end of that first table four times over. **He said two things out loud, at Chapter 494 and at Chapter 498, and the first of them asked the yard to enter that being let is a thing that happened to him, and the second said out loud that he does not know which of the two figures is the better one, and that he has got one thumb in a hollow and knows how deep it is and that is all he knows about it, and that he is the man the third line of that book is about and has never once said so out loud before.** A clerk entered the claim and entered neither the third line nor a date.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP.** Down that lane and up it five times in eleven days before he said one sentence with both figures in it on the twentieth morning after the count. **He said four things out loud: that he had not been thanked and is not going to be and is not going to wait to be thanked; a sentence carrying both figures; keep both; and, at Chapter 496, that being true is the thing this district has least use for and that he came down the lane to find out whether a true thing does anything at all and has found out and is going back up.** He built the second table in the first week of the month and has not been paid for it and has worked out, and said out loud at Chapter 499, that the reason he built it was not to find anything out. **HE WAS NOT THANKED ON ANY OF THE THIRTEEN DAYS.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS.** At the end of that second table on most of the thirteen days, heard all of it, and put his own mark in chalk on the edge of that table once a morning, forty-three of them in a row by the last day, and said nothing at all on all thirteen. **He is the man the fifth of the five things this district does not have is about, and a clerk entered that he was there on nine of the days and that nobody asked him whether any of it was about him and that she is not going to, and that he has thirty-eight marks in chalk on that table and has never been asked for one of them and has never been paid for one of them.**
- **THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN.** In that yard on eleven of the thirteen days and counting thumbs in that hollow on most of them, since the nineteenth of the twelfth month. **He said eight things out loud across the block, and three of them are the block's argument: that two hands in one hollow at once is either a figure about two people or a figure about a stone and he cannot get from here to which; that a figure which cannot be checked is not a figure he can argue about either and that the two of those are the same shape and one of them has a man in it, and that a page is not nothing; and that he is going to keep counting until somebody tells him to stop and nobody is going to and he is not writing it anywhere because he has not been given a column.** He asked out loud at Chapter 499 what a man of fifty-six is for and nobody answered him, and a clerk entered that she did not answer it and entered the reason. A clerk refused twice to copy one word of his number into her own page.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY.** At the east end of that yard on all thirteen days with the flat book under his left arm, and he opened it in the open for about four seconds at Chapter 491 with nobody standing near him. **He said two things out loud, and the first asked a question in front of about nineteen people: whether a person is allowed to write a newer figure beside an older one, and he did not ask her to and said so out loud; and the second, when she had answered, that he has asked the wrong question twice in thirty years and would have found out in about eleven days by watching two figures go past him on a wall, and that he is not going to thank her for it because she did not do it for him.** He was given nothing and nobody asked him anything until he asked.
- **THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST.** At the near end of that first table on day 42 and read two lines out of a three-line lot book in front of about nineteen people in about eleven seconds, and did not read the third one, and said out loud in advance that he was not going to answer the question about why, and that a man who has not worked out an answer is allowed to not say one. **HE SAID TWO THINGS OUT LOUD IN THE WHOLE BLOCK AND THEY WERE THE SAME SUBJECT.**
- **THE ROAD KEEPER.** Up that lane once on every one of the thirteen days, got the four figures off that wall as he went past, and was not asked about the eleven miles on any of them. **He said nothing at all in this block.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES A CART.** Up that lane and on up it on every one of the thirteen days with the near wheel still dragging, and he got it up about nine inches off the ground with one arm every day. **HE STOPPED ONCE, AT CHAPTER 487, WHICH HE DOES NOT DO, AND TURNED A STONE OVER AND WENT ON UP THE LANE, AND HE PUT HIS OWN THUMB IN THE HOLLOW ON ANOTHER DAY AND SAID NOTHING ABOUT HOW DEEP IT IS. HE SPOKE NO WORDS IN A YARD IN THIS BLOCK AND NOBODY ASKED HIM ANY.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR.** At the foot of that low wall with his coat folded on the stones beside him and nothing in his hands on all fifteen chapters, on his hundred and seventy-seventh night of that run rising to his hundred and ninetieth, having slept on a hundred and seventy-six of the first hundred and seventy-seven rising to a hundred and eighty-nine of a hundred and ninety. **GIVEN NOTHING. NOT SENT ON ANOTHER TWELVE DAYS, NOT ASKED TO SIT IN A SECOND CHAIR, NOT OFFERED A PLACE, A DATE, A FIGURE, A PAGE, A THUMB, A STONE, A BOOK OR A SENTENCE, NOT THANKED, NOT REDEEMED, NOT A FAILURE AND NOT A TRAGIC FIGURE. A CLERK ENTERED ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHAT HE IS GOING TO DO WITH HIS HANDS TONIGHT BECAUSE NOBODY ASKED HER. ABOUT NINE PEOPLE WITHIN FOUR FEET OF HIM WERE BUSY WATCHING A MAN AT THE END OF A TABLE ON THE LAST AFTERNOON AND NOBODY OFFERED HIM ANYTHING.**
- **THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT.** Two full buckets down that bank and back up it on all fifteen chapters, and she read the three lines in that lot book standing up on her way past and said nothing to anybody, and nobody there said one word to her. **She said nothing on all thirteen days and a clerk entered on Chapter 488 that about four people have said since that a woman who has come up that bank twice a day for a volume has never once been inside that yard and that nobody has ever worked out whether that is a choice. NOTHING WAS GIVEN TO HER AND SHE ASKED FOR NOTHING.**
- **THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS, WHICH IS AGAINST A MAN, AND WHO HE IS NOT CALLED ANYTHING BY.** Nobody in these fifteen chapters calls him anything and nobody has asked what the figure of twenty-one years is against and a clerk entered on Chapter 490 that nobody asked him whether the man the figure of twenty-one years is against is the man the fifth of the five is about and that she is not going to.

## 2. What this block did to no one

**NOBODY WAS GIVEN ANYTHING ON ANY OF THE THIRTEEN DAYS. NOT ONE PERSON WAS PAID, NOT ONE PERSON WAS APPOINTED, NOT ONE PERSON WAS SENT ANYWHERE, NOT ONE PERSON WAS ASKED TO SIT ANYWHERE, NOT ONE PERSON WAS OFFERED A PLACE, A DATE, A FIGURE, A PAGE, A THUMB, A STONE, A BOOK OR A SENTENCE, AND NOT ONE PERSON WAS THANKED. THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE THINGS THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT HAVE IS A WAY TO PAY A PERSON WHO IS NOT IN A HOUSEHOLD AND IT WAS NOT PAID ON ANY OF THE THIRTEEN DAYS, AND THE COUNT OF IT IS STILL FIVE AND NO SIXTH WAS PROPOSED.**

**AND NOBODY ARRIVED. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-FOUR WITH A CASE, THE MAN WITH THE PORTFOLIO, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE WHO KEEPS A ROAD HOUSE AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY AT A COUNTER ARE ON ZERO DAYS AND STAY ON ZERO DAYS. NOBODY WENT UP THAT BANK. NO CHILD OF ABOUT EIGHT WAS ASKED ANYTHING AND NO CHILD'S THUMB WENT INTO THAT HOLLOW.**

## 3. The one figure a person was entered as, and what it is not

**A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM ASKED FOR A THING TO BE ENTERED AND THE THING IS THAT BEING LET IS A THING THAT HAPPENED TO HIM, AND A CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS ENTERED IT AND ENTERED THAT BEING LET IS NOT A FIGURE AND IS NOT A PAYMENT AND IS NOT A COLUMN. A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE ENTERED HERSELF AMONG THE FOUR PEOPLE WHO LET HIM. IT IS THE ONE MOMENT IN THESE FIFTEEN CHAPTERS IN WHICH THIS DISTRICT PUTS A PERSON ON A PAGE AS A THING THAT HAPPENED TO THEM, AND IT IS NOT A COLUMN AND A CLERK SAYS SO IN THE SAME ENTRY, AND A BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN ASKS IN THE SAME WEEK WHETHER A THING NOBODY HAS TO WRITE DOWN IS A FIGURE, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING SAYS NOBODY IN THAT YARD IS GOING TO THROW EITHER OF THEM OUT.**

## 4. The one absence that is on the page

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING WAS NOT IN THAT YARD AT ANY POINT ON DAY 49 OR ON DAY 50, AFTER THIRTEEN DAYS OF SAYING SOMETHING IN IT, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT HE WAS NOT IN THAT YARD AND ENTERED THAT SHE DID NOT ASK AND ENTERED THE REASON, WHICH IS THAT NOBODY HAS ASKED HER ANYTHING, AND ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE AT THE NEAR END OF THAT FIRST TABLE HAVE SAID SINCE THAT THEY DO NOT KNOW WHEN HE STOPPED COMING AND HAVE NOT ASKED, AND THAT THE YARD HAS NOT WORKED OUT WHETHER THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX CROSSED THOSE FOUR FEET BECAUSE THAT MAN WAS NOT GOING TO BE STANDING THERE, AND NOBODY HAS SAID THAT OUT LOUD AND NOBODY IS GOING TO. HE IS NOT A FAILURE AND HE IS NOT REDEEMED AND HE IS NOT A TRAGIC FIGURE AND HE IS NOT GONE.**
