# VOLUME 11 CLOSE — THE CHARACTER STATE AT THE FIFTIETH MORNING

> **APPENDED BY THE VOLUME 11 CLOSE PHASE, NOT REWRITTEN ABOVE. NO CHAPTER WAS EDITED AND NOBODY WAS GIVEN ANYTHING BY THIS PHASE. MEASURED OVER THE FIFTY CHAPTER FILES: 62, 38, 17, 11, 29, 3, 11, 68, 32 and 13 chapters respectively for the people named below, whole string, case-insensitive.**

**NO PERSON IN THIS VOLUME HAS A NAME. EVERY PERSON IS A MAN OR A WOMAN OF ABOUT AN AGE AND A TRADE, AND THAT IS TRUE OF EVERY ONE OF THE FIFTY CHAPTERS AND OF THE FIVE HUNDRED AND THIRTY BEFORE THEM, AND IT IS THE FIRST THING A VOLUME 12 WRITER HAS TO DECIDE WHETHER TO KEEP.**

## 6. THE CAST AT CHAPTER 550, ONE LINE EACH, AND WHAT CHANGED IN THIS VOLUME

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING.** The protagonist. **MEASURED ON 2026-09-29 AFTER THE REPAIR OF THIS SECTION, AND THE FIGURE THAT WAS PRINTED HERE BEFORE THAT REPAIR WAS `62 of 50 chapters`, WHICH IS A COUNT OF SIXTY-TWO OUT OF FIFTY AND IS NOT TRUE OF ANYTHING: HE IS NAMED IN 459 OF THE 580 CHAPTERS WRITTEN IN THIS REPOSITORY AND IN 30 OF THE 30 CHAPTERS OF VOLUME 12 THAT EXIST, AND HE IS THE MOST-MENTIONED FIGURE IN THE VOLUME. THE OLD FIGURE WAS CARRIED IN FROM AN EARLIER BLOCK AND WAS NEVER RE-DERIVED.** He is at the end of the second table on the last morning With his right hand flat on the boards and not on the stone and not on the copy, and about nine people have said since that this is the first morning anybody has watched where his hand was, and a clerk entered that she is not entering that as anything. **In this volume he said out loud, on day 42, that the only way to tell a stranger which of two sheets is the district's own is for the person who made it to stand beside it and say something about himself, and on day 47 that a fourth line has to be a sentence a person would say about himself, out loud, standing next to it, with his hands doing nothing, and on day 49 one thing about his own trade which was not his name.** **He is not named on any page and his name is not a Volume 12 writer's to settle.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM.** 38 chapters. The person the third line of that lot book is about. He said out loud on day 43, unasked, that a date is the only kind of figure he has ever been in, and that he has been in two, and that nobody has asked him about either. **He was not asked to agree to the fourth line, was not asked whether the nine words are true, came up out of the ditch on the morning they went on and put his own thumb in the hollow in that stone, pressed, took his thumb out, and said one word about nothing. About four people at that table have said since that they do not know whether he agreed, and a clerk entered that she was not asked and did not ask.** He is the only person in this volume whose body is the argument of a book and who was never asked about the book.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THAT CART.** 17 chapters. He put the copy on the second table on the first morning of the volume and had not read one word of it and was not asked about it. He carried it against his own chest for about a minute on day 46 and put it back about an inch along and gave a reason out loud, which is that the far end of that table is going. **He read it out loud in the middle of that yard at about half past four on day 48, the first time anybody has, and said out loud that the third line is ruled and it is empty, and afterwards said out loud that he has said a thing about that sheet and not about himself and that it is still not the book.** A clerk entered that she is not entering his name and entered the reason, which is that a reading is done by whoever is standing there and standing in a yard is not a post, and the column was ruled and empty at about six. **He is the person who made the copy, and he stood beside it, and the one thing that would have settled which sheet is the district's is a thing said about himself, and he did not say it.**

**A CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS.** 389 mentions, 50 of 50 days, and the only person in this volume who touches every figure. She entered the difference between two of the four figures without entering what the difference is, on day 3. She wrote nine words on a fourth line in her own hand at nobody's dictation on day 48 at about ten, in the open, in daylight, in front of about nineteen people, and read them out loud, and entered that no person was asked before that book was written, and entered that no column is going to be ruled for it and why. **She said out loud on day 41 that she does not know what that sheet is for, which put `I do not know` on a page in this district for the first time in six volumes. She said out loud on day 44 that a fourth line is not a column and that a column is something a person looks down and a line is something a person reads, and refused three sentences and put her pen down flat on the boards and did not pick it up again. She said out loud on day 49 that she is not going to start the name question now that four people have looked at him.** Her standing is `the record about the not asking says not asked`, on 216 occasions across 50 of 50 days.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX.** 11 chapters. He reads four figures off that wall every morning, twice, and got all four of them, and about nine people standing within hearing of that wall have said since that a man who has said those four every morning since before the middle of the sixth month has never been asked which two of them he could go and look at. **Two of the four he reads are out and he is told so every morning.** He read that wall for the last time on day 50.

**THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN.** 68 chapters, the second most-mentioned figure in the volume. He counts the words in a speech and reads the number back to himself in a low voice, 226 times on 50 of 50 days. **He counted 225 things in this volume and every one of them is the real word count of the speech in front of it.** He counted the nine words on day 47 before anybody wrote them down. He counted ninety-three marks in chalk on day 50 and ninety-three words in the reading on day 48. **The largest count in the volume is 141 at Chapter 535 and the last is one hundred and ten at Chapter 550, and `tools/measure.py` returns `None` for every one of the eighteen counts above a hundred.** He has not been asked anybody's name on any of the fifty days and has not asked for one.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR.** 3 chapters and 49 days, because the relay that carries him is a string and the string is on 49 of the 50. **Given nothing on 50 of 50 and asked nothing on 50 of 50, at the foot of that low wall with his coat folded on the stones beside him and nothing in his hands, on his hundred and eighty-ninth night of that run having slept on a hundred and eighty-eight of them.** He was not asked a fourth question, and asking is not offering, and a fourth question is a habit. **Those two night figures are his and not the clerk's, they are two separate rows of the ladder, and a review read the `his` in the clerk's four-o'clock entry as the clerk's and was wrong.** He ends Volume 11 having been given nothing on every day of it.

**THE ROAD KEEPER.** Comes up that lane twice a day, reads the four off the wall as he goes by, and was asked about the eleven miles on 49 of the 50 days. **On day 50 about four people at that wall said out loud that a book in that yard has said its own third line is a person and that the man who comes up that lane twice a day has read four off that wall on every morning of this volume and has never read that book.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE.** Put the copy on her own counter in front of customers and said out loud she cannot tell which of the two books is the one this district made, and about nine people in her shop could not either, and two of them were people who had been in that yard. **Shut her own shop for a morning on day 40 and was not thanked, and on day 50 said out loud that she has not been thanked and is not going to be and that about four of them have been putting those two things next to each other all week and they are not next to each other.** She carried a counter weight up that bank on day 45 and left it on the far end of that second table all day, and the cart had to swing wide of it.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-THREE WHO TAKES IN WASHING AT THE SECOND CHANNEL.** Asked, in front of about nineteen people, on day 41, what that sheet is for. She is the only person in this volume to have asked the question the yard spent a month on, and she got four words back.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT.** 32 of the 50 days. She reads the three lines in that lot book standing up on her way past and says nothing to anybody and nobody there says one word to her, on every one of the days she is on. **On day 48 she came past a book that had had a fourth line on it since about ten that morning and did not look at it, and on day 50 she did not look at the fourth line, and about four people have said since that she reads the first three and goes on, and that that is the whole of what she has ever done with that book.** She is the volume's quietest measure of what a fourth line is for.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY.** Refused, out loud, with a reason, the column for the name of whoever read a thing out loud, and the reason is that a reading is done by whoever is standing there and standing in a yard is not a post. He has kept a tally for about thirty years. On day 45 he said out loud that the district would give up the one instrument it has that goes out of date on its own.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS.** 29 chapters. He put his mark in chalk on the edge of that second table on day 34 and said out loud that he is not moving his own mark to meet a sheet of paper, and a clerk entered that the chalk does not reach back as far as the row of cuts and entered no reason why.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP.** 9 chapters. Held a sheet up in the light for about eleven minutes on day 42 and said he could not tell which one he was holding. Took a splinter out of a board about a foot from that book with his own knife on day 47 to clear a place for a hand. **Put the stone back on the corner of that book with both hands on day 49, unasked, and said he is not going to be thanked.** **He and the man who cuts reeds share an age and an occupation and that is a property of the cast, and a close that wants it fixed must say so as a re-scope and not as a typo.**

**A BODY FOUR HUNDRED MILES OFF.** 118 to 167 days past a printing it did not make, no face, nobody watched it, and no arrival of anything is calendared anywhere in the fifty chapters. Nobody arrived on any of the fifty days and no new person was added to this district.

## 7. THE RELATIONSHIP STATE, AND WHAT MAY NOT BE DONE WITH IT

**THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM AND THE MAN WHO MENDS FENCING ARE THE RELATIONSHIP THIS VOLUME HAS AND IT WAS NEVER SPOKEN.** **THEY WERE NOT THE SAME AGE AND THIS LINE PRINTED THAT THEY WERE, FROM AN EARLIER BLOCK, UNTIL THE REPAIR OF 2026-09-29: THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM IS OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE AND THE PROTAGONIST IS OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR, BECAUSE THEY WERE BOTH PRINTED AS `of about thirty-four` AND A CAST IN WHICH EVERY FIGURE IS AN AGE AND A TRADE CANNOT CARRY TWO MEN OF ONE AGE, AND ON FOURTEEN OF THE FIFTEEN MORNINGS OF BLOCK 0002 THEY STOOD AT THE END OF THE SAME TABLE WITHIN TWO PARAGRAPHS OF EACH OTHER. THE THING THIS PARAGRAPH IS ABOUT WAS NEVER THEIR AGE; IT IS THAT THEY ARE BOTH IN THIS YARD ON OVERLAPPING DAYS AND NEITHER OF THEM SAYS ONE WORD TO THE OTHER IN ANY OF THE FIFTY CHAPTERS, AND THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM PUT HIS THUMB IN A HOLLOW IN A STONE ON THE DAY THE WORDS WENT ON WHILE THE MAN WHO MENDS FENCING STOOD AT THE END OF THE SAME TABLE.** **About four people at that table have said since that they do not know whether he agreed. That sentence is the relationship and it is the whole of it, and a Volume 12 that resolves it has resolved something a page said four times that it does not know.** The romance of Volume 09 and 10 did not go up that bank in any of the fifty days of this volume and there is no page on which to stand.

**THE PROTAGONIST'S NAME IS THE RELATIONSHIP NOBODY HAS.** **He is named in 459 of the 580 chapters written and in 30 of the 30 chapters of this volume that exist, and the `62 of the 50 chapters` this line printed before the repair of 2026-09-29 was false, and the false figure was the one a writer reads to size him.** He is on no page with a name, and the volume's own method is that a person says a thing out loud in a yard and a clerk writes it down, and it was applied 225 times in this volume and zero times to him.

## 8. THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN, AND THE DAY HE ALREADY DID THE THING THE LAST BLOCK IS CREDITED WITH

**HE IS ON 68 OF THE 50 CHAPTERS AND HE IS THE SECOND MOST-MENTIONED FIGURE IN THE VOLUME, AND ONE OF THE THINGS THE VOLUME'S LAST BLOCK SAYS HAPPENED FOR THE FIRST TIME ON DAY 48 HAPPENED UNDER HIS MOUTH ON DAY 15.**

On the fifteenth of the second month, Chapter 515, at about eleven, standing between that first table and that second table, in the ordinary voice, in front of about nineteen people, **the boy of about nineteen read the two third lines out loud, one after the other**, and about nine people have said since that it is the first time that anybody in that yard has read the two third lines against each other out loud, on purpose, with both of them in his mouth at once. He read the book's third line, which is a date, and then he said of the copy's third line: **`And that one is nothing, and I have read it out loud, and it is still nothing, and it is going to be nothing this afternoon and it was nothing yesterday morning.`** Nobody answered him. **A clerk of nineteen years entered that a boy said a thing out loud in that yard and entered the thing, and entered that an empty line was read out loud in that yard in front of about nineteen people and that reading it out loud did not make it a figure, and entered that she is not going to enter that it did not make it a figure either**, and the record about the not asking says not asked.

**HE ALSO PUT IT IN THE LEDGER OF THAT CHAPTER, or the yard did and he is the one it is about: `A piece of paper with two lines on it and nothing in the third of it has been on the end of the second table in that yard for the fifteenth morning, and a boy read its empty third line out loud in front of about nineteen people this morning and it was still nothing when he had finished.`**

**THE CLAIM THAT THE READING ON DAY 48 IS THE FIRST TIME THAT SHEET HAS BEEN READ OUT LOUD BY ANYBODY IS CONTRADICTED BY THIS CHAPTER AND BY CHAPTER 504, WHERE A WOMAN WHO TAKES IN WASHING READ THAT SHEET STANDING UP TWICE, AND BY CHAPTER 517, WHERE THE MAN THE THIRD LINE IS ABOUT READ A COPY OF THAT DATE IN A SHOP. THE CHAPTERS ARE CANON AND WERE NOT EDITED AND THE DOCUMENTS THAT CARRY THE CLAIM ARE NOT A CLOSE'S TO EDIT. The full finding is at `state/volume-11-close.md` section 7.9.**

**WHAT THIS DOES TO HIM AND NOT TO HIM.** It does not make his day-15 act smaller. He read an empty line out loud in front of about nineteen people, on purpose, thirty-three days before anybody thought to do it with a document in his hand, and the yard noticed, and a clerk wrote it down, and the ledger of that morning says `it was still nothing when he had finished`. **It makes the day-48 reading narrower, and the narrower thing is a better beat: a man of about twenty-nine who drove that cart put a sheet down on the first morning of the volume, had not read one word of it in forty-eight mornings, and on the last block stood in the middle of that yard and said a thing out loud about it and not about himself.** That is the thing that had not happened. The empty line had been said out loud already, and had been nothing both times, and the district had entered that on day 15 in advance of needing it.

# VOLUME 12 BATCH 0001 — CHARACTER STATE, APPENDED AFTER THE FIFTEEN CHAPTERS

> **APPENDED BY THE VOLUME 12 BATCH 0001 PHASE, NOT REWRITTEN ABOVE. NO WORD ABOVE THIS BLOCK WAS CHANGED AND NO CHAPTER WAS EDITED AFTER ITS LAST PROSE CHANGE.**

## 9. WHO IS ON THE PAGE IN THIS BLOCK, AND WHAT EACH ONE DID

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING, THE PROTAGONIST, UNNAMED.** **ON THE PAGE OF FIFTEEN OF FIFTEEN MORNINGS, AT THE END OF THAT SECOND TABLE, WITH HIS RIGHT HAND FLAT ON THE BOARDS AND NOT ON THE STONE AND NOT ON ANY SHEET, AND HE ASKED NOBODY ONE QUESTION ON ANY OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS.** He said one thing out loud on each of the fifteen days about his own work — string, a rail wanting three turns and then four, nine lengths and two posts, the water turning on a bank at an hour he has never been able to put on paper, a boy who offered to count a month — and on the seventh day he said that standing in front of a thing is what this yard has done instead of looking at where it came from, and on the fifteenth he said that he did not go up that bank with the sheet and is not going to say why and about four of you will think of a reason. **He was not thanked for asking nobody anything, because he did not do it in front of anybody, and nobody noticed it, and that is the state the next block inherits and must not spend.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM, THE PERSON THE THIRD LINE IS ABOUT, NOT NAMED ON ANY PAGE.** **ON THE PAGE OF SEVEN OF THE FIFTEEN MORNINGS AND HE IS THE FIGURE THIS BLOCK EXISTS AROUND.** He crossed the yard with a barrow and stopped at neither table on the twenty-second. **He read the fourth line of the stranger's sheet at about a quarter to eleven on the twenty-fifth and said nothing for about a minute, and nobody asked him one word.** He did the same at about eleven on the twenty-sixth and went back down the bank. **He read the fourth line again at about half past nine on the twenty-ninth and said nothing to anybody and went back down the bank, and a clerk entered that she is not entering that he read it, and the reason is that a man reading with his own eyes in a yard is not a reading in this district.** He was in the yard at about ten on the second of the fourth month and watched a sheet go up that bank and said nothing and was asked nothing. **HE WAS NEVER ASKED WHETHER THE NINE WORDS ARE TRUE AND HE NEVER SAID ANYTHING ABOUT THEM AND HE AGREED TO NOTHING.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THAT CART, WHO MADE THE FIRST COPY.** **ON THE PAGE OF TWELVE OF THE FIFTEEN MORNINGS AND HE DID NOT READ ANYTHING OUT LOUD IN THIS BLOCK.** He said on the first morning that he has been standing at that table waiting to be asked for fifty mornings and nobody has. He said on the third that he has offered twice to say where that sheet came from and nobody asked. He said on the seventh, **unasked and in front of about nineteen people, that the new sheet is not his hand and that he would rather say it than have somebody say it for him and be right.** He said on the tenth that being not asked is the loudest thing anybody has said in that yard for a month. He said on the twelfth that he would rather be doubted than be useful. **He refused a reading four more times in this block and said the same thing every time. AND ON THE FIFTEENTH MORNING HE SAID THE REASON OUT LOUD BEFORE HE PUT HIS HAND ON A SHEET, LIFTED IT, CARRIED IT UP THAT BANK IN HIS OWN TWO HANDS AND PUT IT ON A SHOP COUNTER, AND HE SAID THAT IF IT IS THE WRONG SHEET THEN ABOUT NINE PEOPLE IN THAT SHOP WILL FIND OUT AND HE IS NOT GOING TO BE STANDING THERE.**

**A CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS.** **SHE ENTERED THE NEW COUNT OF SHEETS EVERY MORNING OF THIS BLOCK, WHICH IS THE FIRST COUNT IN THIS MANUSCRIPT THAT RISES.** She moved the count of documents this district does not own from three to four on the twenty-sixth and entered the person and the reason. She refused a boy's offer to count the month on the twenty-fourth and gave the reason out loud. She refused to enter where a sheet came from on the twenty-eighth and gave the reason out loud. She said out loud on the thirtieth that the count of documents did not move because the four is a kind and not a figure about four things, and that a figure may not be changed by anybody including her. She said out loud on the thirty-first that she is not entering whether that morning is the last of the month. **She entered no name anywhere in this block and no column was ruled, and the column for the name of whoever read a thing out loud was ruled and empty at about six on all fifteen days and was named in the ledger of every one of them.**

**THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN.** 97 counted claims in fifteen chapters, every one of which is the real word count of the speech in front of it, read the number back to himself in a low voice, and **not one mismatch**. He counted the nine words on the stranger's fourth line twice and said he was not going to read them out loud. He said the first half of a question out loud on the twenty-ninth and said out loud that he is not going to say the second half until somebody else says the first half to him. He said out loud on the twenty-second that he had hoped a count would move.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE.** **HER SHOP WAS OPEN ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS AND SHE LOST NO MORNING OF TRADE IN THIS BLOCK.** She stood in that yard for an hour on the thirtieth with her door open watching that lane and is the person the count of three is entered against. **On the second of the fourth month a man carried a sheet up that bank in his own two hands and put it on her counter and she said out loud, in front of about nineteen people and then in front of her own customers, that she cannot tell which of them is this district's, and that nobody is going to thank her and she is not going to be thanked.** She said out loud on the twenty-seventh that a shop is a place where a thing comes through a door.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY.** **HE IS THE PERSON THE COUNT OF DOCUMENTS THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT OWN IS ENTERED AGAINST, AND HE SAID THE REASON OUT LOUD BEFORE THE CLERK WROTE IT DOWN.** He named out loud on the nineteenth that there is a month in this year nobody in this yard has ever counted off a board. He said out loud on the thirtieth that the four is a figure about a kind and not about four things. **He turned a page in his own book on the last night of the third month and did not write on it, and said out loud that he has kept that book for about thirty years and has never had a blank page in it at this time of the month.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS.** **HE IS THE PERSON THE FIRST RISE IN THE COUNT IS ENTERED AGAINST AND HE GAVE THE REASON OUT LOUD.** He put his chalk mark at about a quarter to eight on the twenty-fifth where he always puts it and said out loud that there was one sheet then and there are two now and he had not been away from that yard. He said out loud that neither of the two sheets has ever been wet.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP.** He said out loud on the twentieth that he does not keep a book of people and is not going to start one. He gave the reason out loud on the twenty-seventh for not lifting a sheet off a table whose wood is going. He said out loud on the second of the fourth month that a table is a table and he does not care what is on it.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-THREE WHO TAKES IN WASHING AT THE SECOND CHANNEL.** She came up that bank on six mornings, set a basket on the boards of that second table because the step is two inches higher than the bank, and took it off again. **She said out loud that she washes for eleven houses and could name about nine men in the parish and was not going to hand them to a question nobody asked her, and then on the twenty-eighth she said out loud that she knows which of the eleven houses the paper came from and is not going to say.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT.** **ON THE PAGE OF ALL FIFTEEN MORNINGS, READING THREE LINES IN THAT LOT BOOK STANDING UP ON HER WAY PAST.** **ON THE twenty-THIRD OF THE THIRD MONTH SHE STOPPED AT THE END OF THE THIRD LINE AND READ THE FOURTH, AND SAID NOTHING, AND NOBODY THERE SAID ONE WORD TO HER, AND A CLERK REFUSED TWO SENTENCES ABOUT HER OUT LOUD THE SAME MORNING.** On the twenty-fourth she did not stop. On the twenty-fifth she also looked at the fourth line of a second sheet for about as long as it takes to count nine. She read the three lines on the other thirteen days.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX.** **HE READ FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL TWICE ON EVERY ONE OF THE FIFTEEN MORNINGS AND GOT ALL FOUR BOTH TIMES, AND FROM THE SECOND MORNING OF THIS BLOCK HE READ THEM WITHOUT LOOKING AT THE WALL, AND HE WAS ASKED NOTHING ON ANY OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS.** This is the two hundred and seventy-sixth through the two hundred and ninetieth of those mornings, all fifteen on the page.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR.** **AT THE FOOT OF THAT LOW WALL WITH HIS COAT FOLDED ON THE STONES BESIDE HIM AND NOTHING IN HIS HANDS ON ALL FIFTEEN MORNINGS, GIVEN NOTHING AND ASKED NOTHING ON ALL FIFTEEN, ON HIS HUNDRED AND NINETIETH NIGHT OF THAT RUN THROUGH HIS TWO HUNDRED AND FOURTH, HAVING SLEPT ON A HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-NINE OF THEM THROUGH TWO HUNDRED AND THREE.**

**THE ROAD KEEPER.** **HE CAME UP THAT LANE TWICE A DAY ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS AND READ THE FOUR OFF THAT WALL AS HE WENT BY AND WAS NOT ASKED ABOUT THE ELEVEN MILES ON ANY OF THEM, AND THE RELAY IS WHOLE.** On the twenty-ninth he stood at that wall for about as long as it takes to say four figures, which is longer than he has stood at it, and **about four people at that wall noticed and about four of them did not say the other thing they were going to say.** He has never read that book.

## 10. THE RELATIONSHIP STATE AFTER FIFTEEN DAYS, AND IT IS THE SAME ONE IT WAS

**THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM AND THE MAN WHO MENDS FENCING ARE BOTH ON THE PAGE ON SEVEN AND FIFTEEN OF THE FIFTEEN MORNINGS RESPECTIVELY AND NEITHER OF THEM SAYS ONE WORD TO THE OTHER IN ANY OF THE FIFTEEN CHAPTERS.** The loam-digger read the fourth line of a stranger's sheet twice and the fence-mender was at the end of that same table on the first of those two mornings and had said, out loud, in about eleven seconds, that there is a thing in this yard that this yard did not make and that standing in front of a thing is what we have done instead of looking at where it came from. **ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE AT THAT TABLE HAVE SAID SINCE THAT ONE MAN SAID A THING ABOUT A PAGE AND THE OTHER STOOD THERE, AND THAT NEITHER OF THEM LOOKED AT THE OTHER WHILE IT HAPPENED.** The romance of Volumes 09 and 10 did not go up that bank in any of the fifteen days of this block and there is no page on which to stand.

**THE PROTAGONIST'S NAME IS ON NO PAGE OF SIX HUNDRED AND SIXTY-FIVE CHAPTERS AND IT WAS NOT SAID IN A MOUTH IN ANY FORM IN CHAPTERS 551 TO 565. HE WAS IN A POSITION FOUR TIMES IN FIFTEEN DAYS AND DID NOT CROSS IT AND NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK PROMISES IT FOR A LATER ONE.**

# VOLUME 12 BATCH 0002 — CHARACTER STATE, APPENDED AFTER THE FIFTEEN CHAPTERS

> **APPENDED BY THE VOLUME 12 BATCH 0002 PHASE, NOT REWRITTEN ABOVE. NO WORD ABOVE THIS BLOCK WAS CHANGED. WHERE A CHAPTER AND A DOCUMENT DISAGREE THE CHAPTER IS CANON.**

## 11. WHO IS ON THE PAGE IN THIS BLOCK, AND WHAT EACH ONE DID

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING, THE PROTAGONIST, UNNAMED. ON THE PAGE OF FIFTEEN OF FIFTEEN MORNINGS, AT THE END OF THAT SECOND TABLE, WITH HIS RIGHT HAND FLAT ON THE BOARDS AND NOT ON THE STONE AND NOT ON ANY SHEET, AND HE ASKED NOBODY ONE QUESTION ON ANY OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS.** He said one thing out loud on each of the fifteen mornings about his own work, and about a rail that wants five turns, a post at the end of the week, three rails and a post, a rail loose in the rain, and a fence he did not do. **On the twenty-fourth he said out loud that he had four different reasons ready for a man and had had them for an hour and a half and was not going to say one of them. ON THE TWENTY-FIFTH HE SAID OUT LOUD, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, THAT HE HAD STOOD ABOUT NINE FEET OFF THAT MAN FOR A MONTH WITH A QUESTION READY EVERY MORNING AND WAS NOT GOING TO ASK IT, AND THAT THE MAN IN THAT YARD WHO WORKED IT OUT IS NOT ME. ON THE TWENTY-EIGHTH HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT A REMEDY IS A THING YOU HAVE IN YOUR POCKET BEFORE YOU NEED IT AND THAT THIS DISTRICT HAS NOT GOT ONE. ON THE THIRTIETH HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE HAD COME TO THE END OF THAT MONTH OF DAYS WITH THE NINE FEET EXACTLY WHERE HE STARTED, AND THAT HE HAD BEEN STANDING IN ABOUT NINE INCHES OF A DIFFERENT NINE FEET ALL WEEK.** **HE WAS NOT THANKED FOR ASKING NOBODY ANYTHING, BECAUSE HE DID NOT DO IT IN FRONT OF ANYBODY, AND NOBODY NOTICED IT, AND THAT IS THE STATE THE NEXT BLOCK INHERITS AND MUST NOT SPEND.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM, THE PERSON THE THIRD LINE IS ABOUT, NOT NAMED ON ANY PAGE. ON THE PAGE OF SEVEN OF THE FIFTEEN MORNINGS AND HE IS THE FIGURE THIS BLOCK EXISTS AROUND. HE CAME UP THAT BANK AND STOOD AT THE END OF THAT SECOND TABLE AND SAID NOTHING AT ALL TO ANYBODY ON DAYS 18, 19, 20, 21, 22, 25 AND 27, AND ON DAY 25 HE STOOD THERE UNTIL ABOUT A QUARTER PAST TWELVE AND THEN WENT BACK DOWN THAT BANK. NOBODY IN THAT YARD ASKED HIM ONE WORD ON ANY OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS. HE WAS NEVER ASKED WHETHER THE NINE WORDS ARE TRUE, HE SAID NOTHING ABOUT THEM, AND HE AGREED TO NOTHING.** A clerk entered on day 26 that he stood with his back to four pages and entered nothing about it, and on day 25 that she is not entering whether he heard.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THAT CART, WHO MADE THE FIRST COPY. ON THE PAGE OF ELEVEN OF THE FIFTEEN MORNINGS. HE SAID IN THAT YARD ON DAY 22 A THING ABOUT HIMSELF FOR THE FIRST TIME IN SIX VOLUMES, IN ABOUT NINE SECONDS, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE: I DO NOT SLEEP IN THIS PARISH, I HAVE SLEPT IN NINE PLACES SINCE THE FIRST MORNING OF THAT YEAR, AND I HAVE GOT FOUR OF MY OWN TEETH, AND ABOUT FOUR OF YOU HAVE BEEN LOOKING AT THE END OF THAT TABLE AS THOUGH THERE WERE SOMETHING ON IT ABOUT ME, AND THERE IS NOT.** A clerk entered all of it and entered that it is not a name and that she is not entering where he sleeps, with the reason that a clerk is not the person anybody owes a thing to. **ON DAY 29 HE SAID THE REASON OUT LOUD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE BEFORE HE PUT HIS HAND ON A SHEET, LIFTED IT, CARRIED IT UP THAT BANK IN HIS OWN TWO HANDS AND PUT IT ON A SHOP COUNTER. ON DAY 25 HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE MADE ONE COPY IN HIS LIFE IN ABOUT A QUARTER OF AN HOUR OFF THE BOOK ON THAT STONE AND THAT NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM HOW LONG IT TOOK. ON DAY 26 HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE IS NOT A SACK OF FLOUR AND IS NOT OFFERING HIMSELF TO BE PUT ON A PAGE.**

**A CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS. SHE ENTERED THE NEW COUNT OF SHEETS EVERY MORNING OF THIS BLOCK, WHICH IS THE FIRST COUNT IN THIS MANUSCRIPT THAT RISES. SHE ENTERED THE COUNT OF DOCUMENTS THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT OWN EVERY MORNING AND IT DID NOT MOVE, AND ON DAY 27 SHE LOOKED AT IT AGAIN IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHAT THE FIFTH ONE WOULD BE. SHE REFUSED TO READ AN ENTRY OUT LOUD ON DAY 20 AND GAVE THE REASON OUT LOUD. SHE REFUSED TO RULE A COLUMN ON DAY 23 AND GAVE THE REASON OUT LOUD, AND ON DAY 25 SHE SAID OUT LOUD THAT SHE WILL REFUSE TO FILL A COLUMN IF ONE IS RULED. SHE ENTERED THAT A COPY OF A FIGURE COSTS SOMEBODY SOMETHING TO MAKE AND THAT THE REASON THE NINE WORDS ARE ON THOSE PAGES IS THE REASON THEY ARE ON THAT BOOK. SHE ENTERED THAT A MAN WAS ASKED A QUESTION ON THAT LANE ON DAY 27 AND ANSWERED IT. SHE ENTERED THAT SHE DOES NOT KNOW WHO CARRIED A SHEET BACK UP THAT BANK AND ENTERED THAT SHE IS ENTERING THAT SHE DOES NOT KNOW INSTEAD OF A NAME. SHE ENTERED NO NAME ANYWHERE IN THIS BLOCK AND NO COLUMN WAS RULED, AND THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD WAS RULED AND EMPTY AT ABOUT SIX ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS AND WAS NAMED IN THE LEDGER OF EVERY ONE OF THEM.**

**THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN. 100 COUNTED CLAIMS ACROSS FIFTEEN CHAPTERS, EVERY ONE OF WHICH IS THE REAL WORD COUNT OF THE SPEECH IN FRONT OF IT, READ THE NUMBER BACK TO HIMSELF IN A LOW VOICE, AND NOT ONE MISMATCH.** **ON DAY 25 HE ASKED THE QUESTION THAT CHANGED THE VOLUME, IN THE OPEN, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, AND HE PAID HIS OWN PLEDGE FROM THE TWENTY-NINTH OF THE MONTH BEFORE OUT LOAD WHEN A MAN CALLED IT: NOBODY HAS SAID THE FIRST HALF, SO I HAVE SAID THE FIRST HALF AND THE SECOND HALF TOGETHER AND THEY ARE THE SAME QUESTION, AND I AM NOT GOING TO STAND HERE AND PRETEND TO ABOUT NINETEEN OF YOU THAT IT IS NOT.** On day 20 he said out loud that he is not keeping a count of refusals. On day 18 he said out loud that whoever is bringing the pages is bringing them before the yard fills up is a thing he has worked out and is not going to be the one who says the next part. On day 27 he said out loud that the man who has been asked about those eleven miles four hundred times had never once been asked whether the road ends.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE. HER SHOP WAS OPEN ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS AND SHE LOST NO MORNING OF TRADE IN THIS BLOCK.** She said out loud on day 26 that a shopkeeper can tell you the price of a thing and she has been waiting a month for somebody to work that out for a page. She said out loud on day 28 that a stranger coming to her counter will ask her something she cannot answer and nobody has asked her what she would do if he did. She said out loud on day 29 that about four of the people who come into her shop now say nothing at all when they get to the page, and that that is worse for her than the nine who still ask. She is holding two pages on her own counter with the door open.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-THREE WHO TAKES IN WASHING AT THE SECOND CHANNEL. SHE CAME UP THAT BANK ON SEVEN MORNINGS, SET A BASKET ON THE BOARDS OF THAT SECOND TABLE BECAUSE THE STEP IS TWO INCHES HIGHER THAN THE BANK, AND TOOK IT OFF AGAIN. ON DAY 17 SHE SAID OUT LOUD, BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED HER, THAT A PAGE CAME DOWN THAT LANE AND IS NOT OFF ANY OF THE ELEVEN HOUSES SHE WASHES FOR, AND THAT SHE WOULD RATHER BE THE ONE WHO SAYS IT THAN THE ONE WHO DOES NOT. ON DAY 19 SHE SAID OUT LOAD THAT SHE KNOWS EXACTLY WHAT THE THIRD LINE IS BECAUSE SHE HAS SEEN A DATE ON ABOUT NINE MEN'S OWN PAPERS, AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHOSE. ON DAY 27 SHE GAVE HER REASON FOR ASKING A MAN A QUESTION OUT LOUD BEFORE SHE ASKED IT, AND SHE ASKED IT, AND THE MAN ANSWERED HER. ON DAY 28 SHE NAMED THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE OUT LOUD, WHICH IS A REMEDY, AND SAID THAT IT IS WHAT HAPPENS WHEN THAT PERSON SAYS NO, AND THAT SHE HAS WATCHED ABOUT NINE OF HER ELEVEN HOUSES NOT PAY A BILL THEY OWED AND SHE KNOWS EXACTLY WHAT THAT IS. SHE HAS STILL NOT SAID WHICH HOUSE.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY. HE IS THE PERSON THE COUNT OF FOUR IS ENTERED AGAINST, AND HE GAVE THE REASON OUT LOUD BEFORE THE CLERK WROTE IT DOWN, ON DAYS 17, 27, 28, 29 AND 30. HE ANSWERED THE BOY'S QUESTION ON DAY 25 AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT THE THIRD LINE IS THE ONE LINE NOBODY CAN COPY OFF ANYBODY AND THAT HE HAS KNOWN THAT SINCE HE WAS ABOUT THE BOY'S AGE. ON DAY 30 HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT THIS DISTRICT HAS WORKED OUT IN A FORTNIGHT THAT A PAGE CANNOT CARRY A PERSON, AND THAT HE HAS A MAN AT THAT WALL WHO HAS SAID FOUR THINGS OUT LOUD TO HIM EVERY MORNING SINCE BEFORE THE MIDDLE OF THE SIXTH MONTH AND WHOM HE HAS NEVER ONCE ASKED. HIS FLAT BOOK WAS NOT OPENED ON DAYS 2 TO 4 OF THIS BLOCK AND HE SAID OUT LOUD WHY.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS. HE IS THE PERSON THE FIRST RISE IN THE COUNT IS ENTERED AGAINST, AT CHAPTER 568, AND HE GAVE THE REASON OUT LOUD BEFORE THE COUNT WAS WRITTEN DOWN.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP. HE WROTE A DATE IN CHALK ON THE UNDERSIDE OF THAT SECOND TABLE ON DAY 23, WHICH IS THE FIRST THING THAT DISTRICT HAS EVER KEPT THAT WAS NOT A BOOK, AND A CLERK REFUSED TO RULE A COLUMN FOR IT OUT LOUD, WITH A REASON.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-THREE, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT, THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR.** The woman of about fifty-eight read three lines in that lot book standing up on her way past on all fifteen evenings and said nothing to anybody. The man of fifty-six read four figures off that wall twice on every one of the fifteen mornings, got all four both times, **read them twice in one morning on day 26 for the first time in about two volumes, and was asked nothing on any of the fifteen days. THIS IS THE TWO HUNDRED AND NINETY-FIRST THROUGH THE THREE HUNDRED AND FIFTH OF THOSE MORNINGS, ALL FIFTEEN ON THE PAGE.** The man of about sixty-four was at the foot of that low wall with his coat folded on the stones beside him and nothing in his hands on all fifteen mornings, **given nothing and asked nothing on all fifteen, on his two hundred and fifth night of that run through his two hundred and nineteenth, having slept on two hundred and four of them through two hundred and eighteen.**

**THE ROAD KEEPER. HE CAME UP THAT LANE TWICE A DAY ON ALL FIFTEEN DAYS AND READ THE FOUR OFF THAT WALL AS HE WENT BY. HE WAS NOT ASKED ABOUT THE ELEVEN MILES ON FOURTEEN OF THE FIFTEEN DAYS. ON DAY 27 HE WAS ASKED A REAL QUESTION OUT LOUD BY A WOMAN IN THAT YARD, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST ONE, AND HE STOPPED FOUR FEET SHORT OF THE GATE AND ANSWERED IT IN ABOUT NINE SECONDS AND SAID ONE MORE THING IN ABOUT NINE SECONDS, AND NOBODY ASKED HIM A SECOND QUESTION. THE RELAY IS BROKEN ON THAT ONE DAY AND ON NO OTHER.** He has still never read that book.

## 12. THE RELATIONSHIP STATE AFTER FIFTEEN DAYS, AND IT IS THE SAME ONE IT WAS, AND THE ONE THING THAT MOVED

**THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM AND THE MAN WHO MENDS FENCING WERE BOTH ON THAT TABLE ON DAY 25, ABOUT NINE FEET APART, AND NEITHER OF THEM SAID ONE WORD TO THE OTHER.** The loam-digger read the fourth line of a stranger's sheet on day 18 with the man of about thirty-seven who cuts reeds about nine feet from the other end of the table. **ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE AT THAT TABLE HAVE SAID SINCE THAT ONE MAN SAID THE WHOLE OF THE VOLUME OUT LOUD AT ABOUT ELEVEN AND THE OTHER STOOD THERE WITH A QUESTION READY AND DID NOT USE IT, AND THAT NEITHER OF THEM LOOKED AT THE OTHER WHILE IT HAPPENED.** The romance of Volumes 09 and 10 did not go up that bank in any of the fifteen days of this block and there is no page on which to stand.

**AND THE ONE RELATIONSHIP THAT MOVED IS NOT A ROMANCE AND IS THE WORTHIEST THING ON THE FIFTEEN MORNINGS: THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN AND THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY. THE MAN CALLED OUT THE BOY'S PLEDGE FROM THE TWENTY-NINTH OF THE MONTH BEFORE AND THE BOY PAID IT OUT LOAD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, AND THE MAN'S NEXT PUBLIC STATEMENT ABOUT IT, ON DAY 30, WAS THAT HE HAS A MAN AT THAT WALL HE HAS NEVER ASKED. THEY SPOKE TO EACH OTHER TWICE IN FIFTEEN DAYS AND A CLERK ENTERED BOTH.**

**THE PROTAGONIST'S NAME IS ON NO PAGE OF SIX HUNDRED AND EIGHTY CHAPTERS AND IT WAS NOT SAID IN A MOUTH IN ANY FORM IN CHAPTERS 566 TO 580. HE WAS IN A POSITION FOUR TIMES IN FIFTEEN DAYS AND DID NOT CROSS IT AND NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK PROMISES IT FOR A LATER ONE.**

## VOLUME 12 BATCH 0003, CHAPTERS 581 TO 590 — WHAT MOVED IN PEOPLE, AND WHAT DID NOT

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING IS THE ONE PERSON THIS BLOCK CHANGED, AND HE DID NOT CHANGE BY SAYING ANYTHING NEW. HE IS IN THAT YARD ON ALL TEN MORNINGS WITH HIS RIGHT HAND FLAT ON THE BOARDS OF THE SECOND TABLE AND NOT ON THE STONE AND NOT ON ANY SHEET, AND ON THE TWENTY-SECOND OF THE MONTH HE CAME DOWN OFF THE TOP OF THAT BANK SO THAT HE WAS BELOW THE LEVEL OF IT FOR THE FIRST TIME IN A MONTH AND STILL DID NOT SAY ANYTHING, AND ASKED FOR IT TO BE ENTERED THAT THE GROUND MADE NO DIFFERENCE. ON THE TWENTY-THIRD OF THE MONTH A WOMAN WHO KEEPS A SCALE SAID OUT LOUD, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, THAT HE HAD HAD A CHOICE ABOUT IT ON THE TWELFTH AND HAD TAKEN IT, AND THAT HE HAS HAD IT EVERY MORNING SINCE, AND HE DID NOT LOOK AT HER WHILE SHE SAID IT, AND HE SAID OUT LOAD THAT SHE IS NOT TO THANK HIM AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO STAND THERE AND BE A MAN WHO HAS BEEN PATIENT. ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF THE MONTH HE SAID OUT LOAD WHY HE HAS NOT ASKED, WHICH IS THAT A MAN WHO ASKS A MAN AND GETS A NO IS A MAN WHO ASKED AND NOT A MAN WHO MENDS FENCE, AND THAT HE WOULD LIKE THE YARD TO GO ON NOT ASKING HIM FOR THE REASON. ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF THE MONTH HE CROSSED THAT YARD AND STOOD AT THE END OF THAT WALL AND SAID A THING OUT LOUD ABOUT A NUMBER ON IT, AND HE ASKED FOR NOBODY TO MAKE ANYTHING OF IT. HE WAS WITHIN ABOUT NINE FEET OF THE MAN THE THIRD LINE IS ABOUT ON NINE OF THE TEN MORNINGS OF THIS BLOCK, AND ON THE TENTH, THE MORNING THE YARD REHEARSED THE ASKING NINE TIMES, THE MAN WAS NOT IN THAT YARD AT ALL WHILE IT WAS BEING DONE AND CAME UP THAT BANK AT ABOUT HALF PAST ONE AFTER IT WAS OVER. HE DID NOT ASK ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS AND NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS ARRANGED AROUND HIM ASKING SOON.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO DIGS LOAM IS THE OBJECT OF THE VOLUME AND HE IS NOT NAMED ON ANY PAGE OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND HE SAID NOTHING AT ALL ON ALL TEN OF THEM AND WAS ASKED NOTHING ON ALL TEN OF THEM. HE CAME UP THAT BANK ON ALL TEN MORNINGS OF THE BLOCK, AND HE IS NOW IN HIS OWN PARAGRAPH ON ALL TEN, THE TWO CHAPTERS THAT PREVIOUSLY SHOWED A MAN AT THE FAR END OF THAT TABLE WITHOUT NAMING HIM HAVING BEEN REPAIRED IN THE REVIEW PASS. ON THE NINETEENTH OF THE MONTH HE READ THE NOTICE OFF THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE THAT IT IS STANDING AGAINST, BECAUSE THAT IS THE ONLY END IT CAN BE READ FROM STANDING UP, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT SHE IS NOT ENTERING WHAT HE MADE OF IT. ON THE TWENTY-SECOND HE LOOKED UP THE BANK AT A MAN STANDING IN THE SUN AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT SHE IS NOT ENTERING THAT HE LOOKED UP THE BANK. ON THE TWENTY-THIRD HE READ THE FOURTH LINE OF THE FOURTH PAGE WITH HIS FOREFINGER AN INCH OFF IT. ON THE TWENTY-FOURTH HE STOOD AT THE FAR END OF THAT TABLE WITH SIX PAGES ON IT AND LOOKED DOWN THE TWO LONG SIDES OF IT. ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF THE MONTH, THE MORNING THE YARD REHEARSED THE ASKING NINE TIMES, HE WAS NOT IN THAT YARD AT ANY POINT WHILE THE ASKING WAS BEING SAID OUT LOUD IN IT AND WAS NOT IN IT WHEN THE LAST OF THE NINE HAD BEEN SAID, AND HE CAME UP THAT BANK AT ABOUT HALF PAST ONE, AFTER IT WAS OVER, AND LOOKED AT ABOUT NINE PEOPLE STANDING ALONG THAT SECOND TABLE AND SAID NOTHING AT ALL TO ANYBODY, AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD ASKED HIM ONE WORD, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A MAN WAS IN THAT YARD AND ENTERED THAT NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAID ONE WORD TO HIM. HE WAS NOT ASKED WHETHER THE NINE WORDS ARE TRUE. HE WAS NOT ASKED TO AGREE TO ANYTHING. HE AGREED TO NOTHING.**

**THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS HAD THE BLOCK OF HER LIFE ON THE LAST MORNING OF IT AND DID NOT LOOK UP. SHE COUNTED THE THIRD MONTH OF THIS YEAR OFF A BOARD WITH A FINGER ON THE WOOD IN THE OPEN IN DAYLIGHT WITH ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE AT THE WALL AND GOT THIRTY-ONE, AND ENTERED IT, AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT A MONTH THAT HAS ENDED CAN BE COUNTED AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO PRETEND SHE IS NOT PLEASED ABOUT IT. SHE THEN SAID OUT LOUD THAT SHE IS NOT ENTERING WHAT THE OUT FIGURE ON THAT WALL OUGHT TO SAY, AND GAVE THE REASON, AND WENT ACROSS THE YARD AND SAID THE FINDING TO THE MAN AT THE WALL HIMSELF. HER FLAT BOOK IS NOT HERS AND THE BLOCK GAVE HER TWO: THE COMPARISON OF SIX PAGES ON DAY 37, AND THE SIZE OF A YEAR ON DAY 40. SHE RULED NO COLUMN ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS AND APPOINTED NO PERSON TO THE COLUMN THAT WAS RULED AND EMPTY, AND ON THE ONE MORNING WHEN ABOUT NINE PEOPLE SAID A QUESTION OUT LOUD SHE SAID OUT LOUD THAT A PERSON WHO PRACTISES A QUESTION IS NOT A READER.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY IS THE MAN WHO SAID THE VOLUME, AND IN THIS BLOCK HE SAID NOTHING RESEMBLING IT, WHICH IS CORRECT. HE HAD THE FLAT BOOK OPEN ON TWO MORNINGS AND SHUT ON THREE, AND BOTH WERE ENTERED. ON THE NINETEENTH OF THE MONTH HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE IS THE ONE WHO KNOWS THE NAME AND IS NOT GOING TO PUT IT ON A BOARD, AND GAVE THE REASON. ON THE TWENTIETH HE SAID OUT LOUD, WITH HIS OWN HAND HOLDING HIS OWN BOOK DOWN AGAINST THE WIND, EXACTLY WHAT THIS DISTRICT WOULD HAVE TO PUT ON A PAGE, AND HE DID NOT TURN ROUND WHILE HE SAID IT. ON THE TWENTY-FIFTH HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE IS NOT ABLE TO ASK THAT MAN ANYTHING AND WOULD RATHER BE TOLD IN THE OPEN. ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT THE FOURTH OF THE FOUR FIGURES ON THAT WALL IS OUT BY A WHOLE YEAR, AND THREE HUNDRED AND SIXTY-FOUR DAYS, AND HE GAVE THE REASON IN HIS MOUTH, INCLUDING THAT NINE OF THE TWELVE MONTHS IN THAT SUM ARE OFF A LIST AT THE BACK OF HIS BOOK AND HAVE NEVER BEEN COUNTED BY ANYBODY. HE IS STILL THE MAN THE FOURTH OF THE FIVE IS ENTERED AGAINST AND HE ANSWERED IT AGAIN IN BLOCK 0002 AND HE HAS NOT BEEN ANSWERED SINCE.**

**THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN ASKED THE VOLUME'S QUESTION ONCE, ON THE TWELFTH OF THE MONTH, AND DID NOT ASK IT AGAIN IN THIS BLOCK. WHAT HE DID INSTEAD WAS COUNT. HE SAID ON THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF THE MONTH THAT SIX PAGES ARE ALIKE, THAT FOUR DIFFERENT HANDS IN THE FOURTH LINE ARE NOT FOUR PEOPLE, AND THAT HE HAS A FIGURE FOR IT. HE SAID ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF THE MONTH THAT HE HAS THE NUMBER FOR THREE MEN SAYING A THING AND NONE OF THE THREE HAS SAID THE REASON. HE ASKED THE QUESTION OF THE MAN WHO MENDS FENCING ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH AND HE DID NOT ASK IT OF ANYBODY ELSE IN THAT YARD. HE COUNTED FIFTY-SEVEN THINGS OUT LOUD IN TEN MORNINGS AND READ EVERY ONE OF THE FIFTY-SEVEN BACK TO HIMSELF IN A LOW VOICE.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE HAD HER SHOP OPEN ON ALL TEN MORNINGS WITH TWO PAGES ON HER OWN COUNTER, EXCEPT ON THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF THE MONTH WHEN SHE CARRIED THEM DOWN THAT BANK IN HER OWN TWO HANDS WITH A REASON SAID OUT LOUD FIRST AND TOOK THEM BACK UP AT ABOUT ONE, AND ON THE TWENTY-SECOND OF THE MONTH SHE SAID THAT ONE OF THE ABOUT NINE PEOPLE WHO COME INTO THAT SHOP HAD SAID THE REASON FOR HER OUT LOUD, WHICH IS THAT A WOMAN WHO CANNOT TELL SHOULD NOT BE HOLDING THEM AT ALL. ABOUT NINE PEOPLE IN THAT SHOP KNOW SHE HAS BEEN WRONG IN PUBLIC THREE TIMES AND RIGHT THREE TIMES, AND ON THE TWENTY-THIRD OF THE MONTH SHE SAID OUT LOUD THAT SHE HAS BEEN GIVING ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE NOTHING FOR A MONTH, AND ON THE TWENTY-THIRD SHE ALSO SAID THE ONE THING IN THIS BLOCK THAT IS SAID ABOUT A PERSON BY SOMEBODY ELSE IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, WHICH IS THAT THE MAN WHO MENDS FENCING HAS HAD A CHOICE ABOUT IT EVERY MORNING AND HAS TAKEN IT, AND THAT HE SHOULD NOT BE THANKED. ON THE LAST MORNING OF THE BLOCK SHE SAID THAT NEITHER A COUNTED MONTH NOR A COUNTED WALL HELPS HER AND THAT SHE IS GLAD ABOUT BOTH.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THAT CART SAID TWO MORE THINGS ABOUT HIMSELF AND NEITHER IS A NAME. ON THE EIGHTEENTH OF THE MONTH HE SAID HE WOULD RATHER BE ASKED THAN BE READY AND IS NOT GOING TO PRETEND THAT IS ABOUT HIS OWN WORK. ON THE TWENTY-SECOND HE SAID, WITH HIS BACK TO ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, THAT NOBODY IN THAT YARD CAN TELL HIM WHICH MAN THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE THINGS THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT HAVE IS FOR, AND THAT IT MIGHT NOT BE THE MAN AT THE END OF THE TABLE. HE HAS SAID THREE THINGS ABOUT HIMSELF IN TWO VOLUMES AND NONE OF THEM IS A NAME AND NONE OF THEM SETTLES ANYTHING.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-THREE WHO TAKES IN WASHING AT THE SECOND CHANNEL PASSED TWICE AND STOPPED TWICE, AND SHE WAS NOT ASKED ABOUT THE HOUSE ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS. ON THE NINETEENTH OF THE MONTH SHE READ A BOARD STANDING UP AND SAID THAT A BOARD WITH A QUESTION ON IT AND NOBODY IN IT IS A THING PEOPLE WALK PAST, AND THAT THE ONES WHO STOP AT IT ARE THE ONES LOOKING FOR SOMETHING TO COMPLAIN OF. ON THE TWENTY-FIFTH SHE LOOKED ALONG ABOUT NINE PEOPLE STANDING IN A LINE AND SAID THEY WERE STANDING LIKE PEOPLE AT A COUNTER WAITING TO BE SERVED, AND THAT THE THING THEY ARE WAITING FOR IS A THING NOBODY HAS GOT BEHIND THE COUNTER.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX READ THE FOUR OFF THAT WALL TWICE A DAY ON ALL TEN MORNINGS AND GOT ALL FOUR EVERY TIME AND WAS ASKED NOTHING ON ANY OF THE TEN. ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF THE MONTH A CLERK WENT ACROSS THAT YARD AND TOLD HIM, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, THAT THE FOURTH OF THOSE FOUR FIGURES IS OUT BY A WHOLE YEAR, AND HE HEARD EVERY WORD OF IT, AND HE SAID NOTHING AT ALL, AND HE DID NOT MOVE AND HE DID NOT LOOK AT THE WALL, AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD ASKED HIM ONE THING ABOUT IT THEN OR AFTERWARDS. THAT IS THREE HUNDRED AND FIFTEEN MORNINGS AND THE FIRST TIME ANYBODY HAS TOLD HIM ANYTHING AND THE ONLY FIGURE ON THAT WALL THAT HAS BEEN FOUND OUT TO BE OUT.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR IS GIVEN NOTHING AND ASKED NOTHING ON ALL TEN MORNINGS, ON HIS TWO HUNDRED AND TWENTIETH NIGHT OF THAT RUN THROUGH HIS TWO HUNDRED AND TWENTY-NINTH, HAVING SLEPT ON TWO HUNDRED AND NINETEEN OF THE FIRST THROUGH TWO HUNDRED AND TWENTY-EIGHT OF THE LAST, AND THE VOLUME MAY NOT SOFTEN THIS AND THIS BLOCK DID NOT.**

**THE ROAD KEEPER CAME UP THAT LANE ON ALL TEN MORNINGS, READ THE FOUR OFF THAT WALL AS HE WENT BY, WAS NOT ASKED ABOUT THE ELEVEN MILES ON ANY OF THE TEN, AND WAS NOT ASKED A SECOND REAL QUESTION. ON THE TWENTY-SECOND OF THE MONTH HE LOOKED AT A BOARD WITH A QUESTION ON IT AND DID NOT STOP.**

**A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY WHO IS NOT FROM THIS PARISH IS A PERSON STANDING IN A YARD AND NOT A RESIDENT AND WAS NOT ADDED. HE CAME UP THAT LANE BEFORE EIGHT ON THE TWENTY-FIRST OF THE MONTH WITH A PAGE IN THE INSIDE OF HIS COAT, SAID THE REASON OUT LOUD BEFORE HIS HAND WENT ON IT, PUT IT DOWN, AND WENT BACK DOWN THE BANK. HE CAME UP AGAIN AT ABOUT FOUR ON THE TWENTY-FOURTH, SAID THE REASON OUT LOUD FIRST, AND CARRIED IT BACK DOWN THAT LANE IN HIS COAT. A CLERK ENTERED HIM BOTH TIMES WITH HIS REASON. NOTHING WAS FOUND OUT EITHER TIME.**

**THE PROTAGONIST'S NAME IS ON NO PAGE OF SIX HUNDRED AND NINETY CHAPTERS AND WAS NOT SAID IN A MOUTH IN ANY FORM IN CHAPTERS 581 TO 590. HE WAS IN THAT YARD ON ALL TEN MORNINGS, AT THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE, WITH HIS RIGHT HAND FLAT ON THE BOARDS, AND ABOUT NINE FEET off the man the third line is about on nine of the ten mornings of the block, and on the tenth morning, the twenty-sixth of the month, the man was not in that yard at all while the asking was being rehearsed in it and came up that bank at about half past one after the rehearsal was over, and he did not ask. ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE IN THAT YARD NOW KNOW THAT HE HAD A CHOICE ABOUT IT AND TOOK IT, AND ONE OF THEM HAS SAID IT OUT LOUD, AND HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE SHOULD NOT BE THANKED FOR IT, AND THAT IS NOT A PROMISE TO ASK AND IS NOT ARRANGED FOR A LATER BLOCK. HE IS IN A POSITION ON THE LAST MORNING OF THIS BLOCK AND IS NOT IN A POSITION TO ASK, AND THE NEXT BLOCK IS THE ONE THAT ASKS.**

## VOLUME 12 BATCH 0004, CHAPTERS 591 TO 600 — WHAT MOVED IN PEOPLE OVER TEN DAYS, AND WHAT DID NOT

> **APPENDED BY THE VOLUME 12 BATCH 0004 PHASE, NOT REWRITTEN ABOVE. NO WORD ABOVE THIS BLOCK WAS CHANGED. WHERE A CHAPTER AND A DOCUMENT DISAGREE THE CHAPTER IS CANON.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING. THE PROTAGONIST, UNNAMED.** **HE ASKED HIS ONE QUESTION.** On the second of the fifth month at about ten, in the open, in front of about nineteen people, he asked a man of about thirty-one who digs loam whether a man who is not from this parish and has a page in his hand may come up that bank and ask him about it, and he said the words beforehand that he was going to say it in the plainest words he had and was not going to dress it up, and he looked at the boards the whole time, and the yard was quiet for about a minute and a half afterwards. He was not thanked and **he said out loud, five times on five mornings, that he is not to be thanked**, and a clerk entered it every time, and on the fourth of the month he said that what he did was stand about nine feet off a man while that man said yes, and that none of that cost the man a thing. His left hand was in the cloth it has been in since the eleventh of the June on all ten mornings. On the fifth of the month he said he was not going to organise the asking and that a man who has asked one question in a month does not get to organise the second one, and he went up that bank and carried a page to a shop and came back and put his hand on the wood as though he had not been anywhere. On the sixth he did not speak. On the seventh he said the fifth line is the only sentence this district has ever written that tells a person what to do with a piece of paper, and he is not going to be thanked for it, and he is at the end of that second table with his right hand flat on the boards and not on the stone and not on the book and not on any of the five lines. **HIS NAME IS ON NO PAGE OF SIX HUNDRED AND TEN CHAPTERS AND WAS NOT SAID IN A MOUTH IN ANY FORM IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO DIGS LOAM. THE OBJECT OF THE VOLUME, AND HE IS NOW FINDABLE.** He came up that bank on all ten mornings. On the twenty-ninth of the fourth month he put his own hand flat on the boards about a foot from the near edge of them. On the second of the fifth month he was asked a question and said ask me that again this afternoon, and he was not asked a second question that day. On the third of that month he came up at about half past eight, two hours earlier than in two months, stood at the far end of that table for about an hour and a half while about nine people did not use the hour, heard a woman say out loud what agreeing would cost, and said yes at about ten past one, out loud, in front of about nineteen people, and a clerk wrote it down without looking up. **HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE WAS NOT MAKING A SPEECH ABOUT IT AND THAT HE WAS NOT GOING TO PROMISE ANYBODY HE WILL LIKE IT.** On the fourth of that month he said out loud that he did not ask to be written down and is not taking it back, and that about four of you should know that he said yes to being asked and not to being written down, and the second of those two is what you did. On the fifth he looked up that bank at a shop for about two seconds. On the sixth he stood at the near end of that first table for the first time in about two months, said the fifth line is at his dictation and not at anybody's and that he is answering a question with a page instead of with his mouth, and a clerk wrote it in her own hand in the open at about a quarter past ten, and he said he had put his day on the page and where he is on it and had not put his name on it and that the fourth line had not been touched and nobody was going to touch it that day. **HE IS ON A PAGE IN THAT YARD AS A PLACE AND NOT AS A NAME, AND THERE IS NO WAY TO MAKE HIM UNFINDABLE, AND HE DID NOT ASK FOR IT.**

**A CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS.** **ON THE FOURTH OF THE FIFTH MONTH SHE ENTERED A PERSON'S WHEREABOUTS ON A PUBLIC PAGE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN SIX VOLUMES.** She said out loud, before she wrote, what she was not going to write, and gave the reason, which is that a page is a thing a stranger can be handed without anybody standing next to it, and that if his name is on it then a stranger can carry it nine hundred yards from that yard without ever meeting him, and she has watched that happen to about four people in two volumes and has never once been the one who wrote the name. She wrote it in her own hand, in the open, in front of about nineteen people, with about nine of them reading over her shoulder and a boy reading it out loud as she wrote it. She entered that she is not entering a name. **SHE HAS ENTERED NO NAME ON ANY DAY OF FIFTY AND APPOINTED NO PERSON TO THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD, AND ON THE SEVENTH OF THE FIFTH MONTH SHE SAID OUT LOUD THAT SHE HAS BEEN NAMED AS A READER MORE IN THIS VOLUME THAN IN ANY VOLUME BEFORE IT, THAT THE COLUMN IS EMPTY AGAIN, AND THAT THAT IS NOT A FAILURE OF THAT COLUMN.** She said on the sixth of that month that a line is not a column, that a column is something a person looks down and a line is something a person reads, that about four people had been waiting for her to rule one and she is not ruling one, and that no fifth line is a remedy because the fifth of the five things a document that sets a lot out has to say is a remedy and it is unpaid.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE.** **SHE PAID THE PRICE BEFORE HE PAID IT.** On the twenty-ninth of the fourth month she said out loud in that yard that the man the third line is about is the only person in that yard nobody has ever promised anything to. On the third of the fifth month she said out loud, before anybody asked him to pay it, that every copy of that book made from that morning carries the district's own words about where he is, that the number of copies is not a figure this district can hold, and that she was saying it before he agreed and not after and not entered afterwards and said to have been known all along. On the fifth of that month she was asked to hold a page in her own shop in front of customers, had her table out on the pavement before about seven which she does not do before the middle of the year, **SAID AGAIN THAT SHE CANNOT TELL WHICH OF THEM IS THIS DISTRICT'S, SAID HER SHOP WOULD NOT BE SHUT TO DO IT, AND SAID SHE IS NOT GOING TO BE THANKED**, and about nine people in that shop watched a page go onto her counter and none of them read a word of it. On the seventh of that month she said that about four people who come into her shop have stopped asking her which page is theirs and started asking her where a man is, and that she cannot tell them that either. **HER SHOP WAS OPEN ON ALL TEN MORNINGS.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO DIGS LOAM AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING ARE THE RELATIONSHIP THIS VOLUME HAS AND IT WAS SPOKEN TWICE.** The first time was at about ten on the second of the fifth month, when one of them asked the other a question in the plainest words and the other said ask me again this afternoon. **The second time was at about ten past one on the third of that month, when the man who digs loam said yes to being found and the man who mends fencing stood about nine feet away with his right hand hanging at his side and not on anything, and a clerk wrote the answer down without looking up, and afterwards the man who mends fencing said that he stood there and that none of that cost the man a thing.** Neither of them has said the other's name on any page of six hundred and ten chapters, and about four people at that first table have said since that those are the only two times in two months those two men put anything into the same air.

**EVERYBODY ELSE. TOUCHED ONLY BY BEING WHERE THEY ARE.** The man of about fifty-six read four figures off that wall twice a day on all ten mornings and was told once, at Chapter 590, that one of them is out by a whole year, and **was not asked twice and said nothing and was not corrected**. The man of about sixty-four was given nothing and asked nothing on all ten days and is on his hundred and thirty-ninth night of that run, having slept on a hundred and thirty-eight of them. The woman of about fifty-eight read three lines in that lot book standing up on her way past on all ten evenings and said nothing to anybody, and on the fifth of the month she stopped in the doorway of that shop for about a second and a half, looked at three pages on a counter and did not go in. The road keeper came up that lane on all ten mornings, read the four figures off that wall as he went by, **was not asked about the eleven miles on any of the ten, and was not asked a second real question.** The man of about twenty-nine who drives that cart came up that bank on all ten mornings, got it up about nine inches off the ground with one arm on all ten, said nothing in that yard on any of the ten, and on the fifth of the month had a page in his coat and took it out and put it back and did not put it down. The woman of about thirty-three who takes in washing at the second channel came up that bank on two mornings, on the first of the fifth month, and **was not asked about the house on either of them**. The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally stood at the east end of that yard on most of the ten mornings with his book shut under his arm, said on the twenty-ninth of the fourth month that wanting nineteen people to know at once is not the same as not being able to, and did not open his book in that yard on any of the ten. The man of about thirty-two who puts tables up was in that yard on most of the ten mornings with his straight edge and laid it across that second table on two of them. The man of about thirty-seven who cuts reeds put a chalk mark on the underside of those boards on the thirtieth of the fourth month, said out loud that it is not for anything, and put none on the other nine. **NOBODY WAS ADDED TO THIS DISTRICT. A PERSON STANDING IN A YARD IS NOT A RESIDENT.**

---

> **Appended by the Volume 13 outline and Batch 0001 phase, which wrote no close and named no person and added no person. AGES UNALTERED, HANDS UNALTERED, POSTS UNALTERED. EVERY SCOPE BELOW IS COUNTED, NOT ASSERTED: each row's figure is the number of the fifteen chapters on which the descriptor occurs, produced by running the string over the fifteen files after their last prose edit. The descriptive labels are the prose's own and the names in this file are for the writer and are not in the prose. THE PROTAGONIST IS NOT NAMED ON A PAGE IN ANY CHAPTER WRITTEN SO FAR AND MUST NOT BE NAMED IN ANY CHAPTER TO COME.**

**WAS ANY PERSON GIVEN ANYTHING? NO, AND THE ANSWER HAS A NEW FIGURE ON IT THIS BLOCK. `was given nothing`, `was handed` and `was offered` are at zero across the fifteen files. `was given nothing` is on fifteen of fifteen and every one of the fifteen is about the man of about sixty-four. `gave him` is on zero. `offered him` is on zero and the offering in this block is refused in a shop rather than offered at the wall. `thanked` is on fifteen of fifteen and every one of the fifteen is a person saying they are not going to be thanked, or a clerk entering that nobody thanked them.**

HE READ THEM FOR THE THREE HUNDRED AND FORTIETH TIME ON THE LAST MORNING OF THE BLOCK AND GOT ALL FOUR.** HIS FIGURES ARE NOT A FIGURE ABOUT SITTINGS AND NOBODY HAS EVER ASKED HIM WHAT ONE OF THEM IS FOR |

SAID OUT LOUD ON THE EIGHTEENTH THAT SHE HAS TOLD NINE PEOPLE WHERE THAT MAN IS THIS FORTNIGHT AND HAS BEEN WRONG ABOUT IT EVERY SINGLE TIME BECAUSE SHE HAS NOT SEEN HIM, AND THAT SHE IS GOING TO KEEP SAYING IT AND IS NOT GOING TO BE RIGHT ABOUT IT AND IS NOT GOING TO BE THANKED FOR THAT EITHER.** HER SHOP WAS OPEN ON EVERY MORNING OF THE FIFTEEN |

SAID OUT LOUD ON THE FOURTH MORNING THAT HE STOOD AT A GATE AT THE NINTH MILE AT HALF PAST SIX ON FOUR MORNINGS AND A MAN STOOD AT THAT GATE AND HE DID NOT ASK HIM ONE WORD. SAID OUT LOUD ON THE SEVENTEENTH MORNING THAT HE WENT BACK TO THAT GATE AND A MAN HE HAS NOT SPOKEN TO WAS AT IT AND THAT HE DID NOT ASK HIM ONE WORD AND THAT HE WANTS IT ENTERED THAT HE WANTED TO** |

HE ASKED THE VOLUME 12 QUESTION ONCE, ON THE TWELFTH OF THE FOURTH MONTH, AND DID NOT ASK IT AGAIN AND DID NOT ASK THE VOLUME 13 QUESTION, WHICH IS RESERVED FOR DAY 25.** HE WAS TURNED ROUND BY A STRANGER COMING DOWN THAT LANE ON THE THIRTEENTH AND DID NOT TURN FOR ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AND THEN DID |

SAID OUT LOUD ON THE FIFTEENTH THAT HE HAS CHANGED HIS MIND ABOUT SAYING THAT OUT LOUD AGAIN.** HE STILL HAS A FIGURE IN THAT BOOK THAT HAS BEEN ON A LADDER FOR TWO VOLUMES AND THAT NOBODY IN THAT PARISH HAS EVER ASKED HIM FOR |

NOBODY OFFERED HIM ANYTHING AND NOBODY ASKED HIM ANYTHING AND THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT OFFERING SAYS NOTHING WAS ASKED AND NOTHING WAS GIVEN ON ALL FIFTEEN.** HE WAS NOT OFFERED THE SECOND CHAIR AND HE WAS NOT ENTERED IN ANY COUNT OF ANYTHING IN THIS BLOCK |

**1. NO CHARACTER MOVED, SAID ANYTHING NEW, LEARNED ANYTHING, OR CEASED TO BE ASKED ANYTHING.** All eighty-six speeches are byte-identical to their state before this pass. The reversal at Chapter 625, the question, the eleven seconds and the answer, are untouched. The sentence the volume owed, paid at Chapter 626 over a table while a scraper went along the boards, is untouched. **THE ONE FACT IN A MOUTH THAT A DRAFT OF THIS REPAIR CHANGED WAS A BYSTANDER COUNT, FROM NINE TO FOUR, ON CHAPTER 626, AND IT WAS PUT BACK: EVERY LINK OF EVERY DE-RECURSED CHAIN IS NOW DERIVED FROM ITS OWN FRAME, SO NO MOUTH ATTRIBUTES A STATEMENT TO A DIFFERENT NUMBER OF PEOPLE THAN THE MANUSCRIPT DOES.**

**2. THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS.** Unchanged in every particular: her morning entry at about a quarter to eight, her four-o'clock entry, her figure of people who have come down that lane, the column she keeps empty on purpose, the figure of the sheet at that gatepost at four hundred and eleven, and the fact that she has not been asked why. **WHAT A READER NOW SEES DIFFERENTLY IS HER RHYTHM. **7. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT, THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO WHO PUTS TABLES UP, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FORTY-THREE, AND THE STRANGERS.** All unchanged in what they do and say. **THE ONLY FIGURE WHO APPEARS ON NO PAGE OF SIX HUNDRED AND THIRTY CHAPTERS IS STILL NOT NAMED, AND NO PART OF THIS PASS NAMED HIM, PREPARED HIM, OR PROMISED HIM FOR A BLOCK.**

**1. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING. THE PROTAGONIST. UNNAMED, AND HE SAYS FOUR THINGS OUT LOAD IN TEN MORNINGS AND IS ASKED ONE QUESTION ON ONE OF THEM.** He is in that yard on all ten mornings, with his right hand flat on the boards at the near end of that second table, and he asks nobody anything on any of them. **On Chapter 631 he says he has noticed that a line went on a book and that he has said nothing about it for five weeks, and that he has been mending fence. On Chapter 634 he says that he put that line on that book once because a man asked him to, and that the whole difference between him and a clerk is that she waited to be asked to enter a figure and he did not wait to be asked to put a line down. On Chapter 636 he says he is not going to agree with a man about what he minds, because agreeing with a man about what he minds is a thing he has no standing to do. On Chapter 639 he says that the reason he counts that wall every morning is that a man who is counting is not standing about, and that he has been standing about, and that he did not know that until that morning. On Chapter 640 he says what he has done with his own hands this month and that he has not been paid for one item of it.** On Chapter 635 he puts two staples in the loose near leg of that first table with his own hammer, **after saying the reason out loud and before doing it, and the reason is that a leg is a leg and that it is the only thing in that yard nobody is being paid for and that is going to come loose, and that he is not doing it for anybody.** Nobody thanks him and nobody asks him why. On Chapter 637 he is in that yard when a clerk asks a woman a question and is not asked anything himself. **The sentence `outline/volume-13.md` section 3 allocates to him is UNPAID AND IS NOT PAID IN ANY OF THESE FOUR THINGS.** He is asked one question in the block, by a clerk, about a count, and it is not about him.

**2. THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS. THE INSTRUMENT. SHE DID THREE THINGS IN TEN MORNINGS THAT SHE DID NOT HAVE TO DO, AND ENTERED ALL THREE, AND ENTERED THAT SHE WAS NOT ENTERED TO ENTER ANYTHING.** On Chapter 631 she says out loud, two days before she does it, that she is going to count a month off a board in front of everybody, and gives the reason, and a bystander notes that no person in that yard has ever said a thing out loud before the hand went. On Chapter 639 she changes the name of a count out loud and enters that she changed the name and not the figure. **1. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDES FENCING, THE PROTAGONIST, UNNAMED, IN THAT YARD ON ALL TEN MORNINGS OF THIS BLOCK.** He says the reason out loud before he gets down on that bank on Chapter 646 and levers half an inch of footing out from under the low leg of that second table with a bar and a wedge of his own, and no clerk asks him why. HIS NAME IS NOT ON A PAGE IN THIS BLOCK AND NO PART OF THIS BLOCK NAMED HIM, PREPARED HIM, OR PROMISED HIM FOR ANYTHING.**

**2. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO DIGS LOAM, AND THE STATE OF HIM AT THE END OF THIS VOLUME.** Said nothing on the first morning of the block. Said one thing about a ditch on the second. Said on the third that he is not going to say a thing out loud after another man has said it out loud. Said on the fifth that he has read four of the five lines on that book about four hundred times and has not read the fifth this month once, because it is his and he knows what it says. Said on the sixth that a figure is not a way of paying anybody. Said on the seventh that a man being asked nothing and a woman not answering a question are not the same thing. Said on the eighth that he had not gone over to a stranger standing four feet off that table. **SAID NO ON THE NINTH, OUT LOUD, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, TO A STRANGER WHO CAME DOWN THAT LANE ON THE WORDS OF A LINE THIS DISTRICT WROTE, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, TO A THING HE WAS NEVER ASKED ABOUT AND WAS NEVER TOLD HE WOULD BE ASKED ABOUT, AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE WAS NOT TAKING IT BACK. HE WAS NOT ASKED ONE THING BEFORE THAT MORNING IN FIFTY MORNINGS, AND NO MOUTH OF HIS FORESHADOWED THE ASKING.** On the fiftieth morning he is at the far end of that second table with his spade against the leg of it and his own two hands on the rail, and he said out loud that he is still paid nothing and that he is not going to make it into more than that. **He is still findable in the morning. He is still not paid. The fifth line is untouched.**

**3. A CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS, WHO ENTERED A NO.** She entered every count before anything else on all ten mornings. She said out loud on the forty-first that she would enter the figure twice, a figure in the morning and a fact in the afternoon. She said the word remedy out loud on the forty-fifth and entered that she wanted to find out whether the yard would move. She said out loud on the forty-seventh, before the afternoon, that she was not going to ask a woman one thing a second time, and entered the reason. **She entered a no on her own page on the forty-ninth for the first time in nineteen years, and entered the reason, which is that a person said no in that yard and a page is where a person says a thing when it is going to be said twice, and said out loud that she is not going to strike it out tomorrow and entered that now so that it is in the book before she does it. SHE SAID OUT LOUD IN THE SAME BREATH THAT SHE HAS BEEN RIGHT ABOUT A GREAT MANY THINGS THIS MONTH AND THAT A CLERK WHO IS NEVER WRONG IS NOT A CLERK, AND THIS BLOCK'S ONLY ADMISSION THAT SHE IS NOT RIGHT EVERY MORNING IS IN HER OWN MOUTH AND NOT IN A LEDGER. SHE NAMED THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD ON THE LAST MORNING AND ENTERED NOBODY IN IT.**

**4. THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX.** Read four figures off that wall twice a morning on all ten mornings of this block, got all four every time, on his three hundred and sixty-sixth morning through his three hundred and seventy-fifth. **HE WAS NOT ASKED ONE THING ABOUT ANY OF THEM ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS AND WAS ASKED NOTHING AT ALL IN THIS BLOCK.** Two of those four are out, one of them by a whole year, and neither was corrected and the size of neither was said out loud in a yard.

**5. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY.** Said one thing out loud in this block, on the forty-first, and it is not a name: that he has a figure for every month in a book under his arm and has never shown one of them to anybody, and that on the ninth of this month, when a clerk counted a month off a board in the open with about nineteen people at the wall, he stood about nine feet off her with the book shut and did not open it, and has not opened it since. He is the second person in that yard this month to say out loud that he is not going to do a thing.

**6. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE.** **HER SHOP WAS OPEN ON ALL TEN DAYS OF THIS BLOCK AND THAT IS A FACT AND NOT A SCENE, AND SHE WAS NOT ASKED ONE THING ON ANY OF THEM, AND SHE APPEARS IN NO MOUTH OF ANY OF THEM, AND THE COUNT OF THAT FIGURE STANDS WHERE CHAPTER 620 PUT IT. A CHAPTER THAT ASKS HER A SEVENTH TIME IS A CHAPTER THAT HAS RUN OUT OF ITS OWN ROOM.**

**7. THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN.** Said one thing out loud in this block, on the forty-first, and it is about what he cannot count: that he has counted the people who came down that lane three ways this month and has not got the same number out of any two of them, and nobody in that yard counts that lane but the clerk, and he has not asked her. **HE ASKED NO QUESTION ON ANY MORNING OF THIS BLOCK.** He counted thirty-four things said out loud across the ten mornings and read each number back to himself in a low voice, and the count of the figures he read back is the count of the things that were said.

**8. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO WHO PUTS TABLES UP.** Was asked one thing out loud on the forty-second and answered it about himself and not about a page: that he set both those tables up himself, that he has never been able to make the two of them level since, and that nobody asked him to and he is not going to ask anybody to help him. He also said a thing out loud on the forty-third and on the forty-fifth. None of it is a name.

**9. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS.** **Said one thing out loud in this block, on the forty-ninth, standing about eight feet off the near end of that second table, and it is not a name: that a man who says no and says he is not taking it back has made a thing this district has no word for, and that he has been cutting reeds on that water since he was about eleven years old and has a word for a good many things and has not got one for that.** He did not ask the reversal question again and he did not foreshadow it.

**10. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT.** Stood in that yard for about twenty-five minutes on the forty-seventh, with an empty pail in her own hand, which is the first time anybody at that low wall can remember, and read three lines in that lot book standing up and **said nothing at all to anybody, on all ten days.** A clerk did not ask her one thing and entered the reason for not asking, which is that a clerk asked her a question earlier in this month and she said nothing, and a person who does not answer a question the first time is not asked it a second time. She did not come up past those tables at about ten to six that evening. **She is not a resident, she is not in the column, and nobody in that yard has ever asked her what the other two lines say.**

**11. THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR. AT THE FOOT OF THAT LOW WALL ON ALL TEN MORNINGS, ON HIS TWO HUNDRED AND EIGHTIETH NIGHT OF THAT RUN THROUGH HIS TWO HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-NINTH, HAVING SLEPT ON TWO HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-NINE OF THE FIRST THROUGH TWO HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-EIGHT OF THE LAST, AND GIVEN NOTHING AND ASKED NOTHING ON ALL TEN DAYS. HE IS NOT GIVEN A CHAIR, HE IS NOT SENT ON MORE NIGHTS, HE IS NOT ENTERED IN ANY COUNT OF ANYTHING, AND A CLERK SAID OUT LOUD ON THE FORTY-NINTH THAT A CLERK IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THAT YARD WHO CAN MAKE A NO PERMANENT, AND A MAN ON A STONE IS NOT THAT.**

**12. THE ROAD KEEPER.** Came up that lane on all ten mornings, read the four figures off that wall as he went by, **and was not asked about the eleven miles on any of them. THE RELAY IS WHOLE ON ALL FIFTY MORNINGS OF THIS VOLUME AFTER ONE BREAK, IN BLOCK 0002, ON CHAPTER 627, AND A SECOND BREAK IS NOT AVAILABLE.** About four people in that yard said out loud on three of the ten mornings that a man who walks that road twice a day came up a lane with a man in it saying no and did not stop at the top of the bank. About a mile and a half of that road has gone since the frost and that is on no page in that yard.

**13. THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THAT CART.** Got the near wheel up about nine inches off the ground with one arm on all ten mornings and **said no number out loud to nobody in particular on any of them.**

**14. A BODY FOUR HUNDRED MILES OFF.** No face on any of the ten mornings, nobody in that yard watched it, no arrival calendared, and the distance it is past a printing it did not make moved from two hundred and fifty-eight days to two hundred and sixty-seven.

**15. THE THREE STRANGERS OF THIS BLOCK, AND NONE OF THEM IS A RESIDENT AND NONE IS IN THE COLUMN.** **A man of about forty-one on the forty-fourth**, who came down that lane with a folded page in his own right hand and said out loud that he had not come down it to find out whether the page came out of this yard and had not come down it to hand it to anybody, and had come down it because it says there is a man at the end of it. **A man of about thirty-three on the forty-eighth and the forty-ninth**, the same person twice, who came down that lane on the words of the fifth line, stood in that yard from about ten until the sun came off it, was asked nothing by anybody, said two things out loud, put his page down on the end of that second table for about four minutes and took it off it again after giving the reason in his own mouth, came down the same lane again the next morning, asked the man who digs loam What do you want at about eleven, and was told no, and then asked the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing what he thought about it. **A clerk entered him neither by name nor in the column, and a clerk entered that the man who stood in that yard all day on the forty-eighth and the man who came down that lane again on the forty-ninth are one person and are not counted twice.**

**16. AND THE ONE FIGURE WHO APPEARS ON NO PAGE OF SIX HUNDRED AND FIFTY CHAPTERS IS STILL NOT NAMED, AND NO PART OF THIS BLOCK NAMED HIM, PREPARED HIM, PROMISED HIM FOR A BLOCK, OR LET A CHARACTER PREPARE FOR HIM, AND `outline/volume-13.md` SECTION 7 IS THE WHOLE OF THAT RULE.** ON THE MORNING OF THE NO, A MAN SAID OUT LOUD A THING ABOUT HIMSELF THAT HE DID NOT ALREADY KNOW AND THE YARD HEARD IT, AND HE DID NOT SAY THE NAME, AND NOBODY ELSE DID, AND THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD IS STILL EMPTY AND IS EMPTIER THAN IT WAS ON THE FIRST MORNING OF THIS VOLUME.
