## VOLUME 13, BLOCK 0004, THE REVIEW FIX OF 2026-09-29 — WHAT MOVED IN THE PEOPLE AND WHAT DID NOT

**1. THE PROTAGONIST. STILL UNNAMED, AND THE PASS THAT COULD HAVE NAMED HIM DID NOT.** He is on no page of Chapters 601 to 650 and his name is on no page of six hundred and fifty chapters. **THIS CHANGED NO CHARACTER FILE IN ANY OTHER RESPECT: no promise of the name was made, dated, prepared or implied, no character was arranged to say it, and no arrival was set up.** `outline/volume-13.md` section 5.3 forbids settling it in the final image and section 14 lists it as a liability for Volume 14.

**2. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO DIGS LOAM, WHO IS THE FIGURE THE VOLUME RESOLVES ON, AND WHAT BLOCK 0004 DID TO HIM.** A stranger came down that lane on the words of the fifth line, stood in that yard a whole day and was asked nothing, came down it again in the morning, and at about eleven asked him a thing that was not about a page, in the ordinary voice, in front of about nineteen people. **HE SAID NO. AND THEN HE SAID THAT THE LINE NAMES A PLACE AND NAMES HIM AND THAT IS ALL IT DOES, THAT NOBODY IN THAT YARD HAD ASKED HIM ONE QUESTION IN THE WHOLE OF ITS RUN, THAT THE STRANGER HAD NOT ASKED HIM ONE EITHER IN A WHOLE DAY, AND THAT HE IS NOT TAKING IT BACK, AND THAT HE IS GOING TO GO ON STANDING AT THAT END OF THAT TABLE IN THE MORNING AND SAYING THINGS OUT LOUD NOW AND THEN, AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO BE A PERSON THAT A LINE IN A BOOK POINTS AT.** On the fiftieth morning he is at the far end of that second table with his spade leaning against the leg of it and his own two hands on the rail. **HE IS STILL PAID NOTHING AND HE IS STILL FINDABLE IN THE MORNING.**

**3. THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS, AND THE FIRST REFUSAL IN NINETEEN YEARS.** She entered a no on her own page on the twenty-fifth of the sixth month. **She also said out loud that she does not know which mornings she was wrong, and that is the first time in nineteen years that anybody in that yard has told her what to enter and also the first time she has entered a refusal.** She still reads the four figures off that wall twice a morning and gets all four.

**4. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING.** Said out loud what that yard is for on the nineteenth of the sixth month, took up half an inch of the footing of that low leg with a wedge of his own on the twenty-second having said the reason before he touched it, and has still not been paid for one item of anything and has still never said thank you.

**5. THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR.** At the foot of that low wall on his two hundred and eighty-ninth night, having slept on two hundred and eighty-eight of them, given nothing on every morning of the fifty, and asked nothing about what he is to be used for.

**6. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT.** Reads three lines standing up on her own errand and has never been asked one thing, and a clerk decided out loud on the twenty-third of the sixth month that she was not going to ask her a second time.

**7. THE REVIEW REPAIR TOUCHED NO CHARACTER.** The twelve substitutions it made to Chapters 643, 645, 646, 647, 648 and 650 were all of one stale tenure figure in bystanders' mouths, describing how long a table had stood in a yard. **NO PERSON'S ACTION, SPEECH, ENTRY, PAYMENT, REFUSAL OR RELATIONSHIP CHANGED, AND NO CHARACTER FILE ABOVE THIS SECTION IS WITHDRAWN OR QUALIFIED BY IT.** Every speech in which a substitution was made keeps its exact word count, its counted figure and its stamped duration.

# VOLUME 13 CLOSE — CHARACTER STATE, APPENDED 2026-09-29

> **Appended by the Volume 13 close phase. It wrote no chapter and edited no chapter. Every figure below was measured off the fifty chapter files. A cast is a thing a close reports and does not rescore.**

## THE PROTAGONIST. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING. UNNAMED.

**In the yard of Lot Seventeen on all fifty mornings. In the fifty chapters of this volume his name is on no page, he is not asked to state it, he does not offer it, and nobody in that yard says it.** He put the fifth line on that book at his own dictation and has said out loud that he would put it there again. On day 43 he says out loud that he is not going to pretend he does not know what his own yard is for, and a man at the far end of the same table says out loud that he is not going to say it after him. On day 46 he says the reason out loud before he gets down on that bank and levers half an inch of footing out from under the low leg of the second table with a bar and a wedge of his own, and nobody asks him why. On day 49 a stranger asks him what he thinks, and he answers out loud about himself: that he put that line on the book, that he asked the question in that yard on the second of the fifth month, and did not ask whether a stranger might come down that lane with something else, that he knew the answer to that at the time and said it out loud anyway, and that he is the only person in that yard who can be asked that this morning. He says out loud that he is not to be thanked for that morning and that nobody should thank him for that morning and he would like that entered before anything else anybody says about it. **He is not thanked. He is never thanked in any chapter of this volume.**

## THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO DIGS LOAM. THE ANSWER TO A QUESTION NOBODY ASKED.

**On all fifty mornings, at the far end of the second table, with his spade against the leg of it and his hands on the rail. Says one thing out loud on most mornings and nothing at all on some. Says NO on the twenty-fifth of the sixth month, in the ordinary voice, in front of about nineteen people, to a stranger who came down that lane on the words of a line he wrote, and says out loud that he is not taking it back, and says out loud that he is going to go on standing at that end of that table in the morning and saying things out loud now and then and that he is not going to be a person that a line in a book points at. On the twenty-sixth he says one thing: that he is still paid nothing and that he is not going to make it into more than that.**

**He is not asked one question before day 49. No mouth of his foreshadows the asking. He agreed to be asked once, in that yard, in front of about nineteen people, and he said out loud on the morning after that he did not ask to be written down and is not taking it back. He is still findable in the morning and he is still not paid, and the fifth of the five things this district does not have — a way to pay a person who is not in a household — is unpaid on all fifty mornings, and his saying no is not a payment.**

## A CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS

**Enters every count before anything else on all fifty mornings. Counts the fourth month of this year off a board at thirty on day 5 and the fifth month at thirty-one on day 33, both in about eleven and about nine minutes, both in the open, in daylight, with a finger on the wood and about nineteen people at the wall, and neither corrects a date on any page. Says out loud that a count of zero is not a count of nobody. Says out loud that she will enter the same figure again that afternoon whether it moves or not, on the ground that a figure is what I write in the morning and a fact is what I write in the afternoon. Says the word remedy out loud in that yard and the yard does not move. Says out loud why she is not going to ask a woman one thing a second time, before she did not ask it the first time. SAYS OUT LOUD ON DAY 49 THAT SHE HAS BEEN RIGHT ABOUT A GREAT MANY THINGS THIS MONTH AND THAT A CLERK WHO IS NEVER WRONG IS NOT A CLERK, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO ONCE OUT LOUD, IN HER OWN MOUTH, ON THE MORNING OF THE NO.**

**ON DAY 49 SHE ENTERS A NO ON HER OWN PAGE AND ENTERS THE REASON, WHICH IS THAT A PERSON SAID NO IN THAT YARD AND A PAGE IS WHERE A PERSON SAYS A THING WHEN IT IS GOING TO BE SAID TWICE. IT IS THE FIRST REFUSAL SHE HAS ENTERED IN NINETEEN YEARS. SHE SAYS OUT LOUD THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO STRIKE IT OUT IN THE MORNING AND ENTERS THAT NOW, SO THAT IT IS IN THE BOOK BEFORE SHE DOES IT.**

**She is not right every morning and the volume says so once out loud, in her own mouth.**

## THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX

**Reads four figures off that wall twice a morning, in the ordinary voice, without looking at the wall, on all fifty mornings, and got all four of them both times, on his three hundred and twenty-sixth morning through his three hundred and seventy-fifth. He is not asked about any of the four on any of the fifty days and is asked nothing at all. The third of his four is out by a day and the fourth is out by a whole year and neither was corrected and the size of the second was said out loud in that yard twice, on Chapter 608 and on Chapter 609, and by nobody else in fifty chapters. No gate in this repository can see that figure, because every gate reads ladders and this is a mouth.**

## THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN

**Counts what is said out loud in that yard and reads the number back to himself in a low voice, 233 times across fifty chapters, and the figure he reads back equals the word count of the speech above it in all 233 cases, and equals the ledger's own count of things said out loud in all 233 cases. He counted the fourth month of this year off the end of that board with his own thumbnail after a clerk had taken her finger off the wood, and got the same number twice, and said out loud that he was not counting that board, he was counting the people counting it, and that that is the one thing in that yard he has never once tried to do, and that he has found out this morning that he cannot. He asks no question on any morning of this volume.**

## THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO WHO PUTS TABLES UP. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY

**Two men, one morning, the twenty-sixth of the sixth month, about forty minutes at the far end of the second table with a scraper and a cross rail, saying two things to each other in the ordinary voice that nobody in that yard wrote down. The man who puts tables up: a page this yard wrote is the only thing in this parish that can send a man who has never set foot in it up a bank he has never climbed, and it does that by not saying what it is for, and every way of saying what it is for would have cost them the sending. The man who keeps a tally: he has kept a tally for nineteen years and nothing he has ever written down has sent anybody anywhere either, and on the sixth of the fifth month somebody wrote four lines on a page in a clerk's hand and a stranger walked two days and stood in that yard with it, and a clerk entered all three of those and entered neither of these two. Then neither of them said anything else about it and the scraping went on until about half past eleven. The closing ledger of the same chapter refuses it as a payment, on the separate ground that the fifth of the five things this district does not have is a way to pay a person who is not in a household and two men saying two things to each other over a table is not one.**

## THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS

**Says one thing out loud on day 49, and it is the line that a man at the far end of that table has said no out loud and then said out loud that he is not taking it back, and that those two sentences have never been in the same morning before in that yard, and that a man who says both of them on one morning has made a thing this district has no word for, and that he has been cutting reeds on that water since he was about eleven years old and has got a word for a good many things and has not got one for that. He is the man who asks the reversal question on day 25, once, in the ordinary voice, in front of about nineteen people, and nobody answers it for eleven seconds.**

## THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THAT CART

**Comes up that lane at about three on all fifty mornings with the near wheel still dragging, gets it up about nine inches off the ground with one arm, and goes on up the bank. He says no number out loud to nobody in particular on any morning of this volume. His sentence is the most-repeated sentence in the volume and the duplication sweep at `state/volume-13-close.md` section 5.6 finds it whole and identical in four chapters beyond the two that straddle a boundary.**

## THE ROAD KEEPER

**Comes up that lane twice a day on all fifty mornings and reads the four figures off that wall as he goes by. He is asked about the eleven miles on forty-nine of the fifty mornings. On the twenty-seventh of the sixth month, Chapter 627, he is asked `How far is the eleven miles.` and he answers it: `It is eleven miles and it has been eleven miles all my life and about a mile and a half of it has gone since the frost, and I have told that council twice about the mile and a half and been told twice, and that is my answer and I have not got another one`, and he adds that he is telling them what he came up that lane to say and is not asking anybody for anything. THE CLOSING LEDGER OF THAT CHAPTER SAYS THAT A ROAD KEEPER WHO HAS WALKED ELEVEN MILES TWICE A DAY FOR NINETEEN YEARS AND TOLD A COUNCIL TWICE AND BEEN TOLD TWICE IS NOT A PAYMENT AND DID NOT PAY THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE. That is the one break in a protected relay in fifty chapters, and it is the break `outline/volume-13.md` section 10 declared in advance.**

## THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR

**At the foot of that low wall on all fifty mornings, with his coat folded on the stones beside him and nothing in his hands, on his two hundred and fortieth night of that run through his two hundred and eighty-ninth, having slept on two hundred and thirty-ninth of the first through two hundred and eighty-eighth of the last. Nobody offers him anything and nobody asks him anything on any of the fifty days. He is not given a chair. He is not sent on another nine days. He is not given the second chair. He is not entered in any count of anything in this volume. The relay `the record about the not offering says nothing was asked and nothing was given` is on one paragraph in each of the fifty files and the volume did not soften it.**

## THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE

**HER SHOP IS OPEN ON ALL FIFTY MORNINGS AND THAT IS A FACT AND NOT A SCENE. SHE IS PUT IN NO MOUTH ON ANY MORNING OF THIS VOLUME. SHE IS NOT ASKED ONE THING ON ANY OF THE FIFTY DAYS. SHE SAID ON CHAPTER 620 THAT SHE CANNOT TELL, FOR THE SIXTH TIME IN THIS DISTRICT'S HISTORY, AND THE COUNT OF THAT FIGURE STANDS WHERE CHAPTER 620 PUT IT AND DID NOT MOVE. ON CHAPTER 650 SHE IS NAMED ONCE, IN A LEDGER SENTENCE, IN THE CLAUSE THAT SAYS NOBODY IN THAT YARD HAS ASKED HER ONE THING THIS MONTH. A SHOP COUNTER IS A PLACE WHERE THOSE THREE LINES CAN BE READ AND IT IS NOT ONE OF THE FOUR AND IS NOT ADDED TO THEM.**

## THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT

**Reads three lines in that lot book standing up on her way past in the evening on all fifty mornings, and says nothing to anybody. On day 47 she stands at that table for about twenty-five minutes, which is the first time anybody at that wall can remember, and says nothing to anybody, and a clerk does not ask her one thing and enters the reason for not asking before she does not ask it. She is not a resident and is not added to this district.**

## A BODY FOUR HUNDRED MILES OFF

**No face. Nobody watches it. No arrival of anything is calendared anywhere in the fifty chapters. The figure for how far past a printing it is rises by one a morning from two hundred and eighteen to two hundred and sixty-seven and nothing ever comes.**

## THE STRANGERS, NINE OF THEM BY NINE MORNINGS, NONE OF THEM A RESIDENT

**Nine mornings of this volume carry a person who came down that lane with a page in their hand. A man of about forty-one on the twentieth of the fifth month, who said one thing out loud in that yard before anybody asked him anything. A man of about thirty-three on the twenty-fourth and twenty-fifth of the sixth month, who is the same person twice and stood in that yard a whole day and was asked nothing and came down it again in the morning. A person of about twenty-nine on the second of the sixth month, who put a page down face down on that table for about a minute and took it up again. And others, unnamed, on the other six mornings. NONE OF THEM IS A RESIDENT. NONE OF THEM IS ENTERED IN THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD. AND A CLERK ENTERED ON DAY 44 THAT A PAGE A PERSON CARRIED DOWN A LANE IS NOT A FIFTH OF THE FOUR DOCUMENTS THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT OWN, BECAUSE A PERSON BROUGHT IT AND THERE WAS A PERSON AND A REASON IN THE ROOM.**

## THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD, AND ITS STATE

**RULED AND EMPTY AT ABOUT SIX ON ALL FIFTY MORNINGS. NAMED FIFTY-NINE TIMES ACROSS THE FIFTY FILES. NEVER FILLED. NO PERSON APPOINTED TO IT. NO STRANGER ENTERED IN IT. NEITHER THE MAN WHO SAID NO NOR THE MAN WHO ANSWERED A STRANGER ABOUT HIMSELF NOR THE MAN WHO SAID WHAT HIS OWN YARD IS FOR IS IN IT. IT IS EMPTIER AT THE FIFTIETH MORNING THAN IT WAS AT THE FIRST, BECAUSE A CLERK WHO HAS JUST ENTERED A REFUSAL ON IT NAMED IT AGAIN AND PUT NOBODY IN IT AND SAID SO IN HER OWN BOOK. NO COLUMN WAS RULED ON ANY OF THE FIFTY DAYS AND THE COUNT OF COLUMNS OF NOT-ASKINGS IS FOUR AND DID NOT MOVE.**

# VOLUME 14 BLOCK 0001 — CHARACTER STATE AFTER CHAPTER 665

> **APPENDED BY THE BATCH THAT WROTE CHAPTERS 651 TO 665. AGES ARE AS THE PAGES CARRY THEM AND NOBODY'S AGE MOVED IN THIS BLOCK.**

## THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING, THE PROTAGONIST, UNNAMED

Came up that bank before the clerk had that book open on all fifteen mornings and did not say the sentence about what his yard is for on any of them, having said out loud on the fiftieth morning of the last volume that he was not going to say it twice. **Said one thing out loud in this block, on Chapter 653, about a rail he put in that fence about four weeks ago and a man who puts his own two hands on it every morning at about eleven and does not lean on it.** Was at the bottom of that bank and in the ditch for about two hours before the light came on the morning of Chapter 658, came up with mud to the knee of his own left leg that was not off that bank, and nobody at that wall said a word about it and he did not. **His name is on no page of these fifteen chapters and the string `Adrian` is on no page of them.** He is not in the column for the name of whoever read a thing out loud and no person appointed him to it.

## THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO DIGS LOAM, UNNAMED, AND THE ONE THE FIFTH LINE NAMES

Said one thing out loud in this block, on Chapter 654, about not having once put his own spade down while he was standing at that table. At the far end of that second table at about eleven on every morning of the block with his own spade against the leg of it and his own two hands on the rail, and on the morning of Chapter 655 he came up that bank at about half past six and watched the whole ten minutes of the count without moving and said nothing at all to anybody, and on the morning of Chapter 660 he had been at the bottom of that bank since about half past five and in the ditch since before it was light. **On Chapter 662 he was at that table at about eleven and was not at it in the afternoon, and nobody in that yard asked him where he was, and a clerk entered that and entered no reason, and the record about the not asking says not asked.** He is still paid nothing, he was not paid on any of the fifteen mornings, he is not asked one thing about what he is to be used for on any of them, and he is not in the column.

## A CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS

Enters every count before anything else, at the near end of that first table at about a quarter to eight and again at about four. **On Chapter 655 she counted the sixth month of this year off that board with a finger on the wood, in the open, in daylight, with about nineteen people at that wall, in about ten minutes, and got thirty-one, and said the figure out loud in the ordinary voice, and the boy counted what she said, and she entered that the count of counted months is six and was five yesterday, and she entered that it is a count and is kept in a different book from the wall.** Said one thing out loud on twelve of the fifteen mornings and once on Chapter 660. On Chapter 665 she said out loud that there are two fifths in that book and that she has put them next to each other on purpose. She is not in the column and nobody appointed her to it and she is not paid and nobody has asked her about that.

## THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX

Read the four off that wall at about a quarter to eight and again at about four on all fifteen mornings, in the ordinary voice, got all four both times, and **was not asked what one of them is for on any of the fifteen and was not asked one thing about any of the four.** On the morning of Chapter 655 he read his four at about a quarter to eight with his own back to about nineteen people counting a month off a board six feet away from him, and nobody stopped him. About a quarter to eight and about four are the only two numbers of people anybody in that yard keeps about him. He said nothing out loud in this block.

## THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR

At the foot of that low wall with his coat folded on the stones beside him and nothing in his hands on all fifteen mornings, from his two hundred and ninetieth night of that run to his three hundred and fourth, having slept on two hundred and eighty-nine of the first through three hundred and three of the last. **Given nothing on all fifteen and asked nothing on all fifteen, and the block did not soften one of them and did not put a fifth question to him.** On the morning of Chapter 655 he was not counted and has not been counted on any morning.

## THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN

Counted every speech in that yard and read the number back to himself in a low voice, twenty-nine times across fifteen mornings, and the relay equals the claim count. **On Chapter 655 he counted the marks off that board with his own finger an inch behind the clerk's and said no number out loud the whole time, and counted them again with his own thumbnail after she took her finger off the wood and got the same number twice.** Said one thing out loud on seven mornings. On Chapter 665 he said out loud that he has counted every one of about nineteen people and named none of them. **He asked no question on any of the fifteen mornings and he is not in the column.**

## THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY

Said one thing out loud in this block, on Chapter 659, that a man came down that lane at about half past eight with a page folded in his own right hand and did not stop at the top of it and went on down past that bank, and that the reason that man is the tenth is that he came down that lane with a page in his hand. **He gave the reason in the yard, out loud, to about nineteen people, and the clerk put the number in her book after he had said it and not before.** He is not from this parish, is not a resident of this district, and is not in the column.

## THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO WHO PUTS TABLES UP

Said one thing out loud in this block, on Chapter 651, about a piece of card folded under the near leg of that second table and about nobody in that yard ever having once asked him how a table stands. **He has not been paid for one item of what he has done in six volumes and was not paid on any of the fifteen mornings of this block and has not asked anybody for anything.** The second table is still in that yard and goes down that bank on day 36, which is not this block's.

## THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THAT CART

Came up at about three on all fifteen mornings with the near wheel still dragging and got it up about nine inches off the ground with one arm. Said one thing out loud on two mornings — on Chapter 652, that nobody has ever asked him what is in that yard, and on Chapter 663, that he has never once been asked how far that bank is and does not know and has not counted and is not going to. He is not from this parish, is not a resident, and is not in the column.

## THE ROAD KEEPER, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS

**The road keeper** came up that lane twice a day on all fifteen mornings and was not asked about the eleven miles on any of them, and was not asked about the stranger who came down that lane on Chapter 659 either. **The woman of about fifty-eight** came up past those tables at about ten to six on all fifteen mornings and read three lines in that lot book standing up about four feet off the fourth one, did not look at it, did not stop, and said nothing to anybody. **The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale** — her shop is open on all fifteen mornings with a door propped open and three pages on that counter, that is a fact and not a scene, the count of the four places where those three lines can be read is four and did not move, and she is in no mouth on any of the fifteen and was asked nothing on any of them. **The man of about thirty-seven who cuts reeds** said nothing out loud in this block and was not given one.

## THE REVIEW FIX OF 2026-09-29, APPLIED TO BLOCK 0001 AFTER THE FIFTEEN CHAPTERS WERE WRITTEN

**THIS IS AN ENTRY IN THE ROLLING STATE AND NOT A CHAPTER AND IT ADDS NOTHING TO THE STORY. NOT ONE EVENT, FIGURE, DATE, COUNT, SPEECH, CLAIM, DURATION, LADDER ROW, RELAY OR NAME ON PAGES 651 TO 665 CHANGED. WHAT CHANGED IS FIFTEEN TITLES, FOURTEEN BYSTANDER PARAGRAPHS, AND FOUR FIGURES IN TWO STATE FILES THAT WERE MEASURED WRONG.**

**THE FIFTEEN TITLES.** All fifteen were summaries of their own morning's two speeches and ran 28 to 43 words with a median of 35, fourteen over the volume's own thirty-word cap. All fifteen now run 17 to 25 with a median of 22 and none over the cap. **The `On The <Date>` prefix is on all fifteen and was not touched, so the date of record for each morning is unchanged and the fifteen dates are still fifteen consecutive days, the twenty-seventh of the sixth month to the tenth of the seventh month.** A writer loading these fifteen pages for voice should load the prose and not the old titles.

**THE FOURTEEN BYSTANDER PARAGRAPHS.** Fourteen paragraphs across twelve mornings nested one attribution clause inside another inside another, at up to 173 words with no internal sentence end after the first sentence. They were broken into plain sentences. The secondhand-observer register is kept, because it is this volume's signature and it is the reason the fifth line goes unanswered; what was removed is the stacking. The clauses `about four of them have said that`, `about four of you have worked out that` and `have said since that` stand at 49, 76 and 113, against 80, 76 and 117 before. **The rule is written down as guardrail 21 in `workspace/volume-14/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` and a later block does not have to rediscover it.**

**THE FOUR FIGURES.** `state/volume-14-batch-0001-summary.md` Section 9 said three identical paragraphs and the sweep returns two; the third was the second table, which is a sentence inside a longer paragraph, and a sentence is not a paragraph. That miscount had already been copied into the next phase's prompt and is corrected in both. The cart row's `where` column said `every one but 663` and the cart paragraph is absent from 652 and 663. The next prompt's body-word figure of 43,566 was wrong against 43,575 and has been re-derived again after the prose repair at 43,258 body words over 630 segments. Length over the fifteen files is now 43,655 by `wc -w`, mean 2,910.3, against 44,185 and 2,945.7 before.

**AND THE SIX THAT WERE NOT DEFECTS.** `logs/batch-0001.review.log` reports the eighteen-row ladder broken on all fifteen chapters, `three hundredth` resolving to 103, Chapter 661 missing a row, Chapter 660 missing its ledger line, the clerk's short speeches stamped at twice the block's speaking rate, and six titles without a date. All six are wrong and all six were bugs in the reviewer's own hand-built word-number parser — compound ordinals, round-hundred ordinals, ordinal suffixes after `hundred`, the singular verb on a single-claim morning, a reciprocal rate, and hyphenated date words. The ladder is 270 cells with zero failures, Chapter 661 carries its row, Chapter 660 correctly reads `About one thing was said out loud`, and all fifteen titles carry their date. **A later block that builds its own resolver should read the two traps the block record already names at Section 2 and Section 3, because this review re-derived them from nothing and reported them as new.**

# VOLUME 14, BLOCK 0003 — CHARACTER STATE AT CHAPTER 690

**THE PROTAGONIST, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING, IS UNNAMED AND HIS NAME IS ON NO PAGE OF CHAPTERS 1 TO 690. HE CAME UP THAT BANK AT ABOUT A QUARTER TO EIGHT ON ALL TEN MORNINGS AND WAS AT THE NEAR END OF THAT SECOND TABLE UNTIL ABOUT TEN ON DAYS 31 TO 35. ON CHAPTER 686, DAY 36, HE WENT DOWN THAT BANK AT ABOUT TEN WITH NOTHING AT ALL IN HIS OWN HANDS AND DID NOT COME BACK UP IT THAT DAY, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT AND ENTERED NO REASON. ## VOLUME 14, BLOCK 0003 — THE REVIEW REPAIR OF 2026-09-29, AND WHAT IT DID TO THE VOICES

**NOT ONE CHARACTER ON CHAPTERS 681 TO 690 SAID A DIFFERENT THING AFTER THE REPAIR THAN BEFORE IT. THE SAME MEN AND THE SAME CLERK AND THE SAME BOY SPOKE ON THE SAME MORNINGS, IN THE SAME ORDER, TO THE SAME AUDIENCE, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM SAID THE SAME CLAIMS. WHAT CHANGED IS THE JOINING WITHIN THE SPEECHES, AND THE CONSEQUENCE FOR A CHARACTER IS THE SAME AS FOR ANY OTHER PROSE: THEY SOUND LIKE THEMSELVES AGAIN INSTEAD OF LIKE A TEMPLATE.**

**THE FIGURE THAT MATTERS TO ANYONE READING A VOICE: `about four of you have worked out that` WAS THE HOUSE'S ATTRIBUTION FRAME AND IT WAS BEING USED THREE TIMES IN ONE BREATH, WHICH IS NOT AN ATTRIBUTION, IT IS FILLER. IT IS NOW ONE PER SPEECH AND IT OPENS THE SPEECH RATHER THAN REPEATING THROUGH IT. THE ATTRIBUTION REGISTER ITSELF, WHICH IS THIS MANUSCRIPT'S REAL INSTRUMENT, IS UNTOUCHED: `about four of them have said that` IS STILL ON SEVENTY-SIX OCCURRENCES AND `have said since that` ON SEVENTY-TWO, IN THE BYSTANDER PARAGRAPHS WHERE THEY BELONG.**

**AND THE CLOSERS ARE NOW TWENTY-ONE DIFFERENT SENTENCES INSTEAD OF ONE SENTENCE WITH FOURTEEN AND SEVEN VARIANTS. A MAN WHO ENDS EVERY MORNING THE SAME WAY IS NOT A MAN WHO HAS HAD A BAD MORNING; HE IS A WRITER WHO RAN OUT OF SENTENCES, AND HE WAS READ AS THE LATTER, WHICH IS WORSE FOR THE CHARACTER AND BETTER FOR NOTHING.**

**THE CHARACTER STATE ITSELF IS UNCHANGED BY THE REPAIR AND IS RESTATED HERE ONLY SO A SUCCESSOR DOES NOT INHERIT THE PRE-REPAIR FIGURES AS IF THEY WERE VOICE.** THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO WHO PUTS TABLES UP SAID ONE THING ON 681, ONE ON 682, ONE ON 683, ONE ON 687, ONE ON 690, AND THREE ON 686. THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS SAID ONE THING ON 681, ONE ON 683, ONE ON 684, ONE ON 685, TWO ON 686, ONE ON 688, ONE ON 689 AND ONE ON 690. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING SAID ONE THING, ON 683, AND IT IS ABOUT THE WOOD MOVING UNDER HIS OWN HANDS AND IS NOT THE SENTENCE THE VOLUME OWES. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO DIGS LOAM SAID ONE, ON 684. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY SAID ONE, ON 685, AND IT IS NOT THE VOLUME'S QUESTION. THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THAT CART SAID ONE, ON 686, ON THE MORNING HE CARRIED A TABLE DOWN A BANK. `Adrian` IS ON NO PAGE OF CHAPTERS 1 TO 700.**

ON CHAPTER 705 SHE CAME UP THAT BANK AGAIN AND COULD NOT SEE THE FIRST LINE, AND SAID ONE THING OUT LOUD, AND THE THING WAS THAT THE PLACE A PAGE IS READ FROM IS PART OF THE PAGE. ON CHAPTER 711 SHE SAID ONE THING OUT LOUD AND THE THING WAS THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO LOOK AT THE FOURTH LINE THIS EVENING OR ANY OTHER.** She is not in the column and nobody has entered her in it. She is not a failing and a bystander said so on two mornings.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS.** Stood at that table on Chapter 708, read the marks in chalk along the edge of it, put no mark in himself, and said one thing out loud about not having been asked one thing. He is not in the column.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE.** **HER SHOP IS OPEN ON ALL FIFTEEN MORNINGS AND THAT IS A FACT AND NOT A SCENE, SHE IS PUT IN NO MOUTH ON ANY OF THEM, SHE IS NOT ASKED ONE THING, AND THREE PAGES LIE ON HER COUNTER ON ALL FIFTEEN MORNINGS AND THE COUNT OF THE FOUR PLACES DID NOT MOVE.**

**THE ROAD KEEPER.** Up that lane twice a day on all fifteen mornings, reading the four figures off that wall as he went by both times, **and was not asked about the eleven miles on any of them.** A shop counter is a place and is not on the list of four on all fifteen mornings.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THAT CART.** Came up at about three on all fifteen mornings with the near wheel still dragging, got it up about nine inches off the ground with one arm, went on up that bank, and did not stay at the top of it.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR.** At the foot of that low wall with his coat folded on the stones beside him and nothing in his hands, on all fifteen mornings, from his three hundred and fortieth night of that run to his three hundred and fifty-fourth, having slept on three hundred and thirty-nine through three hundred and fifty-three of them. **GIVEN NOTHING AND ASKED NOTHING ON ALL FIFTEEN MORNINGS. NOT OFFERED A CHAIR, A PLACE, A DATE, A FIGURE, A PAGE, A THUMB, A STONE, A BOOK, A WORD OR A THANK YOU. NOT THANKED. NOT A FAILURE AND NOT A TRAGIC FIGURE AND NOT ONE MORNING OF HIS RUN SOFTENED.** A stranger stood about nine feet off a man at the bottom of that bank on one morning and he was not asked one thing about it.

**THE BOY WHO COUNTED THE QUIET.** HE DOES NOT EXIST YET. HE IS ON DAY 25, CHAPTER 725, IN BLOCK 0002, AND HE IS NOT APPOINTED TO ANYTHING, HIS NUMBER IS NOT ENTERED ANYWHERE FOR ANY REASON, AND HE IS NOT IN THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD.

**A STRANGER.** Came down that lane on the twenty-third of the eighth month with a page in his own right hand, looked at a first table with nothing on it, went on down to the foot of that bank, read the five lines about nine feet off the man they are about, said nothing to him, and went back up that lane. **HE IS NOT A RESIDENT OF THIS DISTRICT AND NO NAME WAS ENTERED AND HE IS NOT IN THE COLUMN, BECAUSE HE DID NOT READ A THING OUT LOUD. NO PERSON WAS ADDED TO THIS DISTRICT THAT MORNING AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT.**

# VOLUME 15, BLOCKS 0002, 0003 AND 0004 — CHARACTER STATE AT CHAPTER 750

**THIRTY-FIVE CHAPTERS ARE ON DISK AND WERE IN NO STATE FILE BEFORE THE REVIEW FIX OF 2026-09-30. THIS FILE STOPPED AT CHAPTER 715. THE PEOPLE BELOW ARE THE SAME SIX THE VOLUME HAS CARRIED SINCE BLOCK 0001, AND THE ONE NEW THING ABOUT THEM IS MEASURED: ACROSS THE LAST THIRTY-FIVE MORNINGS THEY ARE STILL CHARACTERISED BY AGE AND TRADE AND BY NOTHING ELSE, WHICH IS THE HOUSE'S OWN SYSTEM AND IS ALSO THE HIGHEST-RANKED PROSE FINDING OF THE BLOCK 0004 REVIEW AND IS ESCALATED AT `state/maintainer-escalation.md` ITEM 5 RATHER THAN FIXED HERE.**

**THE HOUSE NAMES A PERSON BY AN AGE AND A TRADE AND GIVES THEM NO NAME, AND THAT IS A SYSTEM AND NOT AN OMISSION: A NAME IN THIS DISTRICT IS A FIGURE IN A COLUMN, THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD IS RULED AND EMPTY AT ABOUT SIX ON ALL FIFTY MORNINGS OF THIS VOLUME, AND A NAME PUT IN THAT COLUMN WOULD BE A DOCUMENT THIS DISTRICT OWNS. NOBODY IN THIS FILE BELOW HAS BEEN ENTERED IN IT AND NOBODY MAY BE.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO WHO PUTS TABLES UP.** At the foot of that bank on thirty-five mornings of this volume, on eight of the last ten, and not at that table at all on two of the last ten, which are the two mornings the count of mornings a man has been at the foot of that bank held. **He was at about three in the afternoon in that yard for six months and is at about half past nine at the bottom of the bank now, and nobody asked him to be earlier and nobody asked him why.** He cut a length of wood on the nineteenth morning after the thirtieth of the eighth month, being Chapter 735, and put a rail on the near side of that table with it on the twenty-first morning, being Chapter 737, in about ten minutes, with his own two hands, and nobody asked him to and nobody has thanked him on any morning since. **HIS OWN RIGHT HAND IS OPEN AND ABOUT NINE INCHES OF THE SKIN IS GONE AT THE BASE OF THE THUMB, AND HE HAS NOT SHUT THAT HAND ONCE SINCE THE MORNING HE CUT THE WOOD, AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD HAS ASKED HIM TO SHUT IT AND NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM HOW IT GOT THAT WAY.** He said one thing out loud on the twenty-ninth morning of the last block that there is a rail on the near side of that table and that a man stood at it that morning and read the fifth line and did not ask him one thing about the rail, and that the table is not coming up that bank this year, and that he is not going to say when it will and not going to say whether it will. He said one thing out loud on the thirty-third morning that he was not at the foot of that bank the day before and is not going to say where he was, and that there is nothing left on that table to do, and that he is going to stand there anyway. **HE SAID ONE THING OUT LOUD ON THE FIFTIETH MORNING THAT HE PUT THAT RAIL ON IN HIS OWN TIME AND NOBODY ASKED HIM TO AND NOBODY HAS THANKED HIM, AND THAT THIS IS A THING A PERSON DID AND NOT A THING THIS DISTRICT HAS MADE, AND THAT HE IS NOT WAITING TO BE THANKED FOR IT.** He is unpaid. He is not in the column and never has been.

**THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS.** At the near end of that first table at the top of that bank at about a quarter to eight on every morning of the last block, and again in the closing entry at about four, about two hundred feet above the table several of her own figures are a count of. She enters the figure on the sheet at that gatepost and the count of counted months and the count of conditions with no end on it and the count of documents this district does not own and the number of places where those three lines can be read and that a shop counter is a place and is not the list. **SHE ENTERED THE LANE COUNT AT EIGHTEEN AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST TEN ON CHAPTER 745, AFTER A BOY HAD SAID THE NUMBER OUT LOUD IN THAT YARD AND NOT BEFORE, AND SHE ENTERED THAT A STRANGER IS NOT A RESIDENT OF THIS DISTRICT AND ENTERED NO NAME.** On Chapter 745 she also entered the count of mornings a man has been at the foot of that bank at thirty-one on her own page, with the reason entered that the man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally was not in that yard that morning, and on Chapters 743 and 748 she entered that the man who puts tables up was not at that table and entered no hour and entered no reason. **HE WAS AT THE FOOT OF THAT LOW WALL ON ALL FIFTY MORNINGS WITH HIS COAT FOLDED ON THE STONES BESIDE HIM AND NOTHING IN HIS HANDS, ON HIS THREE HUNDRED AND FORTIETH NIGHT OF THAT RUN THROUGH HIS THREE HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-NINTH, HAVING SLEPT ON THREE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-NINE THROUGH THREE HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-EIGHT. HE WAS GIVEN NOTHING ON ALL FIFTY AND WAS ASKED NOTHING ON ALL FIFTY AND NOT ONE MORNING OF HIS RUN WAS SOFTENED AND NO FIFTH QUESTION WAS PUT TO HIM. A CLERK ENTERED AT ABOUT FOUR THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO SET DOWN WHAT HE IS GOING TO DO WITH HIS HANDS TONIGHT BECAUSE NOBODY ASKED HER.**

## THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-EIGHT

**SHE CAME UP THAT BANK AT ABOUT TEN EVERY MORNING AND WENT BACK DOWN IT AT ABOUT TEN TO SIX, BECAUSE THE BOOK IS AT THE BOTTOM OF THE BANK NOW, AND READ THREE LINES IN THAT LOT BOOK STANDING UP ABOUT FOUR FEET OFF THE FOURTH ONE AND DID NOT STOP AND DID NOT LOOK AT IT, AND THAT IS NOT A FAILING. SHE SAID ONE THING OUT LOUD ON NINE OF THE FIFTY MORNINGS, BEING DAYS 1, 5, 11, 17, 21, 31, 35, 43 AND 48.** On Chapter 743 she said that she has read three lines of that book standing up for two years and that this morning her own hand was on the fourth of them and she did not read it, and that those nine words have not changed since that table came down that bank, and that she is not going to say what they are.

## THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE

**HER SHOP IS OPEN ON EVERY MORNING OF THIS VOLUME AND THAT IS A FACT AND NOT A SCENE. SHE IS PUT IN NO MOUTH ON ANY OF THE FIFTY MORNINGS, SHE IS NOT ASKED ONE THING, THREE PAGES LIE ON HER COUNTER ON EVERY MORNING, A SHOP COUNTER IS A PLACE AND IS NOT ON THE LIST OF FOUR, AND THE COUNT OF THE FOUR DID NOT MOVE ONCE.**

## THE ROAD KEEPER, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THAT CART

**THE ROAD KEEPER CAME UP THAT LANE TWICE A DAY ON ALL FIFTY MORNINGS, READ THE FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL AS HE WENT BY BOTH TIMES, AND WAS NOT ASKED ABOUT THE ELEVEN MILES ON ANY OF THEM.** The cart came up that bank at about three with the near wheel still dragging, the man got it up about nine inches off the ground with one arm, went on up that bank, and did not stay at the top of it.

## THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS

**HE CAME DOWN THAT BANK ON CHAPTER 708 AT ABOUT TEN AND READ THE MARKS IN CHALK ALONG THE EDGE OF THAT TABLE ONE AFTER ANOTHER AND DID NOT PUT A MARK IN HIMSELF. HE SAID ONE THING OUT LOUD ON FIVE OF THE FIFTY MORNINGS, BEING DAYS 8, 19, 28, 33 AND 44, BEING CHAPTERS 708, 719, 728, 733 AND 744. On Chapter 733 he said that he can tell from the cut at the end of a piece which way it wants to lie and that he is not going to say, and that a man has had his own two hands on that near leg for a fortnight, WHICH THE CHAPTER THAT CARRIES IT REFUTES.** On Chapter 744 he said there is a length of fencing wire in a man's own pocket at the foot of that bank and he has not taken one piece of it, and that he has wire of his own in a bag in this yard, and that nobody in that yard has asked him one thing about that table and he is not going to stand at that table and wait to be asked.

## THE STRANGERS

**FOUR OF THEM CAME DOWN THAT LANE WITH A PAGE IN THEIR OWN RIGHT HAND ON FOUR MORNINGS OF THIS VOLUME, BEING DAYS 9, 30, 38 AND 45, BEING CHAPTERS 709, 730, 738 AND 745, AND THE LANE COUNT MOVED ON NONE OF THE OTHERS.** Each read the five lines about nine feet off the man they name and said nothing. On Chapter 745 one stood at the far end of that second table, did not look at either rail, said not one word to anybody there, and went back up about half an hour later. **NONE IS A RESIDENT OF THIS DISTRICT, NONE IS ENTERED IN THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD, AND NO NAME WAS ENTERED FOR ANY OF THE FOUR.**

## AND THE ONE THAT IS NOT SETTLED

**THE PROTAGONIST'S ALLOWANCE OF ONE THING SAID OUT LOUD IN THE WHOLE OF VOLUME 15 IS UNSPENT AFTER FIFTY MORNINGS. HE IS NOT IN THE COLUMN. HE IS NOT ON ANY PAGE OF CHAPTERS 163 TO 750, MEASURED, 0 OCCURRENCES IN ANY CASE, AND THE DAY THAT WOULD LET A PERSON NAME HIM IS DAY FORTY-ONE, WHICH WAS CHAPTER 741, AND NOBODY WAS PUT IN A POSITION TO SAY IT, AND THE RULE THAT WOULD PERMIT IT IS NOT A CLOSE'S TO CHANGE. A CLOSE WEIGHED IT ONCE AND REFUSED, AND THAT IS THE SIXTH REFUSAL, AND IT IS OPEN AT `state/maintainer-escalation.md` ITEM 1.**

**THIS APPEND COVERS CHAPTERS 701 TO 750 AND CARRIES THE STATE AT CHAPTER 750, NOT A SUMMARY OF THE FIFTY MORNINGS. ITS FIGURES CAME FROM THE FIFTY CHAPTER FILES OF `chapters/volume-15/`, RE-DERIVED BY THE VOLUME 15 CLOSE PHASE, AND FROM THE FIFTY PAGES OF THE FOUR BLOCK RECORDS. THE VERDICT IS `state/volume-15-close.md` AND THE INDEX IS `state/volume-15-roll-summary.md`.**

# VOLUME 16, BLOCK 0001 — CHARACTER STATE AFTER CHAPTER 760, APPENDED 2026-09-30

**THE REGISTERS OF THESE TWO HUNDRED AND FIFTY YEARS OF VOLUMES 05 TO 15 DESCRIBE PEOPLE BY AGE AND TRADE AND GAVE THEM NO NAMES. THIS BLOCK SETTLES THE PROTAGONIST'S NAME AND FOUR OTHERS AND IT RECORDS WHO IS WHO. A SUCCESSOR MAY NOT RETURN TO THE UNNAMED FORM FOR A CHARACTER NAMED HERE.**

| who | age | what is true at the end of Chapter 760 |
|---|---:|---|
| **Adrian Vale** | 38 | Said his name in that yard on the thirty-ninth morning and had it entered. Fifty-one mornings in that yard. No household, no private lot, no name on any page before Chapter 751 and every page after it. Spent his Volume 15 allowance of one thing said out loud on his own name. Carries a length of fencing wire he has not looked at in fifty mornings. **His want has changed shape: he came to ask what the fifth line is for and he leaves having discovered that the district's long unpayable debt is the reason it cannot hold a seat, and that the fix is an instrument, not a gesture.** |
| **Mara Quill** | 34 | The clerk of this district for fifteen years. Wrote the fifth line at the dictation of the man it names, on an afternoon fifteen years ago, when she was nineteen. Has signed her own work with a figure nine years out of date for fifteen years because a figure may not be corrected and she wrote the rule. Filled the column for the name of whoever read a thing out loud and entered that she was talked into it. Caught Adrian about to hide a security in his own pocket, on the fortieth morning, and made him amend it. **Now wants: a form. She has said the district cannot answer anything without one and she has not drawn one.** |
| **Orren Pike** | 17 | Counted everything said out loud in that yard for fifty mornings and was never asked why. Holds a sheet with four columns, the fourth headed *who was standing next to them*, which is the only witness-like instrument in the basin. His mother's row says *the household*. Refused Adrian the sheet and refused to let him publish his own row. Refused, on the forty-third morning, to write down the near miss in his own list. Gave his name on the second morning. **Now wants: to hand Sela Pike's row to a person with a word where a person should be, and he does not yet know who.** |
| **Seth Arlow** | about 32 | Fitted two rails and cut one length of wood on a table for nobody and has been thanked by nobody. Named himself on the thirty-eighth morning, by letting Adrian Vale use the name first and then saying *you can use it, everybody else here can use it, I just don't use it on a page*; and said out loud on the forty-fourth that he is not waiting to be thanked. Went to Alder Reach and came back with Room Four. **Now wants: to be the first person to be thanked in this district for a thing he did, and he has said he is not waiting for it, which is a claim and not a refusal.** |
| **the man who digs loam** | about 31 | At the far end of the second table every morning, spade against the leg, two hands on the near rail. Dictated the fifth line fifteen years ago at about twenty-two. Said no once, to something, in fifteen years, and will not say to what. **Gave his name to Adrian on the forty-third morning at the foot of that bank and Adrian did not write it down. His name is on no page in this manuscript and that is the point.** |
| **the man of fifty-six** | 56 | Reads four figures off that wall twice a morning and has got them four hundred and eighty-five mornings running and has never once been asked what one of them is for. On the thirty-eighth morning he said in a yard that one of the four is doing something he cannot describe and that he cannot tell you which, and refused to guess. **Now wants: it asked in a yard one morning by somebody who is not Adrian. He also wants, badly, for somebody to ask him something.** |
| **the woman of fifty-eight** | about 58 | Carries for people who are not on anything and is not paid for it. Said the nine words of the fourth line fifteen years ago for nine coppers she has never been able to spend because there is no form. Has read those three lines standing up every working morning for fifteen years. **Now wants: to be asked about the three pages on her own counter, which nobody has done in nine years, and she has now asked to be.** |
| **the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale** | about 36 | A shop at the top of the bank, a scale, three pages on a counter, one of them not hers. Has weighed about nineteen people for nine years. Argued that the count of places where the three lines can be read must stay at four and was right, and stopped the district from moving a figure on a shop window. **Now wants: to be asked.** |
| **the man of about sixty-four** | about 64 | At the foot of the low wall on his three hundred and ninety-ninth night of a run, having slept on three hundred and ninety-eight of them. Given nothing and asked nothing for the whole of the manuscript. **The one figure in this district with no thread attached to it and the largest unpaid debt in the book.** |
| **the man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally** | about 48 | The source of the count of mornings a man has been at the foot of that bank, entered after he has said it and not before. Was at the first table at seven on the forty-first morning and the clerk entered the figure without walking up the hill to look, and a boy of seventeen caught her doing it. |
| **the man of about twenty-nine who drives the cart** | about 29 | Nine years up that bank at about three with a near wheel that drags. Took the answer up the lane on the forty-fourth morning because nobody else was asked. Orren has wanted to count him for three days and has not been asked by the right person. |

She has signed her own work with a figure fifteen years out of date because a figure may not be corrected in this district and she wrote that rule. **SHE NOW ENTERS REASONS, WHICH SHE NEVER DID IN FIFTEEN YEARS, AND SHE DID IT ON THE FORTY-FIRST MORNING FOR A HOLE IN A MAP AND ON THE FORTY-SECOND FOR HER OWN HAND ON THE FIFTH LINE.** |

| **the woman of fifty-eight** | **SHE SPOKE A SECOND TIME IN A YARD IN CHAPTER 754 AND WHAT SHE SAID COST ADRIAN SOMETHING PERMANENT: that saying out loud that a man in the yard could be paid for standing in a room has taken away the district's only remaining answer, which was *we have nobody*.** She is the only person in that yard who has said a true thing about Adrian to his face and then told him the price of it. |
| **the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale** | **ON THE FORTY-FIRST MORNING SHE WAS ONE OF THE FOUR WHO DREW THE MAP, AND SHE WAS TOLD HER TRADE WAS THE FIRST THING ANYBODY HAD SAID TO HER ABOUT IT THAT WAS NOT A QUESTION ABOUT THE PRICE OF SOMETHING.** She still has three pages on her counter and one of them is not hers. |

| who | how it was settled | on what morning |
|---|---|---|
| **Mavis Dorr** | the woman of fifty-eight, said out loud in a yard, at the top of that bank, in front of about nineteen people, **against her own argument that the only protection anybody in this basin has is being the one nobody can look up**. The clerk entered it on the page with nothing ruled on it and not in the lot book. | the fifty-fourth, at about ten to six |
| **Nell Cray** | the woman Mavis Dorr carries for, given out loud **by Mavis Dorr, not by herself**, at the foot of that bank, four feet off the near end of the second table, after Adrian had said in front of her what he would do with the answer. **SHE IS NOT ON ANY PAGE IN THIS DISTRICT AND SAYS *ASK THE MARKET THAT SENT HER* ON A NOTE IN A CORRIDOR IN ALDER REACH, AND THIS MARKET SENT NOBODY.** | the fiftieth, at about ten |
| **Tarin Kest** | forty-three, entered in the lane count on the forty-eighth morning, **the first name ever entered beside that figure in fifteen years**, and he asked for it: *if I don't say it out loud they can't use it, and I've never in my life had a paper that said I was not a person because I had not asked to be one.* | the forty-eighth, at about a quarter past eleven |

## WHAT CHANGED FOR EVERYBODY

| who | what is true at the end of Chapter 770 |
|---|---|
| **Adrian Vale, 38** | Seventy mornings in that yard and no bed, no wage and no office, and not asked for any of the three and did not ask. He wrote the district's answer up that lane with *we* in it seven times and it came back with a figure and a term and *as sent* on the back, and the figure is attached to his name. He asked the man of fifty-six's question to be asked and was refused it, and was refused the asking of a man of about sixty-four, and took his pencil out and put it on the boards. **He is wrong about the eleventh mile and has not conceded it. He has been offered a place on it and it is not a wage.** He read a folded page of his own out loud in a yard on the fifty-fourth morning. The wire is still in his pocket. |
| **Mara Quill, 34** | The clerk of this district for fifteen years. She has now entered, on a page with nothing ruled on it, a line about a man at a foot of a wall and a line about a woman's name, and neither is in the lot book. She entered a no in a margin and said a margin is not a sixth line. She entered the count of the five as five and not as the number of lines on her own page. **She refused a second name in the column and a boy of seventeen was right about her reason and she said she had known it since a quarter to eight. She is the district's instrument and she is the district's first recorded defect.** |
| **Orren Pike, 17** | He found the word in his own first column and said it out loud in a yard without saying whose row. He asked the man of fifty-six for the thing the man asked to have asked, and got it, and read the answer out in a yard from a page that is not a record of anybody. He counted a man for the first time in his life and said seventy, and did not take fifty off. He got the boy of thirty-two to a figure by getting to a count before a clerk of fifteen years could. **He counted nothing said out loud in that yard for the first time in about four years, on the morning a man was thanked, and the margin is blank and he can say why.** He has still not connected the row. |
| **Seth Arlow, about 32** | **The named person this block makes worse off.** Said forty coppers for one year in a yard and was thanked for a rail by about eleven people and understood in about four seconds that being thanked is the receipt. His own sentence about counts is now false about every figure he has entered in nine years. The rail is a thing the district has made in nineteen mouths and the count is still fourteen. He is not a guarantor, the clerk entered that, and he is not paid, and the fifth of the five is not paid. **He has asked for one thing: that nobody thank him again, and the man of fifty-six has forbidden it in a yard, and nobody thanked the man of fifty-six either.** |
| **the man who digs loam, about 31** | Asked the man of about sixty-four what the run is, and refused Adrian the asking to do it himself. Read the fifth line out loud in a yard for the first time in fifteen years. Said the district's one unpaid man is the reason the district cannot send anybody and that he is not the bearer. **Said a second no in fifteen years and asked for it to be written down, and it is in a margin, and he is now findable in a way he spent nine years preventing, and he said out loud that he would rather be a line than be a favour.** He is still unnamed on every page of this manuscript and gave his name to one person on the forty-third morning of Block 0001 and it is on no page. |
| **the man of fifty-six** | Read the four figures off that wall twice a morning for four hundred and ninety-five mornings and for the first time in nine years there was another man on that wall at about four, forty feet off. **He was asked in a yard, by a boy of seventeen, the question he asked to have asked, and he still cannot say which of the four, and he said he wants nothing to happen and that he will be frightened for about a year, and that a yard with a subject is a yard where the man being looked into cannot leave.** He then forbade the thanking of Seth Arlow on pain of four figures read aloud. He read a fifth figure out loud on the fifty-fourth morning because a boy asked him to. **HE HAS NOW BEEN ASKED THE ONE QUESTION HE WANTED AND IT MADE HIM LESS SAFE AND HE SAID SO BEFOREHAND.** |
| **the man of about sixty-four** | On his four hundred and ninth night, having slept on four hundred and eight of them. **He has been asked one question in four hundred and nine nights and he has answered it and the answer cost him the only thing he had, which was that nothing he said could be used.** He is on a page with nothing ruled on it in a clerk's own book. There is no line for him anywhere. He asked for the answer not to be written down and it was written down. He has never been offered a bed, a wage, food, or an end. |
| **the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale** | Read the only copy of a corridor note out loud in a yard at about one and got all four lines. **She is not on any page and could not be, because the column for the name of whoever read a thing out loud has one name in it and the clerk would not take a second and the boy said the clerk was wrong.** Her own sentence: a thing weighed is a thing known, and it is worth more than everything else in the yard, and not because the weight is right. She has three pages on a counter and one of them is not hers and there is still no fifth place. **She is still unnamed.** |
| **the man of about twenty-nine who drives the cart** | **He is the figure twenty-one, in a yard, by the district's own rule, and he accepted it out loud and defended the boy who did it.** He learned Adrian told a stranger about him without asking. He carried the district's answer back up the lane and the corridor note up the lane on the fifty-third morning, and said yes before anybody had finished asking. He is still unnamed and he is now the only person in the district who is a figure because a rule was applied correctly. |
| **the man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally** | The source of the only figure in the district that moves every morning. He was at the top of the eleven feet on the forty-sixth morning watching a figure he had entered come out wrong in front of him. He thanked Seth for the rail, which is one of the eleven. He is still unnamed. |
| **Nell Cray** | The person Mavis Dorr carries for. Went to Alder Reach four months back, came back, said *I've been* to a step, and said *that is what not asking is for* when she was told. She is on a working list of forty-one in a corridor in Alder Reach and the note says ask the market that sent her and this market sent nobody. **She is on no page in this district and has been said out loud twice in a yard here.** |

## STILL UNNAMED, AND A SUCCESSOR MAY NOT NAME ANY OF THEM ON THE STRENGTH OF THIS BLOCK

**the man at the end of the second table; the woman who keeps a scale; the man of about twenty-nine who drives the cart; the man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally; the turner who comes up that lane twice a day and reads the wall and is not one of Kest's and never has been.**

Her no-correction rule is hers at about twenty-five and nine years back, not twenty-four and fifteen. SHE IS THE FIRST RECORDED DEFECT IN THE DISTRICT'S OWN INSTRUMENT.** |

He named the difference between the woman who read the corridor note and the man already in the column as not a difference of kind but of who asked, and said it is his fault. **HE HAS ADMITTED MISUSING HIS OWN INSTRUMENT IN PUBLIC AT SEVENTEEN AND HE DID NOT APOLOGISE FOR IT.** He also has not connected the row. |
| **Tarin Kest, 43** | **He did not get his name into the column and he is not the man in it.** He is the first name ever entered beside the lane count and he asked for that. He offered Adrian an afternoon on the eleventh mile, a mile and a half of nothing, half past two to about four, and said in the same breath that he was NOT offering Adrian the man of about sixty-four's night, and that he has known about that night for two years and has never asked, because he asked a man at a turning once and was told what would happen to the next bundle. **And he said the inherited nine-mornings arithmetic out loud, once, in a yard, and said he got it from a shopkeeper, and said he will not say it again.** |
| **the man of fifty-six** | He is not in the column and never has been, and the clerk has now said in a yard that she is frightened of not offering it to him. | **the man of about twenty-nine who drives the cart** | **He now refuses the yard's praise before it arrives.** He says in that yard that he did not stop to be a person, that he stopped because a man with a list asked him to, that he did not think about it for about a minute and a half, and that this is the whole of the reason and it is not a good one. He is still the figure twenty-one and he is still unnamed. |

| **the man of about sixty-four** | **He has been entered in a clerk's own book against his express wish and he does not know it, and nobody in that district has told him, and the block record did not carry it as owed until a review caught it.** That is now the fourth thread in the book and it is his to learn or not to learn. |
| **the woman who keeps a scale** | **She read the corridor note out loud and is the only person in that yard who did not know its fourth line, which she discovered by having read it.** She is still unnamed and the clerk still cannot enter her. > **THE TABLE IN THE BLOCK 0002 APPEND STANDS AND IS NOT REPEATED. THE FOUR BELOW ARE THE NAME SETTLED BY THIS BLOCK, WHAT CHANGED FOR EVERYBODY, AND WHO IS STILL UNNAMED.**

| who | how it was settled | on what morning |

## WHAT CHANGED FOR EVERYBODY

| who | what is true at the end of Chapter 780 |
