# VOLUME 17, BLOCK 0001 — CHAPTERS 801 TO 810, CHARACTER STATE

- **ADRIAN VALE. THIRTY-EIGHT. ONE HUNDRED AND SIX MORNINGS IN THAT YARD ON THE EIGHTY-FIFTH MORNING AFTER THE THIRTIETH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH AND ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTEEN ON THE NINETY-FOURTH, WHICH IS THE MORNING NUMBER PLUS TWENTY-ONE AND IS COUNTED BY A BOY OF SEVENTEEN. NO BED, NO WAGE, NO OFFICE, NOT ASKED FOR ANY OF THE THREE.** In this block **he understood what had happened to that yard before anybody in it said a word; he stopped about nine people from touching a strip of paper and gave the reason, that a person handling it is this district holding it and a district that holds a matter cannot be asked about the holding of it; he argued that the clerk entered a fact and not an effect and that the fact was the only act open to anybody in that yard; and on the ninetieth morning he went four miles down that lane and put eleven feet of fencing wire across a mile and a half of nothing at a culvert, came back and told the yard out loud, before he did it and in about nineteen people's hearing, that he did not know what it was for, that everybody in that yard knew it was not going to work that afternoon, and that he would like somebody to be angry with him about it in about four days. He said he had carried the wire since the middle of the winter and had said yes to it and said no to it and done neither, and that every reason he had come to in about ninety mornings was a coat full of other people's trouble. ON THE NINETY-FOURTH MORNING HE SAID OUT LOUD, AT ABOUT TEN PAST EIGHT IN THE EVENING, THAT HE WAS GOING TWO MILES UP THAT LANE AND THEN NINE TO FIND OUT WHETHER A WOMAN ON A WORKING LIST HAD BEEN TOLD THAT HER NAME WAS ON A PAGE, THAT HE WAS GOING TO ASK HER AND NOT A WOMAN WITH A KEY AND NOT THAT YARD, THAT HE WAS NOT GOING TO TELL THE YARD WHAT SHE WOULD SAY OR WHAT SHE IS OWED, AND THAT ANYBODY WHO WANTED TO COME HAD ABOUT A MINUTE. **HE WENT DOWN THE LANE WITH NOTHING IN EITHER POCKET AND THIS MANUSCRIPT DOES NOT SAY WHAT HE FOUND. HE IS NOT A BEARER, IS NOT IN THE COLUMN, IS NOT A PARTY TO THE RECORD, HAS ASKED FOR NOTHING, AND HAS NOW FOR THE FIRST TIME IN ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTEEN MORNINGS SAID WHAT HE IS GOING TO DO BEFORE HE DID IT.**
- **MARA QUILL. THIRTY-FOUR, AND THE FIGURE NINETEEN IS HER AGE ON THE FIRST ENTRY OF A BOOK THAT HAS BEEN OPEN FOR FIFTEEN YEARS. THE CLERK OF THAT LOT BOOK.** In this block **she said out loud that she is a person who enters and has entered about nine thousand things in that book and has never once found out one; she entered seven lines on the page at the back with nothing ruled on it, read every one of them out loud before she wrote it, and entered the fact and not the meaning every time; on the eighty-ninth morning she entered the fact that a woman had been named in a record held elsewhere and refused to enter the effect of that on the person named, on the ground that she is not that person's clerk and would not be one; and then she said the harder half, that she is that person's clerk in a way she did not agree to and cannot get out of, and that a person got named in a record this morning because we treated two jobs as one job. On the ninety-third morning she said she was not going to say no before the man at the wall had answered her, and gave the reason, that she had done that to a man on the eighty-second morning and had had nine days of it. SHE IS A PARTY TO THIS DISTRICT'S HOLDING OF A MATTER AND NOT TO THE RECORD ITSELF, AND SHE SAID SO OUT LOUD IN A YARD.**
- **SABRA HOLT. FIFTY-TWO. AN APPOINTED ADMINISTRATOR OF NINETEEN YEARS, HOLDING THE THIRD OF THE NINE OLDEST KEYS. NAMED ON A PAGE AT HER OWN CHOICE ON THE EIGHTY-FIRST MORNING OF VOLUME 16.** In this block **she read the first page in full at about ten on the eighty-sixth morning, twice, including the eleven words of form, and refused to explain them; she picked the strip of paper off the boards and put this district in the position of being the only holder in the basin anybody can be asked about, and said in the yard why, which is that she had held a key for nineteen years and had never used it on a piece of paper and had been saving it for one, and that the reason she used it this morning was a bad one and she would have used a better one if there was a better one; on the ninety-first morning the second page's fourth line gave her own name as the person who filed the application in respect of this district, in a hand that is not hers, and she read it aloud and said one line and stopped; and on the ninety-second morning she said out loud, in the sun, that nobody in this basin knows what the figure four hundred and eleven is the age of, that it is not the age of the sheet and not the age of a print and not the age of a body, and that she was putting it in a book at her own asking and nobody else's. SHE IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THE BASIN WHO HAS EVER BEEN ABLE TO PUT A KEY ON A PIECE OF PAPER, AND SHE USED IT ONCE, AND IT COST HER SOMETHING, AND THE REASON WAS NOT A GOOD ONE.**
- **IVEN TALLOW. SIXTY-ONE, THE CUSTODIAN, HOLDER OF THE FOUNDER'S CLAUSE FOR SEVENTEEN YEARS, THIRTY AFternoons ON THE STONES BY THE END OF THIS BLOCK.** In this block **he said out loud in a yard, unasked, that he put the clause to that use on the eighty-fifth morning between about eight and about nine from a room nine miles up that hill, that the four figures on that wall are what came of it, and that he said nothing to anybody about it on three mornings; he gave the reason, that a settlement at the eleventh place on the Salt Verge has been coming apart for about four days and that the thing that holds a settlement together is somebody being answerable for it; he said the word Custodian out loud in that yard for the first time; he told the yard it had about four days before anybody nine miles up that hill had to ask anybody anything and that he was not going to help; and on the ninety-first morning he said, unasked, that he had been going to end the clause for about four days and had not and was not going to, and that he had said the same thing on the eighty-fourth morning and it had not got less true the second time, which he did not expect and which he said is the only thing he has learned this week. THE CONDITION ON THE SEAT IS STILL UNNAMED AND WAS NOT NAMED ON ANY OF THE TEN MORNINGS, AND SABRA HOLT DID NOT ASK HIM TWICE.**
- **NELL CRAY. ABOUT FIFTY, THE SIXTH OF THE NINE COPPERS, ON WORKING LIST FORTY-ONE IN A CORRIDOR NINE MILES UP THAT HILL FOR NINE YEARS, NEVER ASKED ANYTHING BY ANYBODY IN THIS DISTRICT.** In this block **she was named in a permanent record without being asked, on a sheet that reached the boards of the second table at about a quarter to eleven on the eighty-ninth morning, and she was not in that yard, and the clerk entered the fact of the naming and refused to enter the effect of it on her. SHE HAS NOT BEEN TOLD. ADRIAN VALE WENT OUT OF THE YARD ON THE NINETY-FOURTH MORNING TO ASK HER AND THIS MANUSCRIPT DOES NOT SAY HE GOT THERE.**
- **THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX. UNNAMED. HAS READ FOUR FIGURES OFF A WALL TWICE A MORNING AND HIS OWN STANDING AT THAT WALL IS FIVE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-SIX MORNINGS ON THE EIGHTY-FIFTH MORNING AFTER THE THIRTIETH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH AND FIVE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FIVE ON THE NINETY-FOURTH. THE WALL IS NINE YEARS OLD AND HIS TENURE IS COUNTED IN MORNINGS AND NOT IN YEARS.** In this block **he read four figures off that wall at about a quarter to eight on the eighty-fifth morning that he had never read off it, said so out loud in about nineteen people's hearing, said the four were not changed, said he knows the old four the way he knows the number of steps on that bank, and said he was not going to say which one of the new four he could not have said a week ago. HE SAID THE OLD FOUR OUT LOUD AT ABOUT ELEVEN ON THE EIGHTY-SEVENTH MORNING AND A CLERK WROTE THEM ON A PAGE WITH NOTHING RULED ON IT, AND HE WAS ASKED FOR THE THIRD TIME IN THREE MORNINGS TO HURRY AND SAID NO OUT LOUD. ON THE NINETY-THIRD MORNING HE WAS ASKED TO SAY THE OLD FOUR INTO THE COLUMN AND SAID NO AND GAVE THE REASON OUT LOUD IN FULL: that a column is for somebody being asked, that he has said them to about four thousand people in five hundred and thirty-four mornings and not one of them ever asked him for one, and that the day he goes into that column the four belong to whoever holds the book and not to me. HE WAS ENTERED ON NOTHING. HE IS THE ONLY PERSON WHO CAN PROVE WHAT THE FIGURES ON THAT WALL WERE, HE IS ON NO PAGE, AND HE HAS JUST REFUSED THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THAT WOULD MAKE HIM FINDABLE.**
- **THE MAN AT THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE. ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN, AT THAT TABLE FOR FIFTEEN YEARS, WROTE THE FIFTH LINE AT ABOUT TWENTY-TWO, UNNAMED AND ON NO PAGE.** In this block **he said out loud what the fifth line was for, having refused to be asked the question a woman with a key put and answered a different one instead: he thought it was for stopping a person, it has cost him the ability to want anything since the winter after he wrote it, and he would rather that line were the end of the book than have anybody put a name in a column for him. He said a rule nobody can name had never once been tested because there had never been anything in that yard worth testing it on, and that in about four years two of the district's refusals would be put side by side and it would be right and about the wrong two people. He asked the man of fifty-six for the column, out loud, standing up, in the light, in about nineteen people's hearing, on his own asking, and was refused. HE HAS SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO ASK ADRIAN VALE WHAT THE FENCING WIRE IS FOR, HE WATCHED HIM GO DOWN THE LANE AT ABOUT HALF PAST SIX ON THE NINETY-FOURTH MORNING AND DID NOT ASK, AND HE HAS COME WITHIN ABOUT FOUR INCHES OF ASKING HIM TWICE IN FOUR MORNINGS AND TURNED ROUND BOTH TIMES.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR. UNNAMED. ABOUT FIFTY-NINE OR SIXTY-FOUR AND HAS SAID SO AND HAS NEVER SAID WHICH. HIS FOUR HUNDRED AND FORTIETH NIGHT ON THE EIGHTY-FIFTH MORNING AND HIS FOUR HUNDRED AND FORTY-NINTH ON THE NINETY-FOURTH, HAVING SLEPT ON ONE FEWER THAN THE NIGHT HE IS ON. ON THAT WALL FOR NINE YEARS AND OUT ON THAT ROAD AT ABOUT HALF PAST FOUR EVERY MORNING FOR NINE YEARS, AND ASKED THREE QUESTIONS IN HIS LIFE.** In this block **he found eleven feet of fencing wire at a culvert four miles down that lane at about a quarter past five on the ninetieth morning, did not take it down, did not go round it, and said four sentences out loud to nobody because there was nobody within about four miles of him; he said he was not going to be the man who takes it down and that there was a thing about it he was not going to explain to anybody in a yard in about four mornings; and on the ninety-fourth morning he came up the eleven feet at about a quarter past six with the wire in his hand, which is the first time in nine years he has brought anything up that lane, put it on the second table, and said three things, of which the second was that he had said he would not take it down and had been going to and had come back early, and whose reason is that a thing at a place where a thing cannot be refused is not a thing anybody put there, it is a thing the place has. HE GAVE UP THE ONLY THING HE HAD SAID HE WAS GOING TO DO. HE IS STILL UNNAMED.**
- **ORREN PIKE. SEVENTEEN, THE YARD SAYS ABOUT NINETEEN, KEEPING HIS OWN SHEET FOR FOUR YEARS AND NOT NINE.** In this block **he said twenty-four out loud before the clerk's hand moved on the eighty-fifth morning and twenty-five on the ninety-first, in the order the page arrives and not before it; he wrote a woman's name in the box on the back of his own sheet on the eighty-ninth morning and then picked his pencil up and left the name where it was, because the box is for mornings and a name is a person; he said out loud that he had left out the whole of a man's reason three times in that week and was not going to stop, and offered to have that entered in the box and not in the columns, because a hole is a fact about a morning and not a sentence about a person; and he wrote four figures and a count of nineteen people in the box on the ninety-second and did not join them up. HE HAS NOW WRITTEN A NAME DOWN AND PUT HIS PENCIL DOWN.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THE CART. UNNAMED. HAD SAID NOTHING IN THAT YARD FOR NINE MORNINGS.** In this block **he spoke on the eighty-ninth morning, and the whole of what he said was that a record is not a thing that hides, it is a thing that is already true by the time you get to it, and that a person named in a record has been in it for about four days by the time anybody finds out and the four days will be the reason nobody can do anything about it. HE SPOKE TWICE IN THE EIGHTY-NINTH MORNING AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME IN NINE MORNINGS THAT HE HAS SAID A WHOLE SENTENCE IN THAT YARD.**
- **MAVIS DORR. UNNAMED.** In this block **she put the second question the man at the end of the second table answered, which was what he thought the fifth line was for when he wrote it, and said she had been carrying it since the seventy-first morning and that if she did not put it down this morning she was going to be the woman who asked it once. SHE TOLD HIM AT THE END OF THE EIGHTY-NINTH MORNING THAT HE WAS GOING TO BE THE MAN WHO WAS TALKING ABOUT CLERKS.**
- **THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS A SCALE. UNNAMED. NINE YEARS ON HER OWN STEP ABOUT FORTY FEET OFF THE FOOT OF THAT BANK.** In this block **she said it moved twice in nine mornings and both times because somebody came down that hill with a page in her hand, and that it had not moved once for a thing any of them did; and on the ninety-first morning she said it had moved three times in sixteen mornings, on the eighty-first, the eighty-fifth and a minute before she spoke, and that all three times it moved because a person came down that hill with a page in her hand and a boy got to the figure before a clerk did. SHE IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THAT YARD WHO CAN TELL YOU THAT TWO MOVES ARE THE SAME KIND OF THING.**
- **THE TURNER. ABOUT FORTY-ONE, UNNAMED, AND NOT ONE OF ANYBODY'S.** In this block **he came up the eleven feet for the first time in about nine years, at about eleven on the ninety-second morning, and asked to be allowed to say the thing he had come up for. HE HAD READ FOUR FIGURES OFF A COPY OF THAT WALL AT THE FAR END OF THAT LANE FOR ABOUT SIX YEARS WITH NOBODY IN THE LANE, HE ASKED FOR THEM ON THE SEVENTY-SEVENTH MORNING AND WAS TOLD IT WAS NOT TO HAPPEN TWICE, HE KNEW ON THE SPOT ON THE EIGHTY-FIFTH MORNING THAT THE FOUR ON THE WALL WERE NOT HIS, HE ASKED THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX TO READ THEM OUT LOUD IN THAT YARD WITHOUT BEING ASKED, AND HE SAID HE WAS RIGHT FOR SIX YEARS AND WOULD LIKE IT ENTERED THAT HE WAS RIGHT AND KNEW IT COULD NOT BE. HE IS STILL UNNAMED AND HAS NO FIGURE OF HIS OWN.**
- **AND THE THREE WHO ARE STILL UNNAMED AND MUST STAY SO: THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO WHO PUTS TABLES UP, AND THE ROAD KEEPER WHO KEEPS EVERY ROAD IN THIS BASIN.** THE MAN WHO PUTS TABLES UP TOOK A STONE OFF THE SECOND TABLE ON THE SEVENTY-FIFTH MORNING OF VOLUME 16, CARRIED IT DOWN THE BANK INSIDE HIS COAT, AND REFUSED TO SAY WHERE HE PUT IT. **HE SAID NOTHING ABOUT IT ON ANY MORNING OF THIS BLOCK AND NOBODY ASKED HIM. THE NEAR RAIL IS STILL NOT TO BE THANKED, ON TEN OF TEN. THE STONE IS STILL IN A COAT AGAINST A MAN'S RIBS AND THE HOLLOW IN IT STILL HAS A THUMB WORN IN IT THAT NOBODY MADE.**

---

## VOLUME 17, BLOCK 0002 — CHARACTER STATE AT CHAPTER 820

- **ADRIAN VALE, THIRTY-EIGHT. NO BED, NO WAGE, NO OFFICE, NONE ASKED FOR.** **HIS OWN MORNINGS IN THAT YARD ARE ONE HUNDRED AND SIXTEEN AND RISE ONE A MORNING, THE COUNTING IS DONE BY A BOY OF SEVENTEEN, AND IT IS PRINTED ON SIX OF THE TEN MORNINGS OF THIS BLOCK AND ON NO OTHERS.** IN THIS BLOCK **HE CROSSED THE YARD AT ABOUT TEN PAST FIVE ON THE NINETY-FIFTH MORNING, THE FIRST MORNING OF THE BLOCK, STOOD AT THE TOP OF IT FOR ABOUT AS LONG AS IT TAKES A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX TO SAY FOUR FIGURES OFF A WALL AND WENT STRAIGHT BACK DOWN THE LANE WITHOUT STOPPING AT THE BOARDS, AND WAS IN THAT YARD AGAIN AT ABOUT NINE ON THE NINETY-SIXTH AND ON EVERY MORNING AFTER THAT.** HE SAID WHAT THIS DISTRICT HAS GOT AT THE BOARDS ON `c = 14` AND REFUSED TO CALL IT AN OFFER. HE STOOD AT THE BOARDS AND SAID NOTHING AT ALL ON `c = 13` AND ON `c = 19`. HE TOLD THE TURNER ON THE HUNDREDTH MORNING NOT TO GO AND BE ASKED AGAIN BUT TO COME UP THAT BANK AND TELL THE YARD WHATEVER THE ANSWER TURNED OUT TO BE. **HE CAME BACK TO A SPEAKING POSITION AT ABOUT ONE ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND FOURTH MORNING AND SAID FOUR THINGS: THAT NELL CRAY HAS NOT BEEN TOLD; THAT SHE WAS NOT FINE; THAT SHE DID NOT WANT TO BE TOLD, BECAUSE BEING TOLD WOULD MAKE IT SO THAT SOMEBODY WOULD HAVE TO DO SOMETHING ABOUT IT, AND IF IT WAS NEVER TOLD THEN IT WAS A PAGE AND IF IT WAS TOLD THEN IT WAS A THING THAT HAPPENED TO HER; AND THAT SHE ASKED HIM WHAT THE DISTRICT HAS GOT, IN ABOUT NINE WORDS, IN A CORRIDOR, AT ABOUT ELEVEN AT NIGHT, AND HE HAS NOT GOT AN ANSWER AND IS NOT GOING TO SAY THOSE NINE WORDS AT THOSE BOARDS BECAUSE SHE IS NINE MILES OFF AND IT IS HERS.** HE HAS NOTHING IN EITHER POCKET AND HAS HAD NOTHING IN EITHER POCKET FOR THE WHOLE OF THIS BLOCK.
- **ORREN PIKE, SEVENTEEN, AND THE BOY. HIS SHEET IS FOUR YEARS OLD. ITS BOX IS FOR MORNINGS ON WHICH A THING WAS SAID OUT LOUD IN THAT YARD AND WAS NOT ENTERED ANYWHERE.** IN THIS BLOCK **HE WROTE A FIGURE AND DID NOT READ IT OUT ON `c = 11` AND SAID SO OUT LOUD FIRST; HE DID NOT SAY THE LANE COUNT AT ALL ON `c = 13` AND GAVE THE REASON OUT LOUD, THAT A COUNT WHICH MOVES EVERY TIME A PAGE REACHES THOSE BOARDS WILL IN ABOUT FOUR YEARS BE A COUNT OF HOW MANY TIMES THIS YARD WAS HANDED SOMETHING, WHICH IS A NUMBER ABOUT US AND NOT ABOUT A PERSON; HE WROTE THE HUNDREDTH MORNING IN THE BOX AND READ IT OUT LOUD TO THE BOARDS, INCLUDING THE SENTENCE *IF THIS SHEET IS LOST THEN THE HUNDREDTH MORNING NEVER HAPPENED AND THERE ARE ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE WHO WILL SAY IT DID*, AND ABOUT NINE PEOPLE HEARD IT AND NOBODY ANSWERED HIM; AND ON `c = 20` HE DECLINED TO WRITE THE MORNING DOWN AT ALL AND SAID THAT A SHEET HE HAS KEPT FOR FOUR YEARS IS THE SAME SHAPE AS A COLUMN WITH A HEADING OVER IT, ONLY SMALLER AND WITH HIM IN IT, AND THAT IF THAT YARD WANTS A RECORD OF THE ONE HUNDRED AND FOURTH MORNING IT WILL HAVE TO BE SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT HIM, AND THERE IS NOT ANYBODY.** HE WROTE A WOMAN'S NAME IN THE BOX ON `c = 5` OF THE LAST BLOCK AND PUT HIS PENCIL DOWN, AND **HE HAS SAID OUT LOUD IN THIS BLOCK THAT HE IS GOING TO BE THE INSTRUMENT FOR NOTHING.** THE QUESTION HE RAISED ON THE EIGHTY-EIGHTH MORNING, THAT HE LEAVES THE WHOLE OF A MAN'S REASON OUT OF HIS OWN SHEET ON PURPOSE, WAS TAKEN SERIOUSLY BY THE CLERK AND BY THE MAN AT THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE IN THIS BLOCK AND NEITHER OF THEM ANSWERED IT.
- **MARA QUILL, THIRTY-FOUR, FIFTEEN YEARS AS CLERK OF THE LOT BOOK, NINETEEN LINES ON A PAGE AND A COLUMN WITH ONE NAME IN IT.** IN THIS BLOCK **SHE ENTERED SEVEN LINES ACROSS SIX OF THE TEN MORNINGS, WHICH IS ONE FEWER LINE THAN THE LAST BLOCK ACROSS TEN, AND ON `c = 11` SHE ENTERED NOTHING AT ALL AND GAVE THE REASON OUT LOUD THAT A ROAD KEEPER STOPPING IS NOT A THING THAT HAPPENED IN THIS YARD; AND ON `c = 15` SHE ENTERED NOTHING AND DID NOT SAY WHY; AND ON `c = 17` SHE WROTE ONE LINE AND DID NOT READ IT OUT, AND WHEN A BOY OF SEVENTEEN ASKED HER WHY IN A YARD SHE SAID SHE WAS NOT GOING TO ANSWER THAT ONE IN A YARD OR IN A BOOK, THAT SHE WAS LEAVING THE LINE WHERE IT WAS AND NOT TAKING IT BACK, AND THAT IN ABOUT FOUR DAYS THERE IS GOING TO BE A PAGE IN THIS DISTRICT WITH ABOUT TWENTY-EIGHT LINES ON IT AND ABOUT FOUR OF THOSE LINES ARE GOING TO BE ONES NOBODY HEARD HER READ AND THERE IS NO WAY ON THIS EARTH FOR ANYBODY TO WORK OUT WHICH FOUR.** **ON THE HUNDREDTH MORNING SHE LOOKED AT TWO SPACES ON A FORM AND WROTE NOTHING IN EITHER AND SAID OUT LOAD THAT THERE WAS NOWHERE TO ENTER IT, BECAUSE EVERY ONE OF THE TWENTY-ONE LINES ON THAT PAGE IS A THING THAT HAPPENED IN THIS YARD, AND ONE LINE ABOUT THIS MORNING WOULD HAVE GIVEN THE ROOM NINE MILES UP THAT HILL A FORM WITH NOTHING IN IT AND THIS YARD A PAGE SAYING IT HAD A MORNING, AND THE TWO PUT SIDE BY SIDE WOULD SAY THAT THIS DISTRICT DECLINED, AND THIS DISTRICT WAS NOT ASKED.** SHE REPORTED OUT LOAD THAT THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX SAID A COLUMN IS FOR SOMEBODY BEING ASKED AND SHE WAS ENTERING THE FACT AND NOT THE MEANING. SHE IS NOT A PARTY TO THE RECORD AND THE DISTRICT IS, AND SHE SAID SO HERSELF IN THE LAST BLOCK AND THIS BLOCK DID NOT UNSAY IT.
- **SABRA HOLT, FIFTY-TWO, AN APPOINTED ADMINISTRATOR OF NINETEEN YEARS, THE THIRD OF THE NINE OLDEST KEYS.** IN THIS BLOCK **SHE WAS ASKED IN A YARD, IN ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE'S HEARING, WHAT SHE DID A KEY FOR, BY A WOMAN WHO HAD DECIDED IN ADVANCE NOT TO ASK WHETHER SHE WAS ALL RIGHT AND NOT TO TELL HER AFTERWARDS THAT SHE WAS BRAVE. SHE PUT THE KEY ON THE BOARDS OF THE SECOND TABLE AT ABOUT ELEVEN, SAID THAT SHE HAD BEEN AN APPOINTED ADMINISTRATOR FOR NINETEEN YEARS AND HAD NEVER ONCE BEEN ASKED WHAT ANY OF IT WAS FOR, AND THAT SHE HAD PUT A KEY NEXT TO A PIECE OF PAPER BECAUSE SHE WANTED TO FIND OUT WHETHER ANYBODY IN THIS BASIN WOULD COME AND ASK HER WHAT SHE HAD DONE AND WHY. ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE CAME DOWN THAT LANE IN ABOUT SIXTEEN DAYS, ABOUT NINE OF THEM CAME TO FIND OUT WHAT A RECORD WAS, EVERY SINGLE ONE OF THEM ASKED HER WHAT WAS IN THE PAPER, AND NOT ONE ASKED HER WHY. SHE SAID OUT LOUD THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO BE COMFORTED IN THAT YARD OR OUTSIDE IT AND THAT A DIFFICULT THING IS A THING THAT WAS DIFFICULT AND NOT A THING THAT WAS RIGHT, AND THAT NOT ONE OF THE OTHER EIGHT KEYS HAS EVER BEEN ASKED A QUESTION, AND THAT SHE WOULD HAVE LIKED ONE OF THEM TO HAVE BEEN ASKED A QUESTION IN ABOUT FOUR YEARS.** SHE WAS ASKED A SECOND, DIFFERENT QUESTION BY A MAN AT THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE AND PERMITTED IT AND ANSWERED IT: **THE OTHER EIGHT ARE A ROOM WITH A TABLE IN IT ON THE FAR SIDE OF THAT HILL WITH NOBODY SITTING IN IT, ABOUT FOUR WOMEN WHO ARE NOT OLDER THAN HER AND TWO WHO ARE, AND ONE KEY ON A CHAIN IN A ROOM NINE MILES UP THAT HILL BELONGING TO A WOMAN DEAD FOR ABOUT SIX YEARS, AND THE ROOM IS NOT LOCKED.** SHE HAS NOT BEEN IN THAT ROOM SINCE THE MORNING SHE USED THE KEY.
- **IVEN TALLOW, SIXTY-ONE, SEVENTEEN YEARS AS HOLDER OF THE FOUNDER'S CLAUSE, AND HE SAID THE WORD CUSTODIAN OUT LOUD IN THIS YARD IN THE LAST BLOCK.** IN THIS BLOCK **HE CAME UP THE ELEVEN FEET FOR THE FIRST TIME IN NINE YEARS, ON THE NINETY-NINTH MORNING, AFTER FORTY-FOUR AFTERNOONS ON SOME STONES FOUR FEET OFF A MAN WHO SAYS GOOD AFTERNOON. HE SAID HE HAD BEEN OFFERED A WAY OUT OF THE CLAUSE, THAT IT WAS THE THIRD OF THE THREE WAYS AND WAS FOR HIM AND NOT FOR THE TURNER, AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO TAKE IT, AND THAT THIS IS THE THIRD TIME HE HAS SAID SO IN A YARD AND IT HAS GOT LESS TRUE. HE SAID THE SETTLEMENT AT THE ELEVENTH PLACE ON THE SALT VERGE IS NOT COMING APART, HAS WATER IN THE CHANNEL AND A ROOF ON TWO OF THE HUTS, AND THAT THE PEOPLE THERE ARE NOT LEAVING, AND THAT HE HAS NEVER MET ONE OF THEM, HAS NEVER BEEN TO THAT PLACE, AND CANNOT TELL THAT YARD ONE THING ABOUT ANY OF IT THAT HE HAS SEEN.** **A WOMAN OF FIFTY-TWO ASKED HIM ONCE, OUT LOUD, IN ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE'S HEARING, WHAT THE CONDITION ON THE SEAT IS, AND HE SAID NO. THE CONDITION IS STILL UNNAMED.** HE HAS NOT ENDED THE CLAUSE AND HAS NOT RESOLVED ANYTHING. ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND SECOND MORNING THERE WAS NOBODY ON THE STONES FOUR FEET OFF THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR AT HALF PAST THREE, AND HE DID NOT SAY GOOD AFTERNOON, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM WHY.
- **THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX. UNNAMED. HIS OWN STANDING AT THAT WALL IS COUNTED IN MORNINGS AND IS FIVE HUNDRED AND FORTY-FIVE ON THE LAST MORNING OF THIS BLOCK, AND THE WALL IS NINE YEARS OLD, AND HE HAS BEEN WRITTEN AS HAVING STOOD AT IT FOR NINE YEARS IN DRAFTS THAT WERE REPAIRED IN EVERY BLOCK OF THIS VOLUME.** IN THIS BLOCK **HE SAID THE FOUR OFF THAT WALL THREE TIMES ON ONE MORNING, AT ABOUT A QUARTER TO EIGHT, AT ABOUT HALF PAST THREE AT THE FOOT OF THE WALL WITH A MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR, AND AT ABOUT FOUR, AND GOT ALL FOUR EVERY TIME. HE SAID OUT LOUD AT ABOUT TWO ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND THIRD MORNING THAT HE WANTED THE THIRD WAY, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE AND BEFORE HIS REASONS, AND THEN SAID WHY HE WAS NOT GOING TO TAKE IT: BECAUSE ON THE EIGHTY-FIFTH MORNING THOSE FOUR CHANGED AND THE ONLY REASON ANYBODY CAN PROVE IT IS THAT A RULE IN THIS YARD WILL NOT LET A FIGURE MOVE, AND ONE MORNING A YEAR WHEN A FIGURE MOVES IS THAT RULE WITH A HOLE IN IT THE SHAPE OF A MORNING, AND THE HOLE IS IN THE SAME WALL. HE SAID THAT HE HAS SAID NO OUT LOUD TWICE IN ABOUT TEN DAYS AND GIVEN A REASON FOR BOTH, AND THAT THE MAN AT THE END OF THAT TABLE HAS SAID NO ON MORE MORNINGS THIS WEEK AND HAS NOT GIVEN A REASON FOR ONE OF THEM.** HE HAS SAID NO TWICE IN THIS BLOCK AND HAS BEEN ASKED FOR A COLUMN TWICE.
- **THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS A SCALE. UNNAMED. NINE YEARS ON HER OWN STEP ABOUT FORTY FEET OFF THE FOOT OF THAT BANK.** IN THIS BLOCK **SHE SAID THE FIGURE OUT LOUD FIRST ON `c = 13` AND IT WAS THE WRONG FIGURE FOR THE WRONG REASON AND NOBODY SAID SO; SHE SAID THAT A FIGURE THAT DOES NOT GET SAID IS NOT THE SAME AS A FIGURE THAT DOES NOT MOVE, AND THAT THE SECOND ONE IS A PERSON AND THE FIRST ONE IS A DECISION, AND THAT SHE HAD BEEN WAITING NINE YEARS FOR ONE OF THEM; SHE SAID ON `c = 17` THAT A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE HAD GIVEN THAT FIGURE A PERSON AND THAT FROM THAT MORNING THE WALL HAS A READER IN IT AND THE READER IS NOT THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX; AND SHE SAID ON `c = 19` THAT THERE IS NO WAY ON A BEAM TO TELL A HEAVY THING THAT CAME BY ITSELF FROM A HEAVY THING SOMEBODY BROUGHT YOU, AND THAT SHE HAD BEEN PROVED RIGHT TWICE THAT MORNING BY TWO MEN WHO HAD NEVER SPOKEN TO EACH OTHER AND ELEVEN FEET APART. SHE IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THAT YARD WHO CAN TELL YOU THAT TWO MOVES ARE THE SAME KIND OF THING.**
- **THE TURNER. ABOUT FORTY-ONE, UNNAMED, AND NOT ONE OF ANYBODY'S. HE HAS BEEN AT THE END OF THAT LANE FOR NINE YEARS AND HAS BEEN READING FOUR FIGURES OFF A COPY OF THAT WALL FOR SIX OF THEM WITH NOBODY IN THE LANE.** IN THIS BLOCK **HE CAME UP THE ELEVEN FEET AT ABOUT ONE ON `c = 12`, THREE MORNINGS LATER THAN HE HAD SAID HE WOULD COME, HAVING WALKED FOUR MILES FURTHER THAN THE NINTH MILE AND BACK, AND SAID HE HAD BEEN ASKED IN A ROOM NINE MILES OFF TO BE THE PERSON A NUMBER IS CHECKED AGAINST AND HAD NOT ANSWERED. HE NAMED THE FIRST OF THREE WAYS. HE BROUGHT A PAGE UP THAT LANE ON `c = 13` AND READ IT OUT AT THE BOARDS TWICE. HE BROUGHT A FORM UP ON `c = 16` AND PUT IT ON THE BOARDS AND SAID OUT LOAD THAT NOBODY IN THAT YARD HAD ASKED HIM WHETHER HE WAS GOING TO PUT HIS NAME IN IT, AND THAT A NAME WAS ALREADY IN A ROOM IN A HAND THAT IS NOT HIS. HE SAID YES ON `c = 17` IN ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE'S HEARING AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST ELEVEN, BEFORE HE ANSWERED IN THE ROOM.** HE HAS NOT BEGUN, THE MORNING HAS NOT BEEN NAMED, HE WENT DOWN THAT LANE AT ABOUT TEN TO FOUR ON `c = 20` TO BEGIN, AND HE SAID OUT LOAD THAT IT WILL BE WRONG WITHIN ABOUT FOUR YEARS AND THAT HE SAID THAT. **HE IS UNNAMED, HE IS ON NO PAGE, AND THE MAN AT THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE SAID OUT LOAD THAT HE IS ONE OF THE ONLY TWO PEOPLE A HEADING SAYING *WHOEVER* COULD HAVE COME THROUGH.**
- **MAVIS DORR. UNNAMED.** IN THIS BLOCK **SHE ASKED SABRA HOLT WHAT SHE HAD DONE A KEY FOR, HAVING DECIDED IN ADVANCE NOT TO ASK WHETHER SHE WAS ALL RIGHT, AND SHE ASKED THE TURNER THE QUESTION SHE HAD BEEN CARRYING SINCE THE SEVENTY-FIRST MORNING. SHE TOLD A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE TO STOP TELLING HIM WHAT HE DID NOT KNOW ON THE MORNING HE HAD SAID YES, AND SAID SHE WAS SORRY FOUR WORDS TOO MANY AND THAT SHE DID NOT NEED TO USE HER QUESTION ON HIM.**
- **THE ROAD KEEPER. ABOUT FORTY-THREE, UNNAMED, KEEPS EVERY ROAD IN THIS BASIN AND DOES NOT KEEP RELATIONS.** IN THIS BLOCK **HE CAME UP THAT LANE AND STOPPED AT THE TOP OF THE BANK TWICE IN NINE YEARS, ON `c = 11` AND `c = 19`. HE SAID HE HAD BEEN ASKED FOUR TIMES IN FOUR MORNINGS FOR THE NAME OF A PERSON IN THIS DISTRICT, OF WHICH TWO REFUSALS WERE HIS AND TWO WERE NOT, AND THAT ON THE FIFTH MORNING THEY STOPPED ASKING HIM AND ASKED THE WOMAN WHO WORKS THE TURN AT THE ELEVENTH PLACE ON THE SALT VERGE INSTEAD. HE SAID HE WOULD CARRY THE NEWS DOWN ABOUT HALF PAST FOUR WHETHER THIS DISTRICT PRODUCED A PERSON OR NOT, AND ON `c = 19` HE CARRIED IT DOWN AND SAID THAT FROM A ROOM NINE MILES UP THAT HILL ALL THREE ANSWERS ARE A PIECE OF PAPER WITH A SPACE ON IT, AND THAT NOBODY UP THERE CAN TELL A PLACE THAT WROTE A SENTENCE FROM A PLACE WHERE THE SENTENCE CANNOT BE GOT UP TO, AND THAT THIS DISTRICT DID NOT PRODUCE A PERSON AND THE REASON IT DID NOT IS NOT A REASON ANYBODY CAN WRITE DOWN.** HE SAW ADRIAN VALE CROSS THE YARD AT ABOUT TEN PAST FIVE ON THE NINETY-FIFTH MORNING AND TOLD THE BOY OF SEVENTEEN IN PRIVATE.
- **THE MAN AT THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE. ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN, UNNAMED, FIFTEEN YEARS AT THAT TABLE, WROTE HIS LINE AT ABOUT TWENTY-TWO, AND HE HAS ABOUT THREE YEARS AND FOUR MONTHS OF THE FOUR YEARS HE GAVE HIMSELF.** IN THIS BLOCK **HE ASKED, IN A YARD, IN ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE'S HEARING, WHAT THIS DISTRICT WOULD OFFER FOR THE RIGHT TO ADMINISTER THE TALLY, WHICH HE SAID WAS THE FIRST QUESTION HE HAD EVER PUT IN THAT YARD WHERE THE ANSWER WAS ABOUT THE DISTRICT AND NOT ABOUT A PERSON. HE NAMED THE THIRD OF THE THREE WAYS OUT LOUD AND SAID HE HAD HAD IT FOR SEVEN MORNINGS AND SAID NOTHING, AND THAT THIS WAS THE WORST THING HE HAD DONE IN THE BLOCK. HE SAID OUT LOUD ON THE HUNDREDTH MORNING THAT SOMEBODY NINE MILES UP THAT HILL KNOWS THE HEADING OVER A COLUMN IN THIS DISTRICT, NAMED THE TURNER AND SABRA HOLT AS THE ONLY TWO PEOPLE IT COULD HAVE COME THROUGH, AND DID NOT NAME HIMSELF. HE SAID OUT LOAD ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND THIRD MORNING THAT HE HAS BEEN ASKED THREE TIMES IN NINE YEARS TO BE THE PERSON, SAID NO TWICE AND NOTHING ONCE, AND THAT THE NOTHING IS IN A ROOM NINE MILES UP THAT HILL WITH HIS NAME NOWHERE ON IT, AND SAID THE PRICE OF SAYING IT OUT LOUD, WHICH IS THAT IN ABOUT FOUR YEARS A PERSON CAN SAY *ASK HIM, HE WAS IN THE ROOM*.** **ON THE LAST MORNING OF THE BLOCK HE ASKED OUT LOUD, IN THAT YARD, TO COME UP THE LANE WITH ADRIAN VALE, WHICH IS THE FIRST TIME IN THIS RUN OF MORNINGS THAT ANYBODY HAS ASKED TO GO ANYWHERE, AND WAS TOLD HE COULD NOT BECAUSE HE IS NOW A PERSON WITH A HEADING OVER HIM.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR. UNNAMED. HE IS FIFTY-NINE OR SIXTY-FOUR AND HAS SAID SO AND HAS NEVER SAID WHICH. HE WALKS NINE MILES OF ROAD EVERY MORNING AT ABOUT HALF PAST FOUR AND HAS MISSED NONE IN NINE YEARS. IN THIS BLOCK HE BROUGHT THE WIRE DOWN FROM THE YARD, AND SAID ONE SENTENCE AT THE FOOT OF A WALL TO THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX, AND NOBODY WITHIN ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED YARDS HEARD IT.**
- **NELL CRAY. ABOUT FIFTY. HOLDS THE SIXTH OF THE NINE COPPERS. ON WORKING LIST FORTY-ONE IN A CORRIDOR NINE MILES UP THAT HILL FOR NINE YEARS. SHE WAS NAMED IN A PERMANENT RECORD ON THE EIGHTY-NINTH MORNING IN A HAND THAT IS NOT HERS AND WAS NOT ASKED. SHE HAS NOW BEEN TOLD, BY A MAN OF THIRTY-EIGHT, IN A CORRIDOR, AT ABOUT ELEVEN AT NIGHT, THAT HER NAME IS ON A PAGE, AND SHE WAS NOT FINE, AND SHE DID NOT WANT TO BE TOLD, AND SHE ASKED HIM WHAT THE DISTRICT HAS GOT, AND HE HAS NOT ANSWERED HER AND MAY NOT ANSWER HER AT THOSE BOARDS. SHE HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN ASKED ANYTHING BY ANYBODY IN THIS DISTRICT BEFORE THAT.**
- **MAVIS DORR, THE CLERK, THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THE CART, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO WHO PUTS TABLES UP, THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY** ARE ALL UNNAMED AND ALL STILL UNNAMED, AND **NOBODY IN THIS YARD IS TO THANK THE MAN WHO FITTED THE NEAR RAIL, ON TEN OF TEN.** THE MAN WHO PUTS TABLES UP HAS A STONE IN A COAT AGAINST HIS RIBS WITH A THUMB WORN IN IT THAT NOBODY MADE AND SAID NOTHING ABOUT IT IN THIS BLOCK.

- **THE CLERK, WHO IS MARA QUILL.** IN THIS BLOCK **SHE SAID OUT LOUD ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIFTH MORNING THAT THE TWENTY-SEVENTH LINE WAS THE SECOND ONE SHE HAS WRITTEN AND NOT READ OUT, AND SAID ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTEENTH MORNING THAT SHE HAD TOLD THE YARD THREE FIGURES OUT LOUD ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND FIRST MORNING AND WAS WRONG ABOUT ALL THREE — ABOUT TWENTY-EIGHT LINES AGAINST THIRTY, ABOUT FOUR UNREAD AGAINST THREE, AND ABOUT FOUR DAYS AGAINST ABOUT TWELVE MORNINGS — AND THAT SHE WAS NOT GOING TO WRITE THE NINE SHE HAD PROMISED, AND GAVE THE REASON: A LINE NOBODY HEARD HER READ IS ONLY WORTH ANYTHING IF SOMEBODY COMES BACK AND ASKS HER ABOUT IT, AND ABOUT NINE PEOPLE HAVE NOT. THAT PROHIBITION HAS NOW BEEN BROKEN AND REPAIRED TWELVE TIMES IN THIS VOLUME.**** IN THIS BLOCK **HE CAME UP ABOUT FOUR FEET ALONG THE WALL SO THAT HE COULD BE HEARD FROM THE TOP OF THE BANK, AND AGREED OUT LOUD TO PAY SIX OF THE TOLL, AND SAID THAT HE HAS BEEN CHARGED FOR THE GROUND HE STANDS ON AND THAT IT IS THE FIRST TIME ANYTHING IN THIS BASIN HAS PUT A FIGURE ON THAT PLACE, AND SAID THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHETHER IT WAS WORTH IT; HE CAME UP THE ELEVEN FEET FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS RUN AT ABOUT HALF PAST TWO ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND NINETEENTH AND ASKED A CLERK, OUT LOUD IN ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE'S HEARING, TO ENTER THAT HE HAD GIVEN SIX AND NOT HIS REASON, AND READ THE LINE BACK TO HER HIMSELF; HE WAS TOLD AT ABOUT TEN PAST FOUR ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIRST MORNING THAT A ROOM NINE MILES UP THAT HILL HAS ASKED FOR HIS NAME AND HE REFUSED IN THE ORDINARY VOICE AND ASKED FOR THE ASKING TO BE HEARD; AND HE ASKED A MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR WHETHER HE COULD STOP.**

- **MARA QUILL, THIRTY-FOUR, FIFTEEN YEARS AS CLERK OF THE LOT BOOK, THIRTY-THREE LINES ON A PAGE AND A COLUMN WITH ONE NAME IN IT.** IN THIS BLOCK **SHE ENTERED THREE LINES ON THREE MORNINGS OUT OF TEN AND READ ALL THREE OUT BEFORE SHE WROTE THEM, AND GAVE THREE DIFFERENT REASONS AND ONE REASON AT ALL, AND SAID TWICE THAT THEY WERE THE LAST, AND WROTE ONE ANYWAY AFTER SAYING SO AND SAID THAT OUT LOUD, AND ON THE LAST MORNING OF THE VOLUME SAID THAT SHE WAS NOT ENTERING ANYTHING AND THAT THERE WAS NO REASON AND SHE WAS NOT GOING TO GIVE ONE.** SHE IS ONE WOMAN AND NOT TWO.
- **SABRA HOLT, FIFTY-TWO, AN APPOINTED ADMINISTRATOR OF NINETEEN YEARS, THE THIRD OF THE NINE OLDEST KEYS.** IN THIS BLOCK **SHE SAID *SIXTEEN OF US GAVE FIVE* TO ABOUT NINE PEOPLE AND NOBODY TOOK IT UP; SHE ASKED THE BOY OF SEVENTEEN WHAT THE WOMAN'S HANDS DID BEFORE SHE READ THE SHEET AND THANKED HIM AT THOSE BOARDS, WHICH ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE AT THE TOP OF ELEVEN FEET COULD NOT REMEMBER HER DOING BEFORE; AND SHE SAID THAT A ROOM NINE MILES UP THAT HILL HAS BEEN TOLD THIS DISTRICT HAS A PERSON WHO CAN BE ASKED ABOUT A FIGURE AND DID NOT SEND FOR HER.** **SHE HAS NOW SAID FOUR TIMES IN ABOUT TWENTY-TWO MORNINGS THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO BE COMFORTED AND THAT IT HAS GOT LESS TRUE EVERY TIME. SHE WAS NOT ASKED FOR THE DIFFICULT HALF AGAIN IN THIS BLOCK. THE SENTENCE THE VOLUME OWED WAS PAID BY HER IN A MOUTH IN THE LAST BLOCK AND WAS NOT ENTERED AND IS NOT PRINTED IN ANY DOCUMENT.**
- **IVEN TALLOW, SIXTY-ONE, SEVENTEEN YEARS AS HOLDER OF THE FOUNDER'S CLAUSE, SIXTIETH TO SIXTY-NINTH AFTERNOONS ON THE STONES IN THIS BLOCK.** IN THIS BLOCK **HE CAME UP THE ELEVEN FEET ONCE, ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND SEVENTEENTH MORNING, FOR THE FIFTH TIME IN NINE YEARS, AND SAID HE WAS GOING TO USE THE THING HE HOLDS ONCE AND WOULD NOT SAY WHAT IT WAS FOR; HE MADE THE GROUND AT THE FOOT OF THAT BANK PUBLIC LOT FROM THAT AFTERNOON; HE NAMED A TOLL OF NINETY-EIGHT AND SAID IT IS TAKEN ONCE IN THAT YARD OUT OF THE NUMBER THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT KEEPS; HE SAID THERE IS A CONDITION ON THE SEAT AND HE WILL NOT NAME IT AND THE TOLL IS NOT THE CONDITION; AND HE SAID THAT HE HAD GIVEN AWAY THE LAST GROUND HE STOOD ON AND GOT NOTHING FOR IT AND THAT A MAN GIVING A THING AWAY IS NOT ONE OF THE FIVE THINGS THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT HAVE BEING PAID.** **HE HAS NOT ENDED THE CLAUSE AND HE HAS NOT NAMED THE CONDITION ON THE SEAT AND HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE WAS NOT GOING TO.**

NINE YEARS ON HER OWN STEP ABOUT FORTY FEET OFF THE FOOT OF THAT BANK.** IN THIS BLOCK **SHE SAID, FROM ABOUT FORTY FEET OFF, THAT THERE IS ONE PERSON A ROOM CAN ASK ABOUT A FIGURE AND ABOUT NINE PEOPLE IN THAT YARD WHO HEARD THAT MAN ANSWER ONE AND THAT THE ROOM DOES NOT KNOW ABOUT THE NINE; SHE READ TWO FIGURES OFF A PAGE AT THOSE BOARDS AND REFUSED FOUR TIMES IN FOUR SENTENCES TO SAY WHAT EITHER OF THEM IS FOR; SHE DIVIDED A TOLL OF NINETY-EIGHT BY NINETEEN OUT LOUD, SAID NINETY-FIVE FIRST AND ASKED FOR IT TO BE WRITTEN DOWN THAT SHE SAID IT FIRST, AND SAID THE THREE WHO GIVE SIX ARE THE THREE WHO CAN LOSE SOMETHING THAT IS NOT A FIGURE; AND SHE KEPT THE SUM GOING OUT LOUD THROUGH THE PAYMENT UNTIL IT ARRIVED AT NINETY-EIGHT.** **SHE DID NOT SAY THE LINE ABOUT A BEAM AND A HEAVY THING AND MAY NOT.**

- **THE YOUNG MAN WHO CAME DOWN THAT LANE WITH A PAGE, UNNAMED.** HE CAME DOWN ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FIFTH MORNING AT ABOUT A QUARTER TO TEN WITH A PAGE FOLDED ONCE IN HIS OWN HAND, AND HE SPOKE TO ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE AND NOT TO A ROOM, AND ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND FORTIETH MORNING HE ASKED OUT LOUD HOW LONG THIS DISTRICT HAS GOT AND NOBODY GAVE HIM A FIGURE, AND ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-FOURTH MORNING HE WENT BACK UP THE LANE AT ABOUT TEN TO FIVE WITH NOTHING WRITTEN ON IT. **HE IS NOT ADRIAN VALE AND THE RECORD DOES NOT SETTLE THAT HE IS, AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS VOLUME NAMES HIM.**

SHE IS NAMED IN A PERMANENT RECORD WITHOUT BEING ASKED, HAS NOT BEEN TOLD, AND HAS NEVER BEEN TOLD ANYTHING ABOUT HERSELF. HER NINE WORDS ARE UNANSWERED AND ARE NOT THIS BLOCK'S DEBT.**

AND THE TURNER, ABOUT FORTY-ONE, WHO SAID *AS SENT* OUT LOUD AT THE TOP OF ELEVEN FEET ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FOURTH MORNING AND ABOUT NINE PEOPLE SAID IT AFTER HIM AT THE SAME MOMENT AND OUT OF STEP WITH EACH OTHER.**

# VOLUME 18, BLOCK 0003 — CHARACTER STATE AT CHAPTER 870

> Appended by the phase that wrote Chapters 861 to 870. This append covers those ten chapters and carries the state at the one hundred and fifty-fourth morning. Its figures came from the ten chapter files of `chapters/volume-18/`. A character card inherited from a document is not a character: a name is settled only on a morning a person in this district says it out loud, in a yard or a room, in a scene, with the prose first and the document second. **NO NEW NAME IS SETTLED IN THIS BLOCK AND NO CHAPTER OF IT NAMES A PERSON WHO WOULD SETTLE ONE. THE FOUR PEOPLE NAMED IN ITS PROSE — ADRIAN VALE, MAVIS DORR, ORREN PIKE AND SABRA HOLT — WERE ALL NAMED ALREADY, ON THE PAGE, IN EARLIER VOLUMES, AND THE CLERK IS CARRIED AS A TITLE AND NOT AS A NAME IN THIS BLOCK.**

ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-NINTH AFTERNOON THE QUESTION THAT WAS PUT DOWN WAS HIS QUESTION AND HE WAS ASKED IT AND SAID HE WAS NOT GOING TO SAY ANYTHING ABOUT IT AND WAS NOT GOING TO CARRY IT UP NINE MILES OF HILL EITHER. HE HAS NOT BEEN TOLD HE IS A GOOD MAN, HAS NOT BEEN GIVEN A BED, A WAGE OR AN OFFICE, AND HAS ASKED FOR NONE OF THE THREE. **HE HAS SAID OUT LOAD THAT HE HAS BEGUN TO BE ABLE TO SAY TWO WORDS AGAIN WITHOUT GOING UP THE HILL TO CHECK THEM, THAT HE DOES NOT TRUST IT, AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY THEM A SECOND TIME IN THAT YARD AS LONG AS HE IS STANDING IN IT.**
- **THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX, UNNAMED, AND THE ONLY PERSON WHO CAN PROVE THAT THE FIGURES ON THAT WALL CHANGED.** AT CHAPTER 870: **THE FIVE HUNDRED AND NINETY-FIFTH OF THE MORNINGS HE HAS READ FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL, TWICE A MORNING.** HE SAYS EVERY MORNING AT ABOUT FOUR THAT HE DOES NOT KNOW WHAT ANY OF THEM IS FOR. **HIS SENTENCE WAS HEARD FOR THE FORTY-THIRD TIME RUNNING ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FOURTH AFTERNOON, AND ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTIETH AFTERNOON A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT ASKED HIM, AT THE FOOT OF THAT WALL, WHAT THE FIGURE HE SAYS OUT LOUD EVERY AFTERNOON IS FOR, AND WAS GIVEN THE SAME SENTENCE.** HE IS NEVER GIVEN A SPAN IN YEARS, DAYS, MONTHS OR FORTNIGHTS BY ANYBODY, AND **HE IS NEVER ASKED THE ONE QUESTION AGAIN FOR THE REST OF THE VOLUME AFTER CHAPTER 865, AND NOBODY MAY STATE THAT HIS SENTENCE HAS CHANGED MEANING.**

- **ORREN PIKE, THE BOY OF SEVENTEEN. # VOLUME 19, BLOCK 0001 — CHARACTER STATE AT CHAPTER 900

> Appended by the review fix of 2026-10-01, which wrote no chapter and settled no name. **AGES UNALTERED, HANDS UNALTERED, POSTS UNALTERED. EVERY DESCRIPTIVE LABEL BELOW IS THE PROSE's OWN AND IS HOW THE CHAPTERS NAME THESE PEOPLE; THE NAMES IN IT ARE FOR THE WRITER AND ARE NOT IN THE PROSE, AND NO PERSON IN THIS BLOCK IS NAMED WHO WAS NOT ALREADY NAMED IN THE FIFTY CHAPTERS BEHIND.** The block covers Chapters 891 to 900 of `chapters/volume-19/` and its figures came from those ten files and from `state/volume-19-batch-0001-summary.md`, and from nowhere else.

- **ADRIAN VALE, THIRTY-EIGHT, UNNAMED IN THE PROSE. HE WENT UP THAT LANE BEFORE THERE WAS LIGHT ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-FIFTH MORNING WITH NOTHING IN HIS HAND, KNOCKED AT ABOUT TEN AT A TIME WHEN SOMEBODY WAS IN, WAS GIVEN NOTHING FOR IT, AND WAS ANSWERED BY A WOMAN AT A TABLE.** She asked him for the page at the back of that lot book and asked him who writes on it, and he said no out loud twice, in the same words and at the same speed, and gave no reason to either. **HE CAME BACK OVER THAT BANK AT ABOUT ELEVEN WITH HIS HANDS SHOWING AND SAID ONE THING ABOUT IT IN ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE'S HEARING AND SAID NOTHING MORE ABOUT THAT HILL ALL DAY.** On the one hundred and seventy-eighth he said out loud that he has not read the page and is not going to say that he has and is not going to say that he has not. On the one hundred and eighty-second he was asked whether he would carry that form back up the lane, said he would not, **and gave the second reason anybody in that yard gave out loud in this stretch of mornings, and was not asked twice and was not thanked.** On the one hundred and eighty-fourth, at about ten past four and before the light went off the boards, he said that the fact he brought down that hill is on those boards and not on a page, and that he has no reason either and is not going to say he has one. **HE ASKED NOBODY IN THIS BLOCK AND WAS ASKED ONCE. HE HAS NO BED, NO WAGE AND NO OFFICE, HAS ASKED FOR NONE OF THE THREE, AND HAS NOT BEEN TOLD HE IS A GOOD MAN, AND NOBODY AT THE TOP OF THAT LANE OFFERED HIM ANYTHING AND HE CAME BACK DOWN WITH NOTHING IN HIS HAND.**

- **THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX, UNNAMED, WHO SAYS FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL AT ABOUT FOUR EVERY MORNING AND HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN THANKED.** HE READ THEM AT ABOUT A QUARTER TO EIGHT AND AGAIN AT ABOUT FOUR AND GOT ALL FOUR ON ALL TEN MORNINGS OF THIS BLOCK, **AND ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-THIRD A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY WITH A BARROW ASKED HIM WHETHER HE IS GOING TO BE AT THAT WALL TOMORROW MORNING, AND HE GAVE HER THE ANSWER HE GIVES AT ABOUT FOUR EVERY MORNING, FLAT, IN THE SAME WORDS AND AT THE SAME SPEED, AND WAS NOT ASKED ONE THING ON ANY OTHER MORNING OF THIS BLOCK. THAT IS THE ONLY ASKING OF HIM IN TEN MORNINGS AND IT MAY NOT BE PRINTED IN A FIGURE BLOCK AS A FIGURE. THE CLERK READ THE MORNING OUT LOUD AND WROTE ONE LINE FOR IT AND PUT NEITHER THE QUESTION NOR THE ANSWER ON THAT LINE. NOBODY THANKED HIM AND NOBODY THANKED THE PERSON WHO ASKED.** HE IS STILL MEASURED IN MORNINGS AND IN *SINCE BEFORE THE MIDDLE OF THE WINTER* AND IN NOTHING ELSE, AND HE IS STILL THE ONLY PERSON IN THAT YARD WHO SAYS OUT LOUD WHAT HE DOES NOT KNOW.

- **MAVIS DORR, FIFTY, THE CLERK. SHE IS THE ONLY PERSON WHO MAY WRITE IN EITHER BOOK AND SHE WROTE ONE LINE IN TEN MORNINGS.** She said no out loud three times in this stretch without giving a reason, and the third was said in the same words as the first and at the same speed. On the one hundred and seventy-seventh she said out loud that she is a clerk of the lot book and not of anything behind it and is not going to go and find out on a morning like this. On the one hundred and seventy-ninth she said one thing out loud and said it once: **a page that has never been read is not a record of anything. That is all. I have got nothing else this morning.** On the one hundred and eighty-third she put the form flat on the boards under the clean page, said it is not going in the lot book, read the morning out loud before she wrote it, **and wrote one line on the page at the back in her own hand, which is the only line taken on any morning of this block.** On the one hundred and eighty-fourth about nine people said no to a question in her hearing and **she said nothing at all about it, and about four of them have said since that is not her agreeing with them, and that nobody has asked her whether she agrees. SHE HAS NOT ASKED ANYBODY FOR A NAME AND THE SECOND SPACE ON THAT FORM IS STILL EMPTY, AND THE SPACE ON THE FORM THAT CAME DOWN THAT LANE IS A DIFFERENT SPACE ON A DIFFERENT PIECE OF PAPER AND IS ALSO EMPTY.**

- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR, WHO SAYS HE KNOWS THREE PEOPLE AND DID NOT SAY WHICH THREE, ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-NINTH MORNING, AT ABOUT ELEVEN AND AGAIN AT ABOUT ONE, AND WAS ASKED AGAIN BY ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE AND SAID NOTHING.** At about ten past four that same morning he gave a reason out loud: **if I do not know, then somebody can say that I do not know and we can all carry that; if I say, then it is three people and a lane and a morning, and I have put that on about nineteen people. I am not going to do that and I am not going to pretend I cannot.** On the one hundred and eighty-second he asked a man of thirty-eight whether he would carry the form back up that lane and got more than he asked for. **HE HAS ASKED ONE QUESTION IN THIS BLOCK AND HE HAS BEEN GIVEN THE BLOCK'S FIRST REASON AND ITS SECOND.**

- **THE MAN AT THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE, UNNAMED, WHO IS NOT A PUZZLE AND WHOSE FIFTH LINE IS NOT TO BE PAID.** On the one hundred and eighty-first he said out loud that the two headings are two hands or one hand at two times and that both are possible from where he is standing and he cannot get past those two from here, **and about nine people heard it and did not answer him.** Nobody answered him and nobody paid him anything and nobody in that yard named what he is owed.

- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY WITH A BARROW, WHO ASKED THE ONE QUESTION OF THIS BLOCK.** She came up about four feet of that bank at about half past eleven on the one hundred and eighty-third morning, put the barrow down, and asked the man of fifty-six in the ordinary voice whether he is going to be at that wall tomorrow morning. **SHE WAS NOT THANKED, SHE DID NOT ASK FOR ANYTHING, AND ABOUT FOUR OF THEM HAVE SAID SINCE THAT SHE DID NOT WANT ANYTHING.**

- **THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM, WHO ASKED THE TWO HARDEST QUESTIONS IN THE BLOCK AND GOT NINE ANSWERS TO ONE OF THEM.** On the one hundred and seventy-eighth he asked out loud whether anybody in that yard has read the page at the back of that book. On the one hundred and eighty-fourth he asked out loud whether anybody in that yard is going to fill that space on that form, **and about nine people said no, one after another, and none of them gave a reason, and he did not answer them and did not ask again.** He is the only person in that yard who has twice got nine people to stop in about as long as a man of fifty-six says four figures.

- **THE YOUNG WOMAN WHO CAME DOWN THAT LANE ON THE ONE HUNDRED AND EIGHTIETH MORNING WITH A PAGE FOLDED ONCE IN HER OWN HAND AND NOTHING ELSE ON HER.** She gave it to nobody in particular, said no out loud to four people who asked whether she wanted it put somewhere, and stood at the foot of that bank. **She was not asked how far it is and she was not asked anything at all. She is not named and nobody has asked her to be.**

- **THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS A SCALE, UNNAMED. SHE WEIGHED A SACK OR A BAR OF IRON AT ABOUT ONE ON EVERY MORNING OF THIS BLOCK, THE NEEDLE CAME TO REST A LONG WAY SHORT OF THE MARK WHERE THE LEAD WEIGHTS BEGIN ON ALL TEN, AND SHE SAID NOTHING TO ANYBODY ON ALL TEN.** She has now said nothing out loud for longer than she said nothing before it, and nobody in that yard has said out loud that she is keeping to herself.

- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY, UNNAMED. HE SAID HIS NUMBER INTO ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED YARDS OF AIR AT ABOUT HALF PAST ONE ON ALL TEN MORNINGS AND NOBODY WROTE IT DOWN.** On the one hundred and eighty-first he put his thumb on the heading over the space on the form and said out loud that it is not the heading over the one name in that column and that there is nothing under it. He has not been given a bed, a wage or an office either, and has not asked for any of the three.

- **ORREN PIKE, THE BOY OF SEVENTEEN, WHO IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THAT YARD WHO SAYS A FIGURE OUT LOUD.** He said the lane count at about ten to eight on all ten mornings and a figure on **four** of them — the one hundred and seventy-fifth, seventy-eighth, eightieth and eighty-third — and no figure on the other six. **HE HAS NEVER ONCE SAID WHAT THE NUMBER IS ABOUT AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD HAS ASKED HIM.** His own figure is not a ladder: the morning before this block he said one hundred and ninety-five, and on the four mornings of this block he said one hundred and ninety-six, one hundred and ninety-nine, two hundred and one and two hundred and four, **and it is neither the morning number nor the count of mornings a man has been at the foot of that bank, and it has already slipped by one in the fifty chapters behind, where `chapter-0885.md` prints one hundred and eighty-nine on a morning the pattern would give one hundred and ninety. IT MAY NOT BE GIVEN AN INTERCEPT, A PROJECTION, A CARRIED FIGURE OR A CLOSING TOTAL, AND IT IS NEVER PRINTED IN A FIGURE BLOCK.**

- **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR, UNNAMED, WHO IS NOT ASKED ANYTHING AND WAS NOT ASKED ANYTHING IN THIS BLOCK.** He was at the foot of that low wall on his five hundred and thirty-ninth night of that run at the end of this block, having slept on five hundred and thirty-eight of them, and a man of sixty-one was on the stones about four feet off him for the one hundred and twenty-ninth afternoon running, and they said good afternoon, and a man of sixty-one said good afternoon and said nothing else, **and nobody went near the man at the foot of that wall on any morning of this block, and nothing in his hands was entered, and nobody offered him a chair, a bed, a wage or an office.**

- **AND THE PEOPLE THIS BLOCK ADDED: IT ADDED NOBODY BY NAME AND IT SETTLED NO NEW NAME.** A young woman with a page, a woman of about fifty with a barrow, a man of thirty-four who knows three people, a turner and a man who mends fencing are printed as the chapters print them, and **A NAME IS SETTLED ONLY ON A MORNING A PERSON IN A YARD SAYS IT OUT LOUD IN A SCENE, AND NOBODY DID.**

# VOLUME 19, BLOCK 0002 — CHARACTER STATE AT CHAPTER 910

> **Appended 2026-10-01 by the review fix of Volume 19 Block 0002. It wrote no chapter, altered no chapter, and restarted no batch. Every count below is measured against the twenty chapter files of `chapters/volume-19/`, and where a person is not in those files this section says so rather than inheriting them from an earlier volume.**

**THE STANDING FACT ABOUT THE NAMED LEAD, MEASURED RATHER THAN INHERITED.** `Adrian Vale` is on the page **by name** in Volumes 16, 17 and 18 — 48, 34 and 29 chapter files — and is **not named in a single one of the twenty chapters of Volume 19.** He returned to the page at Chapter 751 in Volume 16 and has been off it by name for the whole of Volume 19 so far. `state/volume-19-batch-0002-summary.md` section 4.1 carries his name in its heading while the figure it measures is said by a boy of seventeen, and **that heading is a house label for a standing question and not a claim that he is in the yard. WHETHER THE NAMED LEAD RETURNS TO THE PAGE IN VOLUME 19 IS NOT SETTLED BY ANY DOCUMENT IN THIS TREE AND IS NOT A WRITER's TO SETTLE.** Two figures that were in earlier volumes and are **not** in these twenty chapters: **Mavis Dorr** and **Nell Cray**, at zero each, and Nell Cray's nine words remain unanswered somewhere other than this volume's page.

**THE CLERK, WHO IS THE FIGURE THIS BLOCK IS ABOUT.** She writes the morning on the page at the back of that lot book in her own hand and takes no line for it. She said no out loud without a reason three times in Block 0001 and again in Block 0002. She says out loud that she is a clerk of the lot book and not of anything behind it. She says out loud that nobody in that yard has read that page, that that goes with her as well, and that it is not a reason for anything. **At `c = 20` she read that page to herself with her mouth moving and nothing anybody in the yard could hear, and said one word out loud, and the word was three, and about nineteen people heard it, and it is not an answer to anything and nobody in that yard has called it one.** A successor may not say why she read it to herself, may not print which three, and may not make it a figure.

**THE MAN OF THIRTY-EIGHT, WHO IS THE YARD'S STANDING DEBT.** He has no bed, no wage and no office, has asked for none of the three, and has not been told he is a good man. He went up the lane before light on the one hundred and seventy-fifth morning with nothing in his hand, said no out loud to two people, and came back over the bank at about eleven with his hands showing. He gave a reason out loud on the one hundred and eighty-second morning for refusing to carry a form back up the lane, and about nine people heard every word of it. On the one hundred and eighty-ninth morning he went up the lane in the afternoon to one of the four places, took nothing, asked for nothing, and came back down it with nothing in his hand, having said out loud that he is not going to be thanked for it and is not going to say he is a good man for it. **He stands at the top of that bank with his hands showing three times in this block — at his first light, at about eleven and at about six — and once in the last morning of it. That is a house phrase for what his hands are doing while he stands there, it carries no figure, and neither a space nor a hand is counted anywhere in this volume.**

**THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX, WHO IS ASKED NOTHING.** He says four figures off that wall at about a quarter to eight and again at about four and gets all four both times, and then puts his hand flat on the stone and says, in the ordinary voice, that he does not know what any of them is for. **Nobody in that yard asked him anything out loud on any of the ten mornings of this block, and a line on the page at the back is only ever for an asking of him.** He is given no span in years, days, months or fortnights anywhere in these twenty chapters, including a negative one.

**ORREN PIKE, WHO PUTS A NUMBER INTO FOUR HUNDRED YARDS OF AIR EVERY MORNING.** He is named in **22** of the twenty chapters and says the lane count out loud at those boards at about ten to eight. The lane count is **thirty** and it moved once in the volume, on the one hundred and eightieth morning, when a young woman came down that lane with a page folded once in her own hand and nothing else on her. He says no figure on some mornings and a figure on others.

**THE BOY OF SEVENTEEN, WHO SAYS A FIGURE THAT HAS NO INTERCEPT.** He says a figure out loud at the near end of the first table before the clerk's hand has moved, on **three mornings in ten** in this block — chapters 902, 906 and 909 — and the three figures are *two hundred and thirteen*, *two hundred and fifteen* and *two hundred and seventeen*. **He has not said what the figure is about and nobody has asked him, and the figure block on every morning of this block says *a figure* and gives no figure.** He is not checked against a cell, may not be estimated, recovered, inferred or carried forward, and may not be printed in a figure block. His three figures are not two apart by design and no pattern may be read into them.

**THE OTHERS IN THAT YARD, ALL UNNAMED AND ALL MEASURED.** The man who digs loam, who asked out loud on the one hundred and seventy-eighth morning whether anybody had read that page, spent the whole of the one hundred and ninety-second morning at the north end of that yard, came back into the middle of it on the next, and asked the clerk to read the page; the asking was refused out loud. The turner, who put his apron on the second table and said out loud that a page goes down that lane and that the holding of one is a person he is not going to name. The man who makes the stone, who put his chisel down and his hand flat on the low wall and said a change in the hand is not a figure. The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally, who put his thumb on the heading over that space. The man at the end of the second table, who stood up on the one hundred and ninety-second morning and sat down again without asking anything. The man of about forty-one, the man of about thirty-four, the man of about twenty-nine who drives the cart, the woman who keeps a scale, a woman of about fifty-two, a woman of about fifty with a barrow, and the young woman who brought the page down the lane. **About nineteen people are in that yard and about nine of them are at the top of eleven feet, and the two counts are different counts and neither is a measure of anybody.**

# VOLUME 20, BLOCK 0001 — CHAPTERS 931 TO 940 — CHARACTER STATE

**NOBODY IN THIS BLOCK WAS GIVEN A NAME AND EVERY PERSON ON THESE TEN MORNINGS IS A DESCRIPTOR. THE CLERK IS *THE CLERK*. THE FIGURES BELOW ARE WHAT TEN MORNINGS DID TO EACH PERSON AND NOTHING ELSE IS SETTLED.** **AND THE *NOBODY NEW ENTERED* CLAIM WAS TOO LOOSE AND IS CORRECTED IN PLACE ON 2026-10-02, BECAUSE A REVIEW FOUND IT RESTING ON A WORDING THE CHAPTERS DO NOT USE: ON `c = 23` THE MAN WHO MADE THE STONE CAME DOWN THAT BANK AT ABOUT TEN AND SAID ONE THING OUT LOUD FOR THE FIRST TIME IN HIS LIFE, AND AN EARLIER FORM OF THIS LINE LET *SAID NOTHING OUT LOUD BEFORE* STAND FOR *HAD NOT STOOD IN THAT YARD BEFORE*, WHICH IT IS NOT THE SAME AS. HIS OWN SENTENCE SETTLES IT AND IT IS CANON: HE SAYS HE HAS STOOD IN THAT YARD ON A GREAT MANY MORNINGS AND HAS NEVER ONCE SAID THAT. **THE FIGURE THAT IS FIXED IS THAT NO NAME WAS SETTLED AND THAT EVERY PERSON ON THESE TEN MORNINGS IS A DESCRIPTOR. NO CLAIM IS MADE ABOUT WHO CAME DOWN THAT BANK, AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD IS NEW TO IT.**

- **A MAN OF THIRTY-EIGHT.** Said one thing out loud on the two hundred and fifteenth morning, once, describing what forty mornings of that yard look like from the end of his own table, and **nobody answered it and nobody agreed with it and nobody said it was not so.** On the two hundred and seventeenth morning he was asked, once, out loud, whether the reason he gave on the one hundred and eighty-second morning is on the page at the back of that lot book, and **he said no, one word, and gave no reason for the no, and went back to the end of his own table.** **THAT NO IS THE ONLY WORD HE HAS SAID OUT LOUD IN THAT YARD SINCE THE DESCRIPTION. HE SAID NOTHING ELSE ON ANY OF THE OTHER EIGHT MORNINGS OF THIS BLOCK, AND ON THE TWO HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FOURTH HE WAS AT ABOUT FOUR FEET OFF THE NEAR END OF THE FIRST TABLE FROM ABOUT NINE UNTIL THE BOARDS WENT DOWN AND SAID NOTHING OUT LOUD AT ALL.** He has not been given a bed, a wage or an office, has not asked for any of the three, has not been told he is a good man, and is not to be thanked.
- **THE CLERK, THIRTY-FOUR, FIFTEEN YEARS AS CLERK. ONE WOMAN AND NOT TWO.** Read the morning out loud before she wrote anything on nine of these ten mornings and **did not read it on `c = 8`.** Wrote one line carrying the morning and not the reason on `c = 7` and took no line on the other nine, **naming the morning on the page in her own hand without a line every one of those nine times.** She is the only person in that yard who has said the same shape of sentence out loud nine mornings running and the shape changed on the one morning she did not say it.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR, UNNAMED.** Kept his mouth shut on nine of the ten mornings. **Said the one reason of this block on `c = 7`, about himself and not about his work** — that he stood at that table because he could not make himself go home, and that he has not said it to his wife and will not say it to her in that house, and that in that house he is the one standing at the end of a table too. **Nobody asked him for it, nobody asked him to say it again, nobody said it for him, and nobody said one word back.** He put his hand flat on the top of the second table at about one that afternoon and took it off again. **The reason may not be asked for again by anybody, may not be said for him by anybody, may not go in a figure block, and may not go on any page by any means other than the measure.**
- **THE TURNER, ABOUT FORTY-ONE, UNNAMED.** Said the same sentence he said on the one hundred and eighty-eighth morning, unasked, on `c = 2`, and **nobody asked him to say it again.** Said on `c = 9`, once, that a reason said out loud and a reason asked for are two different things going into the same place, that he is not putting them together and is not saying they are the same, and that **nobody answered him and nobody asked him to say it again and nobody asked him what the same place is.** His wheel did not stop for any of it on any of the ten mornings.
- **THE MAN AT THE END OF THE SECOND TABLE, ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN, UNNAMED.** Said one thing on `c = 5`, once, about himself: he has stood at that end of that table since the morning the ground under it was made and no one in that yard has ever told him out loud what he is standing there for, and he is not asking for it. **He was answered by nobody and he went back to the end of the table.** **He gives no new line in this block and his fifth line is not to be paid.**
- **ORREN PIKE, SEVENTEEN, THE BOY.** Said the lane count out loud at the boards at about ten to eight on **every one of the ten mornings**, and it is **thirty** on all ten. Said a figure out loud before the clerk's hand had moved on **three mornings in ten** — **nine hundred and forty** on 931, **one thousand and eleven** on 936, **two hundred and ninety-four** on 940 — and said no figure and said so out loud on the other seven. **Those three figures have no intercept, no rule, no expected value and no cell, were not chosen to be any distance apart, and no check may be run against them.**
- **THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX, UNNAMED.** Read the four figures off that wall at about a quarter to eight and again at about four and got all four of them on **all ten mornings**, the six hundred and fifty-sixth to the six hundred and sixty-fifth of those mornings, and said his same four words on **all ten**, the hundred and fourth to the hundred and thirteenth time running. **He was asked nothing out loud on any of the ten. He is measured in mornings and in *since before the middle of the winter* and in nothing else.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO WHO PUTS TABLES UP, UNNAMED.** Put his table up about four feet along from the second table and squared it with the back of his heel on **all ten mornings**, and on `c = 8` **said nothing at all**, which was the first morning in four he had put that table up and NOT said a word.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES THE CART, UNNAMED.** Came through the gap at about half past nine on `c = 4`, slowed in the gap the way he slows in a gap, **did not stop, and went out of the far side of that yard without going up the lane**, and **nobody in that yard said one word about it, then or afterwards.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR, UNNAMED.** At the foot of that low wall on all ten mornings, on the five hundred and seventieth to the five hundred and seventy-ninth night of that run, having slept on five hundred and sixty-nine to five hundred and seventy-eight of them. **He was asked nothing on any of the ten and that is the seventy-first to the eightieth morning running of it.**
- **A MAN OF SIXTY-ONE, UNNAMED.** On the stones in the afternoon of every one of the ten mornings, said good afternoon on every one of the ten, and said nothing else on any of them, and nobody went near the man at the foot of that wall on any of the ten.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY, UNNAMED.** Said his number into about four hundred yards of air at about half past one on all ten mornings and **said no sentence before it on any of the ten.**
- **THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS A SCALE, UNNAMED, NINE YEARS WEIGHING THINGS AT THE FOOT OF THAT BANK.** Set her scale up at about four feet off the near end of the second table on `c = 2`, `c = 6` and `c = 7`, weighed a bar of iron or a sack out of a basket, wrote the figure on the edge of a board of her own with a bit of chalk and said nothing to anybody. **She said no reason out loud on any of the ten and the name of the fifth of the five was not said on any of them.**
- **THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM, THE MAN WHO MADE THE STONE, THE MAN WHO MADE THINGS ROUND.** Present and silent through the block. **THE MAN WHO FITTED THE RAIL IS NOT IN THAT YARD AND WAS NOT THANKED ON ANY OF THE TEN MORNINGS, AND HIS RAIL REACHED THE TWO HUNDREDTH MORNING ON `c = 6` WHILE NOBODY IN THAT YARD LOOKED UP.**
- **AND THE FOLLOWING ARE NOT PLACED IN ANY OF THESE TEN MORNINGS BY THE DAY MAP AND WERE NOT PLACED IN ONE: **the woman of about fifty-two**, who came down that bank six times in the ten mornings behind this block and asked one thing out loud in six times down it; **the young woman who brought the form down that lane once**, who is not in that yard again; **and SABRA HOLT, FIFTY-TWO, NINETEEN YEARS AN APPOINTED ADMINISTRATOR, THE THIRD OF THE NINE OLDEST KEYS, WHOSE ONE MORNING IN THIS VOLUME IS `c = 37` AND IS NOT ONE OF THESE TEN.**

**ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE ARE IN THAT YARD AND ABOUT NINE OF THEM ARE AT THE TOP OF ELEVEN FEET, AND THE TWO COUNTS ARE DIFFERENT COUNTS AND NEITHER IS A MEASURE OF ANYBODY.**

# VOLUME 20, BLOCK 0002 — CHAPTERS 941 TO 950 — CHARACTER STATE

- **A MAN OF THIRTY-EIGHT, UNNAMED.** Came down the slope at about half past nine with his hands showing and worked at about four feet off the near end of the first table, and **said nothing out loud at all from about nine until about four on `c = 16` and said nothing out loud on `c = 13`, `c = 14`, `c = 17`, `c = 18` and `c = 19` either.** On `c = 12` **he went up that lane in the afternoon with nothing in his hand and came back down it at about half past four with nothing in either hand and said one thing at the bottom of the bank, that he did not ask on purpose, that there was nobody up there to ask and nothing to put in a hand, and that a man does not say a thing in that yard a second time because nine miles is nine miles in either direction.** Nobody answered him, nobody asked him why he went, nobody thanked him for going or for coming back. **He has not been given a bed, a wage or an office, has not asked for any of the three, has not been told he is a good man, was offered nothing at the top of that lane, and is not to be thanked. HE IS NOT ASKED ANYTHING ON ANY MORNING AFTER `c = 3`.**
- **THE CLERK, THIRTY-FOUR, FIFTEEN YEARS AS CLERK, ONE WOMAN AND NOT TWO.** **SAID NOTHING OUT LOUD TO ANYBODY FROM ABOUT TEN UNTIL ABOUT TWO ON `c = 18`.** On `c = 13` she was asked one question out loud about a line on that page and **said one sentence back and did not say why**: that it is not one of the things she has ever been asked about and she is not going to start this morning. **On `c = 20` SHE STOOD UP AT THE NEAR END OF THOSE BOARDS AT ABOUT ELEVEN AND SAID ONE SENTENCE OUT LOUD, ONCE, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, AT ABOUT FOUR FEET OFF THE NEAR END OF THE SECOND TABLE, IN ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE's HEARING: *I do not know what one of them is for and I have been writing them a long time.* IT IS HER OWN SENTENCE AND IT IS ABOUT HER OWN LINES. NOBODY ANSWERED HER, NOBODY ASKED HER WHY SHE HAD SAID IT, NOBODY ASKED HER TO SAY IT AGAIN, AND NOBODY SAID ONE WORD BACK TO HER.** She read the morning out loud before she wrote it on **all ten** of these mornings, wrote one line carrying the morning and not the reason on `c = 15`, and named the morning in her own hand without a line on the other nine. **She is never asked why she said it and she is never asked to say it again.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR, UNNAMED.** **Said one thing out loud on `c = 11` at about ten, about the morning before this one and not about his work: he said a thing out loud in that yard on the two hundred and twenty-first morning, he has not said it since and is not going to, nobody has asked him for it, and what he has been doing since the light came up is standing at that table thinking about that morning and not about this one.** He did not say the thing again. Nobody answered him, nobody asked him to say it again, nobody asked him what the morning before this one was to him. **He is asked for nothing on any of these ten mornings and he asks nobody for anything on any of them.** He looked once across about four hundred yards of that yard at about eleven on `c = 16` and at a woman mending a net at about midday on `c = 17`.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO WHO PUTS TABLES UP, UNNAMED.** Put his table up about four feet along from the second table and squared it with the back of his heel on **all ten mornings**. **Said one thing out loud on `c = 15` at about eleven, about another man and not about a page: what the man at the near end of the first table said on the one hundred and eighty-second morning was about him and not about the back of that book, a man who has said a thing about himself once is not going to say it again, and that is a man being allowed to do that.** He did not say the thing itself. **Nobody asked him how he knew it was on the form, nobody asked him to say it again, nobody said it for him, and nobody answered him.** He said nothing else out loud on any of the ten and went back along the top of his own table with the cloth.
- **THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS A SCALE, UNNAMED, NINE YEARS WEIGHING THINGS AT THE FOOT OF THAT BANK.** Set her scale up at about four feet off the near end of the second table on `c = 13`, `c = 16`, `c = 17`, `c = 18` and `c = 19`, weighed a bar of iron out of a basket and wrote the figure on the edge of a board of her own with a bit of chalk. **Said one thing out loud on `c = 18` at about half past twelve, that the reason is on the form and not on the page at the back of that book because the form came down that lane in a hand and the page at the back never did, and that she is not going to say it again.** Nobody asked her to say it again and nobody asked her how she knew. **She did not name the fifth of the five and was not asked for it.**

- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO, UNNAMED. PLACED ON ONE MORNING OF THIS BLOCK, AT `c = 17`, WHICH IS WHERE THE DAY MAP PUTS HER.** Came down that bank at about a quarter to eleven with a folding frame under one arm, a canvas bag over her shoulder and netting cord round her forearm, set the frame up about nine feet back from about four feet off the near end of the first table, hooked a net onto it and went at a tear in it with her own shuttle all morning. **Asked nothing at all and nobody in that yard said one word back to her about it.** Nobody asked her what she was doing and nobody asked her how long she meant to be there. **The second space on that form was still empty when she set her bag down beside the frame and she did not fill it and nobody asked her to.**

## VOLUME 22, BLOCK 0002 — CHARACTER STATE AFTER CHAPTERS 1021 TO 1030

**THE POSITION AT THE END OF THESE TEN MORNINGS, IN THE HOUSE's OWN FIGURES. NOTHING IN THIS SECTION SETTLES ANYTHING AND NOBODY IS ASKED ANYTHING BY IT AND NO COUNT OF THE FIGURES EITHER OF THEM SAYS IS PUBLISHED IN IT.**

**1. THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS A SCALE. UNNAMED. NINE YEARS WEIGHING THINGS AT THE FOOT OF THAT BANK. **SHE SAID A FIGURE OF HER OWN OUT LOUD AT ABOUT HALF PAST ONE ON THE THREE HUNDRED AND SIXTH MORNING, ONCE, AT HER OWN SCALE, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, AND **NOBODY ANSWERED HER, NOBODY THANKED HER, NOBODY ASKED HER WHAT SHE WEIGHS THINGS FOR, AND NOBODY ASKED HER WHERE ANY FIGURE CAME FROM.** SHE SAID ONE AGAIN ON THE THREE HUNDRED AND ELEVENTH MORNING, AND **INSIDE A MINUTE A SECOND FIGURE WENT OUT OF THAT YARD ABOUT NINE FEET OFF HER, AND SHE DID NOT GO OVER, AND THE PAGE TOOK NO LINE FOR EITHER.** ON EVERY OTHER MORNING OF THIS BLOCK SHE SAID NOTHING OUT LOUD AT ALL. **WHAT SHE WENT BACK OVER AFTERWARDS ON THE THREE HUNDRED AND ELEVENTH WAS THE WIND: IT HAD NOT DROPPED AND IT HAD NOT RISEN AND NOTHING IN THE AIR HAD CHANGED BETWEEN THE TWO FIGURES.** SHE WAS TOLD FROM HER ON THE THREE HUNDRED AND SIXTH AND ON THE THREE HUNDRED AND ELEVENTH. HER OWN BOARD IS OPEN AND **NOT ONE CHARACTER OF IT APPEARS IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, AND NEITHER DOES A PIECE OF CHALK OR A BOARD.**
